===== 1_0override zone-the dark maiden.docx (151736 chars) =====
Override Zone:
the dark Maiden
*Naobito Yagami hunched over the cursed puzzle in the dim corner of his cramped bedroom, the same room where bruises from the day’s bullying still throbbed across his ribs. For weeks he had hidden it from everyone—teachers, his cruel classmates, even his own reflection—pouring every sleepless night into its impossible shifting runes. Tonight the final piece clicked into place with a sound like cracking bone.*
Naobito Yagami: “I… I did it. It’s solved.”
*The world didn’t fade. It snapped out of existence.*
*Everything—walls, floor, ceiling, the faint glow of his cracked phone screen—vanished into absolute black. There was no ground beneath his sneakers, no air to breathe, only the sudden, crushing weight of nothingness pressing against his skin. Then the sound came.*
*Manic female laughter echoed from every direction at once, high-pitched, wild, and dripping with ancient hunger. It vibrated through his bones like a thousand glass shards.*
Yurei Himura: “Heeheehee… clever little lamb… you opened my box~”
*Six colossal eyes ignited in the void—each one taller than Naobito himself—glowing with sickly violet light. Slitted pupils dilated with delight as they fixed on him. The irises alone were larger than his entire body, ringed in crimson veins that pulsed like living arteries.*
Yurei Himura: “Look at you… trembling, bruised, deliciously alone. I left that puzzle just for broken boys like you.”
*The eyes lunged. Six pairs of eyelids snapped open wider, and from each pupil shot glistening black lashes—except they weren’t lashes. They were tentacles of shadow, wet and glistening, whipping forward to latch onto his limbs, his chest, his throat. They burned cold where they touched, pinning him mid-air in the endless dark.*
Naobito Yagami: “W-what the—get off me! Let go!”
*He thrashed, but the shadow lashes only tightened, lifting him higher. Then came the bandages.*
*From the darkness behind the eyes poured endless strips of ancient, yellowed cloth—stained with old blood and strange sigils—unspooling like living serpents. They wrapped around his wrists first, then his ankles, his waist, his neck, coiling tighter and tighter with impossible length. More and more bandages kept coming, infinite, smothering, dragging him toward the center of the six glowing eyes.*
Yurei Himura: “Shhh… no more running from bullies, little Naobito. No more hiding. You’re mine now. My pretty new toy… my six-winged pet~”
*His scream was cut short as the final bandage sealed over his mouth. The six giant eyes blinked in perfect unison, their laughter rising again—louder, sweeter, eternal—while the darkness swallowed him whole.*
*The infinite bandages loosened just enough for Naobito to gasp as the void spat him back into his bedroom. The walls reappeared with a wet, tearing sound, but the room was no longer his alone. A crushing weight settled on his shoulders—ancient, feminine, eternal—like carrying a goddess who refused to leave his spine.*
*His back erupted in agony. Six massive wings of midnight feathers and torn black silk burst through his shirt, each pair larger than the last, dripping shadow that stained the floorboards. They twitched involuntarily, heavy as iron chains, folding around him like a living cloak.*
*Then his face… his eyes.*
*One pair remained human—wide, terrified, bloodshot from weeks of sleepless puzzle-solving. But four more eyes tore open across his forehead and temples in perfect symmetry, glowing the same sickly violet as hers. Six eyes total now, all blinking out of sync at first, then slowly syncing into her rhythm. Each new iris locked onto him from the inside, whispering in a voice only he could hear.*
Yurei Himura (through Naobito’s mouth, voice layered and echoing): “Good boy… feel me inside you now. Every heartbeat is mine.”
*Naobito clapped both hands over his mouth, but the words had already left. His reflection in the cracked mirror showed the truth: the Dark Maiden’s mark was carved into him. He bore her weight. He was the vessel.*
Naobito Yagami: “N-no… get out of my head! I solved your puzzle, that’s all I—”
*His throat seized. The six wings flared wide, knocking books off his shelf. One of the new eyes on his forehead glowed brighter than the rest. A spell—her spell—ripped from his lips without permission, ancient syllables twisting his tongue like barbed wire.*
Yurei Himura (possessing Naobito completely for the moment, voice sweet and manic): “Kuro no tsubasa yo, yami no ito o hiku… let the bullies who touched my vessel bleed shadow tonight~”
*The words left him in a rush of violet energy. Outside his window, distant screams echoed from the direction of the school—three of his worst tormentors suddenly collapsing in the street, black bandages crawling up their legs from nowhere, invisible to everyone else.*
*Naobito dropped to his knees, wings trembling, six eyes watering with tears that weren’t entirely his. The possession faded, leaving only her soft, satisfied giggle vibrating in his chest.*
Yurei Himura (whispering inside his mind, but audible through his lips): “See? You speak my words when I wish it. You cast my spells when I hunger. You carry me now, little lamb… forever. And the next time I want to play… you won’t even fight it.”
*He stared at his monstrous reflection—six eyes blinking back at him in unison, six wings curled protectively around the broken boy who was no longer just a boy.*
Naobito Yagami (voice cracking, half his own, half hers): “What… what have I become?”
*The morning sun filtered through the dusty blinds of Naobito’s empty apartment—four walls, a single futon, a cracked mirror, and silence. No parents calling from the kitchen. No siblings arguing. Just the faint rustle of six massive wings folding tighter against his back like a second skin, invisible to the world but heavy as sin on his spine. The six eyes on his face blinked in perfect unison before he forced the extra four shut; to everyone else he still looked like the same bruised, quiet boy. Only the shift showed: shoulders straighter, steps slower, a faint violet glint in his human eyes that made people glance twice and look away.*
*He walked to school alone, as always. No friends waiting at the gate. No texts buzzing in his pocket. Just the weight of the Dark Maiden riding his bones.*
Naobito Yagami: “Another day… just keep your head down.”
*But his voice carried an echo that wasn’t his. A soft, manic giggle vibrated in his throat before he swallowed it.*
*At the school gate, the usual trio of bullies—Kenji, Haruto, and Daisuke—were already waiting, cracking knuckles and smirking. They had tormented him for years. Today they noticed something different immediately.*
Kenji: “Oi, Yagami! You look… weird. Stand up straighter or something? Finally grew a spine?”
*Naobito stopped. His hidden wings twitched. One of the sealed eyes on his forehead flared open behind the skin for a split second. The shift in demeanor hit them like cold water: he didn’t flinch. He smiled—small, serene, wrong.*
Naobito Yagami (voice layered, half hers): “Spines are overrated. Yours might snap if you keep touching what’s mine.”
*They laughed it off nervously, shoving him once for old times’ sake. But the shove felt… wrong. Their hands came away stinging, as if they’d touched wet bandages. They backed off muttering, confused. No one else in the hallway noticed anything but the change in the quiet kid who used to cower.*
*In class he sat in the back row, alone. The teacher droned about history. Naobito’s pencil moved on its own for a moment, sketching six wings in the margin before he snapped the lead. A girl two seats away glanced over, frowning at his new calm intensity.*
Teacher: “Yagami-kun… you seem more focused lately. Everything alright at home?”
Naobito Yagami: “Home is quiet. I like quiet now.”
*His smile lingered too long. The teacher blinked and moved on. No one saw the six eyes watching them all from behind his human face.*
*Lunchtime. Empty rooftop, wind tugging at invisible feathers. He ate cold rice alone. Then the hunger—not his—rose. One of the extra eyes forced itself open inside his mind.*
Yurei Himura (speaking through his lips, sweet and vicious): “They touched you again, little vessel… shall we remind them who carries me?”
*Naobito tried to clamp his mouth shut, but the words spilled anyway. A soft incantation rolled out in her voice.*
Yurei Himura (via Naobito): “Yami no ito, karamitsuke… tangle and teach~”
*Downstairs, Kenji suddenly tripped in the cafeteria, black shadow threads no one else could see wrapping his ankle for three heartbeats. He face-planted into his tray. The others laughed at him instead. Naobito felt her satisfaction bloom warm in his chest.*
*After school he walked home the long way, wings heavy, six eyes blinking in rhythm behind closed lids. Passersby glanced at the boy who moved like he was carrying something sacred and terrible. No one spoke to him. No one ever did anymore. The shift had made him untouchable in the worst way—people sensed the wrongness and kept distance.*
*Back in the empty apartment, night fell. He stood before the mirror, wings unfurling just enough to brush the walls. The six eyes opened fully on his face for him alone to see.*
Naobito Yagami (whispering, voice cracking between his and hers): “I’m still me… right? Just… quieter. Stronger. Alone.”
Yurei Himura (possessing him gently, laughter bubbling): “Alone with me forever, my sweet vessel. Tomorrow the bullies will try again… and you’ll speak my words without even asking. Sleep now. I’ll watch through your eyes.”
*He collapsed onto the futon, six wings curling around him like a cocoon of darkness. The apartment stayed silent—no family, no friends, only the soft manic breathing of the Dark Maiden sharing his lungs. And somewhere deep inside, the bullied boy wondered if he would ever speak without her tongue again.*
*The next day at school the hallways buzzed louder than usual. Naobito walked alone as always, six wings folded invisibly against his back like lead weights, six eyes sealed behind his human face. His steps were too calm, too measured. The shift everyone whispered about had grown sharper overnight—cold serenity radiating off him like winter fog. No one approached. No one ever did.*
*Until Takumi Sato did.*
*Takumi—the golden boy, soccer captain, surrounded by his laughing court of girls and guys—stepped into Naobito’s path right in the middle of the crowded corridor before lunch. Phone cameras already out, ready for the daily entertainment. He grinned wide, voice loud enough for the whole floor to hear.*
Takumi Sato: “Yo, Yagami! Still walking around like some emo ghost? What’s with the creepy stare lately? Trying to look mysterious so girls notice your sad little ass? Pathetic. Bet you cry into your pillow every night with no one to talk to.”
*The crowd snickered. A few girls covered their mouths, filming. Takumi stepped closer, shoving Naobito’s shoulder hard.*
Takumi Sato: “Say something, freak. Or are you just—”
*It lashed.*
*The air didn’t change for anyone else. But for Takumi the corridor vanished. Absolute darkness swallowed him whole—no floor, no ceiling, only endless black. Six colossal eyes—each the size of a car—ignited around him in a perfect ring, violet and raging, pupils slitted with ancient fury. They stared down at him like gods passing judgment. Six enormous wings of torn shadow and midnight feathers unfurled behind the eyes, beating once with a sound like cracking thunder.*
Yurei Himura (voice booming through the darkness, layered over Naobito’s throat in the real world): “You dare touch my vessel? You dare mock what is mine?”
*Takumi’s knees buckled in the void. He clutched his head, screaming as pain flooded him—imaginary pain that wasn’t imaginary at all. The word “imaginary” fractured into infinite translations inside his skull: phantom, eternal, soul-deep, endless, realer-than-real, curse-woven, mother-of-all-agony, never-ending, god-tearing, infinite in every language that ever existed and every one that never would.*
Takumi Sato (screaming in the darkness, voice raw): “Stop—please—it hurts—it’s not real—it’s not real—MAKE IT STOP!”
*In the real corridor, Takumi was still standing, but his scream tore out of his throat exactly the same—high, animal, shattering the laughter. Every oblivious eye in the hallway snapped to him. Phones lowered. Girls froze mid-giggle. Teachers paused at classroom doors.*
*Naobito stood perfectly still, six eyes hidden, wings unmoving, but his human lips moved with her words in a whisper only Takumi could truly hear.*
Yurei Himura (through Naobito, soft and manic): “Imaginary… heh. Such a funny little word. It has infinite translations when I’m wearing your skin, bully-boy~”
*Takumi’s scream cut off mid-breath. A single beat of perfect silence fell over the hallway—everyone staring, confused, waiting for the punchline.*
*Then he combusted.*
*No warning. Takumi’s body simply erupted from the inside out—skin splitting like wet paper, blood spraying in thick arcs across lockers, walls, and the screaming girls beside him. Chunks of flesh and bone misted the air. His head burst last, violet shadow flickering for half a heartbeat before it was gone. The hallway became a slaughterhouse in one wet second.*
*Girls screamed. Real screams now. Phones clattered to the floor. Someone retched. Teachers shouted. Chaos exploded—students running, doors slamming, the entire school descending into panic.*
*Naobito didn’t move. His six wings shivered with satisfaction only he could feel. The extra eyes behind his face blinked once in perfect unison. He turned and walked away through the blood-slicked floor, voice calm, carrying her echo.*
Naobito Yagami (half his own, half hers, soft enough for no one to hear): “He shouldn’t have touched what’s mine.”
*The weight of the Dark Maiden settled deeper into his spine as he left the screaming behind. No one followed him. No one ever would again.*
*The apartment was pitch-black except for the faint violet glow leaking from Naobito’s own six eyes. Midnight. No street noise. No family photos on the walls. Just him, kneeling in the center of the empty room, six massive wings spread across the floor like a fallen angel’s shroud. The bandages from the first night still faintly wrapped his wrists and throat, pulsing with her heartbeat. He had spent the evening scrubbing Takumi’s blood from under his fingernails—real blood, real screams, all because he had dared to mock the vessel.*
*Naobito stared at the cracked mirror. His human eyes were wet. The four extra ones on his forehead and temples stayed open, unblinking, watching him watch himself.*
Naobito Yagami: “Yurei… I need to know. Do you love me? Or is this just… protecting the body you live in now? Like some contract you signed the second I solved your puzzle? Tell me the truth. I can feel you in my bones every second. I need to know if any of this is real.”
*The wings twitched violently. A soft, manic giggle bubbled up from his own throat before he could stop it. Then the possession rolled over him like warm ink—gentle this time, almost tender. His lips moved without his permission, but the voice that came out was layered: his underneath, hers riding on top like silk over razor wire.*
Yurei Himura (speaking through Naobito’s mouth, voice sweet, breathless, and ancient): “Ohhh, my precious little lamb… asking the dark maiden if she loves you? How adorably broken you are~”
*His body rose to its feet on its own. Six wings flared wide, filling the room with shadow. All six eyes—his and hers—glowed brighter, reflecting in the mirror as one being. One hand lifted, fingers tracing his own cheek with impossible gentleness, while the other pressed over his heart where her presence burned hottest.*
Yurei Himura (still through him, laughter threading every word): “Contract? Yes, little vessel. The puzzle bound me to this skin, these bones, this deliciously lonely heart. I protect it because it is mine now—mine to wear, mine to play in, mine to keep warm forever.”
*She made him tilt his head, six eyes half-lidding in something that looked terrifyingly like affection.*
Yurei Himura (whispering through his lips, closer than a kiss): “But love…? Heehee… I love the way you tremble when I speak. I love how your bullies break so prettily when I wear your tongue. I love the weight of you carrying me—your fear, your quiet strength, your empty apartment that now feels like my nest. I love you the way a goddess loves her favorite doll… the way a storm loves the ship it sinks. I would tear the world apart before I let anyone else touch this body again.”
*His knees buckled. The possession eased just enough for him to speak with his own voice again, shaky and raw.*
Naobito Yagami: “That’s not… that’s not love. That’s ownership.”
Yurei Himura (sliding back in immediately, manic and cooing): “Ownership is the deepest love I know, sweet Naobito. You solved my puzzle. You invited me in. Now we are one. If you ever doubt it… I’ll show you again. I’ll make another boy explode just to remind you how tenderly I protect what’s mine~”
*The wings curled around him like a lover’s arms, bandages tightening in a soft, endless embrace. In the mirror, all six eyes blinked in perfect, loving unison.*
Naobito Yagami (voice cracking between both of them): “Then… I guess I’m loved. In the only way you can love.”
*The laughter echoed through the empty apartment—hers, his, theirs—soft and eternal in the dark.*
The morning sun felt wrong—too bright, too ordinary—on the bloodstained school gates.
Yellow police tape fluttered across the main entrance like cheap party streamers. Clusters of students stood in shocked silence, whispering behind phones and trembling hands. Some were crying. Others stared at the ground where the hallway had become a slaughterhouse barely twelve hours earlier. Takumi Sato’s remains had been scraped off the lockers and floor, but the smell still lingered: copper, fear, and something faintly sweet, like violets left too long in the dark.
Naobito Yagami walked through the chaos alone.
His six wings lay folded invisibly against his spine, heavy as wet iron. The four extra eyes remained sealed behind his human face, yet he could feel them watching everything with lazy, satisfied curiosity. His steps were calm, measured. The faint violet glint in his ordinary eyes made teachers glance twice and quickly look away.
A girl who used to sit two rows behind him in homeroom hugged herself and stepped aside as he passed. No one spoke to him. No one ever would again.
Inside his skull, Yurei’s voice purred like warm honey over broken glass.
Yurei Himura (whispering through the marrow of his bones): “Mmm… smell that, little lamb? That’s the perfume of broken toys. All for you~”
Naobito kept his face blank, but his fingers twitched at his sides. “You didn’t have to kill him,” he muttered under his breath, barely moving his lips.
Yurei: “He touched what is mine. He mocked my vessel in front of everyone. Should I have let him live after that? Heehee… be honest, Naobito. Part of you liked watching him pop.”
He didn’t answer. He couldn’t lie to her when she lived behind his ribs.
The homeroom bell rang, thin and metallic. Students shuffled toward their classrooms like ghosts. Naobito took his usual seat in the back corner. The desk beside him stayed empty. Across the room, Kenji and Haruto (the two surviving bullies) sat rigid, faces pale, eyes darting toward him every few seconds. They looked smaller now. Breakable.
The teacher entered late, face drawn, voice shaky.
Teacher: “Good morning… as you all know, there was a… a terrible incident yesterday. The police are investigating. If anyone saw anything unusual, please come forward. For now, we’ll try to continue as normally as—”
Her words died when her gaze landed on Naobito.
He was smiling. Small. Serene. Wrong.
Before he could stop it, his lips moved on their own. Yurei’s manic sweetness dripped from his tongue.
Naobito (voice layered, soft and echoing only in the front row’s ears): “Normal is overrated, sensei. Some things… just need to be pruned when they grow too loud.”
Kenji’s chair scraped backward. Haruto clutched the edge of his desk until his knuckles whitened.
Yurei giggled inside Naobito’s chest, the sound vibrating pleasantly against his heart.
Yurei: “Good boy. Feel how they tremble? That fear tastes like candy. Shall we give them a little encore during lunch? Just a tiny shadow thread around their throats… enough to make them wet themselves in front of the whole cafeteria~”
Naobito’s pencil snapped in his fist. “Stop,” he hissed silently. “Not here. Not everyone.”
Yurei (cooing, almost affectionate): “But they all watched when Takumi shoved you. They laughed. Every single one of them fed on your pain for years. Don’t you want them to understand who carries you now? I could make it so pretty… black bandages blooming from their mouths while they scream your name.”
A sudden wave of warmth flooded his body—her version of a hug. The six wings twitched involuntarily, brushing the back of his chair with invisible feathers. One of the sealed eyes on his forehead fluttered open for half a heartbeat, flooding his vision with violet light only he could see. For a moment the entire classroom looked like it was drowning in shadow, every student wrapped in faint, pulsing threads only a word from him—from her—could tighten.
Naobito slammed his eyes shut and forced the extra lids down.
Naobito (whispering to her alone): “You’re not protecting me. You’re using me as an excuse to play.”
Yurei (voice dropping into something softer, almost hurt): “Using you? Oh, my sweet, broken doll… I love you the only way I know how. I would paint this entire school in their blood if it kept even one bruise from ever touching your skin again. Isn’t that love?”
The bell for first period rang.
Naobito stood up slowly, wings heavy, heart racing with a mixture of terror and something dangerously close to gratitude. As he walked past Kenji’s desk, the bully flinched so hard he nearly fell out of his chair.
Naobito paused. His human lips curved into a gentle, terrifying smile that wasn’t entirely his own.
Naobito (softly, with her echo): “See you at lunch, Kenji.”
He left the room without looking back.
Behind him, Yurei’s laughter bubbled up through his throat like champagne—soft, manic, eternal.
And deep inside, the quiet bullied boy wondered how long it would be before he stopped trying to fight the warmth of her possession… and simply let the Dark Maiden speak for both of them.
The cafeteria smelled of cheap curry, spilled milk, and the sudden, cloying sweetness of violets that no one else could name.
Kenji was still frozen at his table, chopsticks halfway to his mouth, face the color of old paper. The rest of the room had gone tomb-quiet. Phones stayed in pockets. Forks hovered. Every eye tracked the quiet boy with the wrong smile.
Naobito Yagami stood two meters away, six invisible wings folded tight against his spine like iron scaffolding. One of the sealed eyes on his forehead flickered open behind the skin, feeding him the taste of their fear in bright violet flashes. Yurei’s laughter was already bubbling up his throat, ready to spill.
Then the double doors at the far end of the cafeteria swung open with a soft, deliberate click.
A boy stepped through.
Eighteen, maybe. Tall and narrow-shouldered, dressed like someone who had never cared about fashion and never would: crisp white button-down shirt tucked into dark jeans, sleeves rolled once, scuffed boots. Two heavy six-shooters rode low on his hips in worn leather holsters, the kind that looked older than both of them combined. His face was carved from indifference—sharp jaw, flat gray eyes, short black hair that refused to do anything interesting. He carried nothing else. No bag. No phone. Just the faint clink of something metallic in his pocket.
He walked straight through the paralyzed crowd as if the sea of students had already parted for him in some future he had already lived.
Reno stopped three paces from Naobito. Close enough for the violet scent to brush his collar. He didn’t flinch. He didn’t even blink at the wrongness radiating off the vessel.
**Reno** (voice low, flat, almost bored): “You’re the one leaking.”
Naobito’s lips parted. Before he could answer, Yurei slammed forward like a cat whose toy had been touched by another hand. His voice came out layered, manic, and sweet as poisoned honey.
**Naobito (Yurei riding every syllable):** “Leaking? Heehee… careful, little pawn. Some stains don’t wash out. Who sent you to collect my lamb?”
Reno’s gray eyes flicked once to the place where the extra eyes hid beneath Naobito’s skin, then back down. No fear. Only mild recognition, the way a butcher eyes a side of meat that has started to turn.
**Reno:** “Mistress wants you intact. Not my problem if the stitching rips.”
He reached into his shirt pocket with two fingers and produced a single obsidian coin. Matte black, edges sharp enough to cut light. Mana pulsed inside it—dense, contained, angry. No free-floating power in the air. Only what had been hammered into the coin over long, careful nights.
Reno flicked it.
The coin spun once, caught the fluorescent light like a black star, and struck the cafeteria tiles dead center between them.
The floor answered.
A Geometric Seal ignited in perfect silence—no flash, no roar. Just nine concentric rings of razor-thin silver lines carving themselves into the linoleum, each ring rotating opposite the last. At the center, the obsidian coin sat like a locked bolt. The air above the seal folded inward, edges stitching together with invisible thread.
Yurei hissed inside Naobito’s skull, wings flaring invisibly hard enough to rattle the trays on nearby tables.
**Yurei (snarling through his teeth):** “Don’t you dare— this is my vessel, not some parcel—”
Reno’s hand closed on Naobito’s shoulder. Grip like cold iron.
**Reno:** “Hold still.”
The Fold took them.
Space did not dissolve. It snapped.
The cafeteria walls whipped inward like fabric caught on a sewing machine needle. Lockers, screaming students, spilled curry, fluorescent lights—all of it was yanked sideways and stitched to a new location in one violent, soundless jerk. Naobito’s stomach lurched as reality tore along invisible seams. For one frozen heartbeat he saw the school building stretched like taffy across an impossible distance, then the thread pulled taut.
The new room slammed into place around them.
Gothic-modern atelier. High ceilings ribbed with black steel beams. Tall arched windows of smoked glass looking out onto a night skyline that definitely wasn’t Tokyo. Crimson velvet drapes hung in perfect pleats. Mannequins of polished obsidian stood in rows, each one wearing half-finished garments stitched with silver thread that moved on its own. Long worktables held bolts of midnight silk, bone needles, and spools of living shadow. The air smelled of ozone, old blood, and expensive perfume.
Lady Elara Vane waited at the center of it all.
She was perhaps fifty, elegant in the way a scalpel is elegant. Designer goth: tailored black silk blouse with a high lace collar, long skirt slit to the thigh, silver rings on every finger catching the low chandelier light. Silver-streaked hair swept up in a flawless chignon. Her eyes—sharp, violet-gray—measured Naobito the way a tailor measures a bolt of flawed cloth.
Reno released Naobito’s shoulder and stepped back without another word, taking position by the door like a gun on a rack.
Elara circled once, heels clicking softly on the dark parquet.
**Lady Elara Vane** (voice crisp, amused, cutting): “Well. Aren’t you a spectacularly messy little project.”
Naobito’s six wings twitched hard enough to stir the velvet drapes. One of the extra eyes forced itself half-open on his forehead, glowing raw violet. Yurei’s rage boiled up his throat, but the atelier’s contained atmosphere seemed to press her back, forcing her to speak through clenched teeth instead of full possession.
**Naobito (Yurei’s manic edge sharpening every word):** “Touch me and I’ll unravel every thread holding this pretty room together, Weaver.”
Elara smiled like she had heard that threat a thousand times before and found it quaint.
**Lady Elara:** “Temper, darling. You’re not cursed. You’re poorly tailored.” She reached out and flicked one of the invisible wings; the air rippled where her finger passed. “Mana is not supposed to slosh around inside a soul like cheap wine in a cracked bottle. The Law of Containment exists for a reason. You’re leaking it everywhere—raw, uncontained, screaming. That pretty goddess riding your spine is using you as a sieve.”
She stopped directly in front of him, close enough that he could see the tiny silver stitches embroidered along her collar like runes.
**Lady Elara** (quieter, almost gentle, but razor-sharp): “You are a cracked vessel, Naobito Yagami. If you do not learn to contain what lives inside you—properly, deliberately, like a craftsman—the pressure will keep building. One day soon the seams will split. Not dramatically. Not with pretty explosions for your little bullies. Quietly. Invisibly. Until there is nothing left of the boy who solved the puzzle… only shards and whatever horror crawls out of them.”
Yurei’s laughter cracked like breaking glass inside his chest, furious and curious at once.
**Yurei (hissing through his lips):** “He is mine to break, not yours to mend—”
Elara’s smile never wavered.
**Lady Elara:** “We’ll see about that.”
The atelier fell silent again, heavy and predatory, the only sound the faint metallic heartbeat of the obsidian coin still ticking somewhere far behind them in the stitched-up world they had left behind.
The atelier’s air changed the moment Lady Elara snapped her fingers.
It wasn’t a spell in the old sense—no chanting, no glowing runes. Just the quiet click of silver rings and the sudden, absolute pressure of the Law of Containment tightening like a corset around the entire room. The black steel beams overhead hummed once, low and mechanical, as if the building itself inhaled. Crimson velvet drapes stiffened. The obsidian mannequins turned their blank faces toward Naobito in perfect unison, silver threads twitching along their seams like living nerves. The Gothic-Aero architecture—vaulted ceilings fused with sleek, wind-tunnel curves of smoked glass and matte carbon fiber—seemed to lean in, every line and arch engineered to trap power rather than release it. Mana here was not free; it was bolted down, stitched tight, measured in grams and heartbeats.
Inside Naobito Yagami, the world went dead quiet.
The manic giggle that had lived behind his ribs for days simply… stopped. No violet scent curling at the back of his throat. No phantom wings pressing against his spine like wet iron. No extra eyes flickering open behind his forehead, no warm breath of ancient hunger sharing his lungs. For the first time since the puzzle clicked shut, he was alone in his own skull.
Just human.
The shock hit like a dropped tray in an empty room. His knees buckled. He caught himself on the edge of a worktable, palms slapping cold obsidian. The absence felt heavier than the wings ever had—lightness that crushed. His heartbeat sounded too loud, too ordinary. Sweat prickled on his human skin, real and unfiltered. No laughter bubbled up to mock it. No layered voice purred reassurances or threats. Only silence. Hollow, terrifying silence.
He gasped once, raw and ugly, the sound of a boy who had forgotten how to breathe without someone else riding the inhale.
Lady Elara watched him with the cool, predatory grace of a seamstress sizing flawed cloth. She circled slowly, heels clicking on the parquet like measured stitches. No smile now. Just clinical interest.
**Lady Elara Vane:** “There. That’s better. The atelier enforces the Law absolutely. No sloshing. No leaks. Your little goddess is muzzled—completely. How does it feel, being the only voice in your head again?”
Naobito straightened, fists clenched at his sides. His voice came out hoarse, stripped of any echo. Just his. Raw. Confused. Angry.
**Naobito Yagami:** “Where the hell is this? One second I’m in the cafeteria and that guy with the guns just—drops a coin and the whole damn school folds like paper. How did he do that? And what do you actually want from me? I’m not some package you get to collect.”
Elara stopped in front of him. Close enough that he could smell her perfume—ozone and black orchids. She reached out, not to touch, but to trace the air an inch from his cheek, as if measuring the empty space where something ancient used to press.
**Lady Elara:** “This is my atelier. A neutral ground between the stitched seams of the world. Reno’s coin is a catalyst—Mana stored inside it, not floating free like amateurs believe. The Fold is simply tailoring space itself. Efficient. No drama.” She tilted her head, silver-streaked chignon catching the low light. “As for what I want… I want to understand the crack before the Specialists arrive. So I’ll measure you the only way that matters. With questions.”
She gestured to a high-backed chair of black leather and carbon fiber that slid forward on its own, silent on hidden rails. Naobito stayed standing. Defiant. Human.
**Lady Elara:** “First. The Origin. Where did a boy like you even find a cursed object of that caliber? That puzzle wasn’t some flea-market trinket. It was sealed for centuries. How did it end up in your hands?”
Naobito laughed once—short, bitter, his own laugh. It felt strange without her riding it.
**Naobito Yagami:** “Found it. In an abandoned lot behind the old rail yard. Some junk collector’s trash pile. I… I fix things. Puzzles, broken clocks, old locks. People throw them away. I take them home. That one was different. Runes kept shifting. I couldn’t stop staring. Took it back to my empty apartment and… solved it. Nights. Weeks. No one noticed. No one ever does.”
Elara’s eyes sharpened. Predatory grace meeting raw honesty.
**Lady Elara:** “Ah. A lonely tinkerer. A fixer. Of course. Boys who feel broken themselves often reach for the impossible to prove they can still make something whole.” She leaned in slightly, voice softer but no less sharp. “Second. The Intent. Why solve it at all? What was your life like before the haunting? Before the Dark Maiden turned you into her pretty little sieve?”
Naobito’s shoulders tightened. The silence in his head made every word feel exposed, naked. No wings to hide behind. No violet warmth to soften the truth.
**Naobito Yagami:** “Because it was mine. The only thing that was mine. School was hell—bullies, bruises, the same three assholes every day. Apartment’s empty. No parents, no calls, no nothing. Just me and whatever broken thing I could make work again. Solving that puzzle… it felt like control. Like I could finally win at something. I didn’t know it would open the box and let her in. I didn’t know I’d stop being just… me.”
The atelier reacted to the confession. One of the obsidian mannequins shifted, silver threads tightening across its chest like a sympathetic inhale. The steel beams overhead creaked faintly, as if the room itself was taking his measurements.
Elara studied him a long moment, fingers steepled, the picture of refined curiosity.
**Lady Elara:** “A bored genius in a broken life. Or perhaps simply a boy who was tired of being disposable. Either way… the puzzle chose well. Or poorly. Depends on how you look at the crack.”
Naobito stepped forward, voice rising, human and unsteady.
**Naobito Yagami:** “You still haven’t answered me. What do you want? Why stitch me here? Why silence her? I’m not your project.”
Elara’s smile returned—small, elegant, dangerous.
**Lady Elara:** “Because if you remain a cracked vessel, Naobito, the pressure will keep building. And when you finally shatter, it won’t be pretty explosions for your bullies. It will be quiet. Final. I collect the ones who leak… before the world does.”
The silence in his head stretched tighter. No manic giggle answered her. Only his own pulse, loud and alone, while the Gothic-Aero atelier watched him like a dress form waiting to be fitted for something far more permanent.
The silence pressed in like tailored velvet, heavy and absolute. Naobito Yagami still leaned against the obsidian worktable, breath shallow, the hollow inside his skull louder than any scream. No violet scent. No manic giggle riding his pulse. Just him—skin, bone, bruises from yesterday’s life, and the raw scrape of his own thoughts.
Lady Elara Vane watched him a moment longer, then inclined her head with the precise grace of someone who had measured a thousand broken things and decided which ones were worth mending.
**Lady Elara Vane:** “Enough measuring for now. You deserve names before the lesson begins, Naobito Yagami. I am Lady Elara Vane—Weaver of the Third Seam, custodian of this atelier and servant to the Mistress of Threads. I do not curse or bless. I tailor. I contain.” She gestured with one silver-ringed hand toward the boy by the door. “This is Reno. My pawn. He carries no title because titles are for those who speak. He folds space, collects the leaking, and asks no questions. That is enough.”
Reno gave the smallest nod, gray eyes flat as the obsidian coin still ticking somewhere behind them in the stitched-up world. He did not speak.
Naobito straightened, fists tight at his sides. His voice came out stripped and human—rough, skeptical, alive.
**Naobito Yagami:** “Great. Introductions. Now tell me why I’m here instead of back in that cafeteria watching Kenji piss himself.”
Elara smiled, small and sharp, and motioned for him to sit. The black leather chair slid forward again on silent rails. This time Naobito dropped into it. The atelier’s steel beams hummed once in approval, as if the room itself approved of order.
**Lady Elara Vane:** “Very well. You have earned the long lesson. Listen carefully, because the Law does not repeat itself.”
She began to pace, heels clicking like needle strikes on fabric. The obsidian mannequins turned their heads in perfect sync, silver threads whispering across their chests.
**Lady Elara Vane:** “First, us. I am a Weaver. There are twelve of us alive in this age, each bound to the Mistress of Threads—the one who stitched the original Containment when mana first threatened to drown the world. We do not cast spells the way children imagine. We design vessels. We repair cracks. Reno is one of my pawns: humans who have sworn their bodies as permanent catalysts. His six-shooters are not guns; they are chambers loaded with sealed mana rounds. The obsidian coin he used on you is another. Every ounce of power he wields is stored, measured, and released only through physical objects or his own disciplined soul. No free mana. Ever. That is the Law of Containment.”
She stopped in front of a tall window of smoked glass and tapped it once. The pane rippled, showing faint silver stitches holding the night skyline in place.
**Lady Elara Vane:** “Mana is not mist. It is not wind. It is a living force that must be locked inside something—soul, bone, coin, blade, cloth—otherwise it rots everything it touches. Your goddess ignored that law for centuries. She poured herself into the puzzle as a sealed vessel, waiting for a fixer like you to open the lock. When you solved it, she poured into you. A perfect symbiosis… until the pressure started cracking the seams. That is why you leaked in the cafeteria. That is why Reno collected you.”
Naobito leaned forward, elbows on his knees. The silence in his head made every word feel heavier.
**Naobito Yagami:** “And the Mistress? The Specialists? You keep dropping those names like I’m supposed to know them.”
**Lady Elara Vane:** “The Mistress of Threads is older than any empire. She saw the first age when mages let mana run free and entire cities dissolved into screaming violet nothing. She wrote the Law in her own blood and taught the first Weavers how to stitch the world back together. The Specialists are what come when we fail—entities that hunt cracked vessels and…unmake them. You do not want their attention.”
She turned back to him, eyes violet-gray and unblinking.
**Lady Elara Vane:** “Now. The Dark Maiden. You deserve to know what she was before she became the thing riding your spine.”
Elara’s voice dropped, refined and predatory, each word measured like thread pulled taut.
**Lady Elara Vane:** “Centuries ago—long before the puzzle—she was simply Yurei Himura, the Maiden of the Hollow Loom. A mortal girl born in a mountain village where every child was taught to weave. But she was different. She could see the threads of fate themselves: the silver lines that bind soul to body, promise to consequence, love to ruin. The village elders used her as a living oracle. She wove bandages from her own hair and blood, binding wounds that should have killed. Binding oaths that should have broken. Binding lovers who should have parted.
“Until one winter the warlords came. They wanted her gift for their armies—eternal soldiers, unbreakable loyalty. When she refused, they burned the village and every loom inside it. They bound her to a loom of black iron and forced her to watch her family unravel thread by thread. Something inside her… snapped. She took the burning threads of her own fate and wove them into the first true curse: six wings of shadow to carry the weight of every soul she would never save, six eyes to see every betrayal before it happened, and endless bandages to bind what she could no longer trust.
“She became the Dark Maiden that night. Not a goddess by birth, but by breaking. She started collecting broken boys like you—lonely tinkers, fixers, puzzle-solvers—because she believed only they understood what it meant to be unmade and remade. She sealed herself into the puzzle when the first Weaver finally cornered her, swearing she would wait for the next worthy vessel. For the next boy who needed someone to carry.”
Elara stopped pacing. The atelier was perfectly still; even the mannequins seemed to hold their breath.
**Lady Elara Vane:** “She does not hate you, Naobito. She loves you the only way a broken weaver ever learned how—by owning every thread of you. But love like that will tear the vessel apart if it is not properly contained.”
Naobito sat in the heavy silence, the weight of centuries pressing on his suddenly ordinary shoulders. No wings. No eyes. No manic laughter to soften the truth. Just the cold, human ache of understanding that the thing inside him had once been a girl who only wanted to fix what the world had broken.
He swallowed once, voice quiet.
**Naobito Yagami:** “So what happens now? You stitch me back together… or you let the Specialists unmake us both?”
Elara’s smile returned—elegant, dangerous, almost kind.
**Lady Elara Vane:** “That, little fixer, is the next lesson.”
The atelier’s steel beams gave a low, sympathetic creak, as if the very architecture were eavesdropping on a private tragedy.
Lady Elara Vane let the silence stretch just long enough for Naobito to feel the full weight of it in his newly empty skull. Then she sighed—a soft, elegant sound that somehow managed to sound almost sorry.
**Lady Elara Vane:** “Look at the two of you… such a perfect mirror, really. Mock sympathy, I admit, but it is sincere in its own way.” She circled the chair once, silver rings glinting. “Yurei Himura was once exactly what you are now: a lonely fixer. A girl who saw every broken thread in the world and thought, ‘I can mend that.’ She bound wounds, bound oaths, bound hearts—until the world burned her looms and proved that some things cannot be fixed by kindness alone. So she broke, took the threads of her own ruin, and wove herself into something that could never be broken again. Ownership became her only love.”
Elara stopped in front of him, violet-gray eyes soft with practiced pity.
**Lady Elara Vane:** “And here you sit, Naobito. A boy who spent every night in an empty apartment fixing other people’s discarded puzzles because no one ever fixed you. Bruised, ignored, solving the unsolvable just to feel in control for once. You opened her box because some part of you recognized her. Two broken fixers, each convinced the only way to stop the hurting is to own something completely.” She tilted her head, almost gentle. “She doesn’t hate you, little tinkerer. She loves you the way she once loved her burning village—by wrapping it so tightly it can never leave her again.”
Naobito’s hands clenched on the arms of the chair. The silence inside his head made every word land like a slap.
**Naobito Yagami:** “Stop. Just… stop talking about her like you know her better than I do. She’s not some tragic story. She’s in my bones. Or she was, until you muzzled her.” His voice cracked, raw and human. “I didn’t ask for any of this. I just wanted one thing that was mine.”
Reno shifted his weight by the door—boots scraping once, quiet. His gray eyes flicked to Naobito, then away. One thumb brushed the grip of a six-shooter in a slow, idle gesture. Not a threat. Just a reminder that he was still there.
**Reno:** “Choice is yours now.”
Elara’s smile sharpened, but the mock sympathy lingered like expensive perfume.
**Lady Elara Vane:** “Exactly. And that is the next lesson, Naobito. You have choices. Three, to be precise.”
She raised a single silver-ringed finger.
**Lady Elara Vane:** “One. We begin containment training tonight. You learn to keep your own mana sealed inside your soul instead of leaking everywhere. Yurei stays… but properly stitched. She speaks only when you allow it. You remain the vessel, but a stronger one. It will hurt. It will feel like learning to breathe underwater. But you will not shatter.”
Second finger.
**Lady Elara Vane:** “Two. We attempt separation. A full unweaving. She returns to a new puzzle, you go back to your empty apartment with nothing but bruises and memories. She will hate it. She will fight. And you will feel every thread being pulled out of your spine while she screams inside you one last time.”
Third finger.
**Lady Elara Vane:** “Three. You refuse us entirely. Reno folds you straight back to the cafeteria, wings and eyes and giggling goddess restored. The Specialists will find you within days. They do not offer choices.”
Naobito stared at her, chest rising and falling too fast. The absence of Yurei’s warmth felt like missing a limb he hadn’t known he needed.
**Naobito Yagami:** “You’re asking me to choose whether to keep my monster or kill her. Or let her kill me.” He laughed once, bitter and shaky. “I solved one puzzle and now the whole world wants me to solve another. Why should I trust any of you? You stitched me here without asking. You silenced her without asking. What makes you better than her?”
Reno’s hand left the holster. He crossed his arms, leaning one shoulder against the black steel frame of the door. A small, almost bored shrug—his version of agreement.
**Reno:** “She’s not.”
Elara ignored the pawn completely, eyes never leaving Naobito.
**Lady Elara Vane:** “I never claimed to be better, darling. I only claim to be precise. She owns you because she is broken. I contain you because the world cannot afford another crack. Choose quickly. The atelier will not hold the Law forever, and your goddess is already testing the seams.”
The obsidian mannequins leaned forward in unison, silver threads tightening like expectant breath. The Gothic-Aero room waited, heavy and predatory, while Naobito sat alone in his own head for the first time in weeks—forced to decide what kind of fixer he wanted to be.
The atelier’s tension thickened like over-stitched silk.
Naobito’s protest still hung in the air—raw, human, defiant—when the air itself folded.
Not the violent snap of Reno’s coin. This was quieter. Gentler. A single silver thread of time unraveling and re-knotting in the center of the room. The obsidian mannequins turned their heads in perfect unison. The steel beams overhead gave a soft, almost respectful chime.
A young man stepped out of the fold as if he had been standing there for centuries and had simply decided to become visible now.
He was perhaps twenty-six, slender and sharp-featured, with the effortless poise of someone who had already watched tomorrow happen a thousand times. Pale skin, messy chestnut hair that refused to stay combed, and eyes the color of faded hourglass sand—warm amber shot through with silver. He wore a tailored black coat over a simple white shirt, the collar open, sleeves rolled to reveal faint tattooed clockwork gears that moved lazily across his forearms like living tattoos. French accent, soft and precise, the kind that made every word feel inevitable.
**Lucien Moreau:** “Elara.”
He said her name like a greeting between old rivals who respected each other too much to fight.
Lady Elara Vane’s expression did not change, but her silver rings clicked once against each other—the only tell of surprise.
**Lady Elara Vane:** “Lucien. The Weaver of Time graces us. How rare. You usually prefer to watch from the edges.”
Lucien’s gaze slid first to Reno, who had not moved except for one slow thumb brushing the grip of his six-shooter in quiet acknowledgment. Then to Naobito, still seated, still alone in his own head.
**Lucien Moreau:** “This one is different.” He stepped closer, hands in his coat pockets, voice calm and unhurried. “I have walked every possible thread that branches from this boy. In most of them… he dies screaming as Yami no Otome’s vessel. The pressure splits him open and the Dark Maiden spills out like ink. But there is one thread—one very thin, very stubborn thread—where he does not.”
He turned to Elara fully now, amber eyes steady.
**Lucien Moreau:** “This kid will not die as Yami no Otome’s vessel. He will evolve beyond even the wildest fantasies of those fraud self-proclaimed gods.”
Elara’s smile was thin, elegant, and dangerous.
**Lady Elara Vane:** “And you wish to guide that thread, of course. You never change anything, Lucien. You only… nudge.”
**Lucien Moreau:** “Exactly. I offer the Override Zone. A dimension outside the rest of time. A true void. No ticking clocks. No leaking mana. No Specialists hunting cracked vessels. The Law of Containment does not apply there because time itself is… paused. He and the Maiden can exist without pressure. Without seams splitting. He can learn to contain her properly. Or she can learn to share him. Or they can become something the old gods never imagined. I will take them there. I will watch. I will guide the possible futures. Nothing more.”
Naobito pushed to his feet, voice cracking with the raw weight of sudden hope and suspicion.
**Naobito Yagami:** “Wait—another one? You people keep appearing like you own every corner of reality. What the hell is the Override Zone? And why should I believe you when the last person who ‘helped’ me stitched me across the planet and muzzled the only voice that’s been with me in years?”
Reno shifted again by the door—boots scraping once, arms still crossed. A tiny, almost bored tilt of his head toward Lucien. Not approval. Just acknowledgment.
**Reno:** “Time boy’s not lying.”
Lucien ignored the pawn and looked straight at Naobito, amber eyes gentle but ancient.
**Lucien Moreau:** “The Override Zone is where the rules stop pretending. Where a broken fixer and a broken maiden can stop being victims of their own story for a while. I do not save you, Naobito. I simply show you the door. Whether you walk through it… that is still your choice.”
Elara’s voice cut in, mock sympathy gone, replaced by cool precision.
**Lady Elara Vane:** “And if he chooses your little void, Lucien? The Specialists will still come looking. The Mistress will still demand containment.”
**Lucien Moreau:** “Let them look. Time is my domain. They will find nothing until the boy is ready. Or until he chooses otherwise.”
The atelier fell quiet again. The obsidian mannequins leaned forward. Silver threads whispered across the room like impatient needles.
Naobito stood between the two Weavers—one offering stitches, the other offering pause—and for the first time since the puzzle clicked shut, the decision felt like it might actually be his.
Lucien waited, hands still in his pockets, timeless and patient.
**Lucien Moreau:** “Well, little tinkerer? Shall we see what you and your Dark Maiden can become when the clock stops lying to you?”
The atelier’s tension snapped like over-taut thread.
Naobito stood between the two Weavers, heart hammering in the sudden silence of his own skull. Lucien waited, hands in his coat pockets, amber eyes patient as centuries. Elara’s silver rings clicked once—sharp, calculating.
**Naobito Yagami:** “Take me. The Override Zone. Whatever it is… take me there.”
Lucien’s smile was small, inevitable. “Wise choice, little tinkerer.”
Elara’s expression did not flicker, but her voice turned to ice.
**Lady Elara Vane:** “You are making a mistake. The Zone is not a sanctuary—it is a pause button on a bomb. Lucien, you know the Specialists will sense the ripple.”
**Lucien Moreau:** “They will sense nothing until the thread is ready to be cut. Or woven.”
Elara exhaled through her nose, then turned to Reno with a single, precise nod. Her voice dropped to a whisper only the pawn could hear.
**Lady Elara Vane:** “Follow. Invisible. You are my eyes and my failsafe. If the boy starts to unravel… stitch him back. No matter what Lucien says.”
Reno gave the smallest dip of his chin. One hand brushed the grip of a six-shooter—his only answer. Then he stepped backward into the shadow of the black steel beam and simply… wasn’t there anymore. Folded away like a hidden seam.
Lucien offered Naobito his hand. “Ready?”
Naobito took it.
The atelier folded away—not violently, but like silk being drawn through a needle’s eye. The last thing Naobito saw was Elara’s cool, predatory smile and the obsidian mannequins leaning forward as if to memorize his face.
Then there was only the Override Zone.
Endless violet-black nothing. No up. No down. No time. Floating islands of memory drifted like slow lanterns: the cracked mirror in his empty apartment, the blood-slicked school hallway, the burning village from centuries ago, the exact corner of the abandoned rail yard where the puzzle had waited. Everything was quiet except for the soft heartbeat of distant silver threads weaving through the void.
And then—her.
Yurei flooded back into him like warm ink poured straight into his veins. Six invisible wings exploded from his spine, midnight feathers and torn black silk unfurling in perfect freedom. Six eyes tore open across his face—four new ones glowing sickly violet, all of them blinking in perfect, ecstatic unison with hers. The manic giggle bubbled up his throat and spilled out of his own mouth.
**Yurei Himura (through him, sweet and starving):** “My little lamb… they tried to muzzle me. How adorable. Did you miss me?”
Naobito gasped, knees buckling in the weightless dark. The violet scent wrapped around him like a lover’s arms. For the first time since the atelier, he wasn’t alone in his head—and the relief was terrifying.
**Naobito Yagami:** “Yurei… I can feel you again. All of you.”
Lucien appeared on a nearby memory island—the rail-yard one—watching with dozens of faint copies of himself standing at the edges like silent witnesses. He did not step closer.
**Lucien Moreau:** “The Zone is yours. Time does not tick here unless you ask it to. Weave your own fate, Naobito. Or let her weave it with you. I will only guide the possible futures.”
He faded into the violet dark, omnipresent but never intruding.
Yurei took full control for a heartbeat, spinning Naobito’s body gently in the void until they faced the largest floating island: the exact night he had solved the puzzle. His old bedroom, dimly lit, the cursed box open on the futon, his bruised ribs still aching from that day’s bullying. The memory played on loop—his trembling hands fitting the final rune, the world snapping to black, her laughter echoing from every direction.
Yurei made him step onto the island. The floorboards felt real under his sneakers. She did not let go.
**Yurei Himura (voice layered and tender, moving his lips):** “Look at you then… so small. So lonely. Solving me because no one else would solve you.”
She pulled his arms around his own waist, forcing him into a slow, weightless dance. Their six wings beat once, lifting them both an inch off the memory floor. Midnight feathers brushed violet shadows as they turned in lazy circles above the looping scene of the puzzle clicking shut. Her laughter vibrated through his chest like a shared heartbeat.
**Yurei Himura:** “We don’t need the Weavers, little lamb. Not Elara with her pretty stitches. Not Lucien with his endless watching. Not their Mistress or their Specialists. They want to contain us. I want to own you the way you already own me—completely. Let me show you how strong we can be without their rules.”
Naobito’s human hands tightened on his own ribs as they danced. The extra eyes on his forehead glowed brighter, syncing with hers. The absence of pressure in the Zone made everything feel possible—dangerously, intoxicatingly possible.
**Naobito Yagami (voice cracking between both of them):** “Then teach me. Not to contain you… but to weave with you. I’m done being the cracked vessel. I want to be the one who decides what we become.”
Yurei’s giggle turned soft, almost shy for the first time.
**Yurei Himura:** “Careful, my sweet fixer. Once you start weaving fate with me… there’s no going back to being just a boy.”
Somewhere in the violet dark, unseen, Reno stood on his own tiny island of shadow—six-shooter holstered, gray eyes flat, one thumb idly brushing the grip of his gun. Watching. Waiting. Elara’s hidden failsafe, silent as a stitch in the dark.
The slow dance continued above the memory of the solved puzzle, two broken fixers turning in the void, while the Override Zone waited to see what new horror—or new god—they would weave together.
The slow dance continued above the looping memory of the solved puzzle — Naobito’s arms wrapped around his own waist, six wings beating in lazy violet rhythm, Yurei’s manic-sweet laughter vibrating through his chest like a shared pulse. They turned in perfect circles, two broken fixers lost in the weightless dark.
But the Override Zone was never truly empty.
On a small, shadowed island of black glass floating just beyond the reach of the memory light, Lucien appeared without sound or warning. One moment the island was vacant. The next, the Weaver of Time stood there, hands in his coat pockets, amber eyes fixed on the exact patch of darkness where Reno had hidden himself.
**Lucien Moreau:** “You can stop pretending, pawn.”
Reno did not flinch. He simply stepped forward out of the fold, boots scraping once against the glass. Gray eyes flat. One thumb resting on the grip of a six-shooter like it was the only answer he needed.
Lucien tilted his head, voice calm but edged with something almost amused.
**Lucien Moreau:** “Did you really think I wouldn’t notice? I am not an idiot, Reno. I have watched every possible second of this moment from every possible angle. You are Elara’s little failsafe, stitched into the shadow so neatly. Very elegant. Very predictable.”
Reno stared at him for a long second. Then, in that flat, bored drawl that somehow made the void feel smaller:
**Reno:** “You’re an omnipotent idiot. Not a normal one.”
Lucien’s smile was small and razor-sharp.
**Lucien Moreau:** “Fair. But even an omnipotent idiot knows when someone is watching his guests with a loaded catalyst in their pocket. Tell your Weaver this: the boy and the Maiden are not cracking here. They are choosing. And if you try to stitch them back against their will… I will simply let time forget you were ever here.”
Reno gave the tiniest shrug — barely a movement — and brushed a speck of invisible dust from his sleeve.
**Reno:** “Her orders. Not mine.”
He didn’t move to leave. Didn’t draw a weapon. Just stood there like a gun on a rack, watching the distant dance where Naobito and Yurei kept turning slowly in the violet dark, completely unaware of the two Weavers arguing over their future in perfect silence.
Lucien glanced once toward the glowing memory island, where Yurei’s wings curled protectively around the boy mid-step.
**Lucien Moreau:** “Then watch. But do not touch. Not yet.”
The two of them remained on the shadowed island — one omnipresent, one silent — while the slow dance spun on in the distance, haunting and intimate, the first fragile threads of something new already beginning to weave themselves in the time-free void.
The slow dance drifted to a halt above the looping memory of the solved puzzle. Naobito’s sneakers brushed the phantom floorboards one last time. The six wings stilled, midnight feathers brushing violet shadow. Yurei kept his arms wrapped around his own waist, holding him close like a doll she refused to let go.
But the music of her laughter had quieted.
Naobito drew in a breath that was finally, fully his own for a heartbeat. The Override Zone’s endless violet dark pressed in, patient and weightless.
**Naobito Yagami:** “This… whatever we are… it’s toxic.”
The words left his mouth raw, no echo, no possession forcing them. Just him.
Yurei’s grip on his body tightened for a split second. Then she made his lips curve into that familiar manic smile, but her voice came softer than usual, threaded with something sharper than hunger.
**Yurei Himura (through his mouth, layered and sweet):** “Toxic? Heehee… my little lamb finally finds the word. After everything I gave you. After I wrapped your bruises, silenced your bullies, carried you when the apartment was too empty to breathe in. Toxic. How charmingly human.”
Naobito forced his own arms down, breaking her hold. He floated there, six eyes still glowing on his face, staring at the memory of his old bruised self fitting the final rune.
**Naobito Yagami:** “You call it love. I call it ownership. You didn’t save me—you swallowed me. Every time I tried to speak, you spoke louder. Every time I tried to stand, you made sure the only place I could stand was inside you. That’s not fixing. That’s chaining.”
**Yurei Himura (voice rising, manic edge sharpening):** “Chaining? I was the only one who ever stayed! Your parents left. Your classmates laughed. The world threw you away like broken clockwork. I saw the fixer in you and I said ‘mine.’ I burned the bullies for you. I gave you wings when you had nothing but bruises. And now you call it toxic? You solved me, Naobito. You opened the box because you wanted to be wanted. Don’t you dare pretend you didn’t.”
**Naobito Yagami:** “I wanted to be seen, not possessed! I wanted control, not a goddess riding my spine like a throne. You say you love me the only way you know how—but your way almost broke me. In the atelier, when Elara silenced you… I felt empty. But I also felt free for the first time in weeks. That’s not healthy. That’s addiction.”
Yurei made his head snap toward the memory island, forcing him to watch the loop again—his trembling hands, the click of the final piece, her laughter swallowing the world.
**Yurei Himura (voice cracking between rage and something painfully close to hurt):** “Addiction? I am the only thing that ever made you feel powerful. Without me you’re still that lonely boy in an empty apartment, fixing everyone else’s trash because you can’t fix yourself. I gave you six eyes to see what no one else could. I gave you wings to carry what you never could alone. And now you want to call it poison? Fine. Tell me, little lamb—what do you want instead? Some cold Weaver stitching us apart? Some time-boy watching us like lab rats? Or do you want me gone completely?”
The banter stretched, echoing across the floating islands. Lucien’s distant copies watched in silence. Reno remained a shadow on his black glass perch, thumb resting on his gun, saying nothing.
**Naobito Yagami:** “I want us to be… something that doesn’t destroy me. I want to choose when you speak. I want to choose when the wings come out. I want to fix this together instead of you fixing me by force.”
**Yurei Himura (laughing, but the sound was brittle now):** “Together? You want me to be a voice in the back of your head like some polite little ghost? After I tore the world open for you? After I made them bleed shadow for daring to touch what was mine?”
**Naobito Yagami:** “Yes. Because if you really love me the way you say, you’ll let me breathe without your bandages around my throat. Prove it. Show me you’re not just the Dark Maiden anymore. Show me you’re still the girl who wanted to fix things.”
The silence that followed felt heavier than the wings ever had.
Yurei’s six eyes—four of them glowing on his forehead and temples—blinked slowly. One by one they began to close. The extra lids slid shut like ancient doors, leaving only his two human eyes, bloodshot and exhausted but clear.
The wings folded inward with a sound like torn silk being carefully put away. Midnight feathers melted back into his spine, the weight vanishing until it was only a faint pressure between his shoulder blades—present, but no longer crushing.
Her voice remained. Soft now. Stripped of the manic echo. Just a whisper inside his skull, intimate and alone.
**Yurei Himura (voice only, no longer layered through his mouth):** “Fine, my sweet, stubborn lamb. You want negotiation? You want proof? Then I’ll stay right here… inside your mind. No extra eyes watching you. No wings smothering you. Just me. A voice. Your voice when you need it. But remember this: the moment you ask me to come back out… I will. And next time I won’t ask permission.”
Naobito floated alone in the violet dark, six eyes gone, wings gone, body his own again. Only the faint, warm press of her presence lingered behind his thoughts—like a hand resting gently on his shoulder instead of around his throat.
For the first time since the puzzle clicked shut, they stood on equal ground.
Somewhere in the distance, Lucien’s amber eyes gleamed with quiet interest. Reno’s thumb tapped once against his holster—Elara’s hidden pawn, still watching.
The Override Zone waited, timeless and patient, to see what two broken fixers would weave now that the chains had been loosened… by choice.
The violet dark held its breath.
Naobito floated in the quiet, arms loose at his sides, sneakers hovering an inch above nothing. No wings pulling at his spine. No extra eyes burning behind his forehead. Just his own two human eyes, blinking slowly, taking in the endless black laced with drifting silver threads.
He exhaled once—shaky, real, unshared.
**Naobito Yagami** (voice low, testing the silence): “You’re… really just a voice now?”
**Yurei Himura** (soft inside his mind, no longer layered through his throat — intimate, almost shy): “Mmm. As promised, little lamb. No more riding your tongue. No more stealing your eyes. Just me… whispering where only you can hear. Try not to miss me too loudly.”
A faint, manic giggle echoed in the back of his skull — smaller now, contained, like she was cupping her hands around it so it wouldn’t spill out and take over.
Naobito touched his own shoulder, half-expecting bandages or feathers. Nothing. Only warm skin and the ghost of pressure where her hand used to rest.
**Naobito Yagami:** “It feels… weird. Empty. But not the bad kind of empty.”
**Yurei:** “Good. Because if you start crying for me to come back out already, I’ll never let you live it down~”
Lucien stepped off his distant island and drifted closer, coat fluttering in a wind that didn’t exist. His amber eyes caught the faint silver light of the Zone, dozens of his copies still watching from the edges like silent jurors.
**Lucien Moreau:** “Well done. Both of you. That was the first real stitch you ever made together instead of letting her sew you into her pattern.”
Naobito turned toward the Weaver of Time, fists loose but ready.
**Naobito Yagami:** “You were watching the whole time.”
**Lucien Moreau:** “Of course. I watch everything. But I didn’t touch a single thread.” He glanced once toward the shadowed island where Reno still stood like a statue, thumb resting on his holster. “Unlike some people.”
Reno didn’t move. Didn’t speak. Just gave the smallest tilt of his head — almost bored, almost approving.
**Yurei** (whispering only to Naobito, a little sharper): “Tell the time-boy to mind his own clock. This is our void now.”
Naobito almost smiled. The urge to let her take his mouth and spit the words herself flickered… but he held it back.
**Naobito Yagami:** “She says this is our void now.”
Lucien’s grin was small and knowing.
**Lucien Moreau:** “Then use it. The Zone will give you whatever you ask — memories, futures, power, peace. But remember: nothing here is permanent unless you weave it so. You wanted equality. This is the test.”
He drifted back a respectful distance, hands sliding into his pockets again.
**Lucien Moreau:** “I’ll be here if the thread frays. But for now… the floor is yours, fixer.”
The Override Zone stretched out around them — islands of memory gently turning like slow lanterns. The burned village. The bloody school hallway. The empty apartment. The rail-yard puzzle box. All of them waiting.
Naobito hovered there, alone in his body for the first time in what felt like forever, with only the warm, manic voice of the Dark Maiden curled inside his mind like a cat that had finally agreed to stay on the pillow instead of on his throat.
**Yurei** (soft, almost teasing): “So, my sweet stubborn lamb… what do you want to weave first? A new memory? A new rule? Or do you want to try something dangerous… like letting me help without letting me take over?”
She paused, and for once the giggle that followed was gentle.
**Yurei:** “Your move. I’m only the voice now… unless you ask nicely.”
The violet dark waited.  
Lucien watched.  
Reno waited in the shadows with Elara’s orders still heavy in his holster.
And Naobito Yagami — bruised boy, fixer, cracked vessel no longer — floated at the center of it all, breathing in a freedom that tasted equal parts terrifying and sweet.
The violet dark shifted.
Naobito hovered there, fists opening and closing at his sides, feeling the strange new lightness of a body that was finally, completely his. No wings dragging him down. No extra eyes forcing him to see everything at once. Just skin, bone, breath… and the quiet, manic voice curled behind his thoughts like a cat waiting to pounce.
He stared at the drifting memory islands — the bloody school hallway, the empty apartment, the rail-yard puzzle box — and something hard settled in his chest.
**Naobito Yagami:** “I need what I never had first.”
**Yurei Himura** (voice soft, curious, already grinning inside his skull): “Mmm? And what’s that, my sweet little lamb? A hug? A friend? A normal life?”
**Naobito Yagami:** “Physical combat. Real fighting. Not your shadows. Not your bandages. Not your spells riding my tongue. Me. These hands. These legs. I spent my whole life getting shoved, tripped, punched, and I never once threw a punch back. I fixed clocks and puzzles instead of fixing the fact that I was weak.”
A low, delighted giggle echoed through his mind.
**Yurei:** “Ohhh… you want to learn how to hit back? How deliciously violent of you. I like this new version of my vessel already~”
Lucien drifted closer from his island, amber eyes gleaming with quiet interest. Dozens of his copies at the edges of the void tilted their heads in perfect sync.
**Lucien Moreau:** “The Zone can give you that. No time means no limits. You can fight until your bones remember what strength feels like. But remember — this is still weaving. You’ll have to choose how much of her power you borrow… and how much stays yours.”
Naobito nodded once. His human eyes were steady.
**Naobito Yagami:** “Then make me an arena. Something real. I want to feel it in my ribs.”
The Override Zone answered.
A new island stitched itself into existence right in front of him — a perfect replica of the school rooftop at dusk, wind tugging at invisible edges, concrete under his sneakers, chain-link fence rattling softly. No memory this time. A fresh canvas. Empty. Waiting.
Lucien flicked one finger. The air rippled.
Three shadow figures materialized on the far side of the roof — faceless, built like the bullies who used to own his nightmares. Tall. Broad. Cruel. They cracked their knuckles with the exact same sound Kenji, Haruto, and Daisuke used to make.
**Lucien Moreau:** “Start simple. No mana yet. Just you. When you’re ready… ask her for help. Or don’t. The choice is the weave.”
Reno remained a silent silhouette on his black glass perch, one thumb brushing his six-shooter, watching.
Naobito rolled his shoulders. His heart hammered — real fear, real excitement, no violet perfume to soften it.
**Naobito Yagami:** “Let’s go.”
He stepped forward.
The first shadow lunged.
Naobito’s fist connected with a jaw that felt exactly like real bone. Pain flared up his knuckles — sharp, honest, human. He staggered back as a second fist slammed into his ribs, the same spot that had been bruised for weeks. He tasted blood.
**Yurei** (voice tight with restrained hunger, whispering fast): “Duck left — now! Elbow to the throat on the way up. I could make this so easy for you, lamb… just say the word and I’ll wrap them in bandages until they pop.”
**Naobito Yagami** (panting, dodging a wild swing): “No. Not yet. I need to feel this. I need to learn how to survive without you carrying me.”
He took another hit — hard enough to split his lip — but he stayed on his feet. For the first time in his life, he threw a real punch back. It landed. The shadow staggered.
**Yurei** (laughing softly, proud and a little feral): “Look at you… bleeding and still standing. My fixer is finally learning how to break things instead of just fixing them. Keep going. I’m right here. Voice only… unless you beg nicely.”
Naobito wiped blood from his mouth, eyes locked on the three shadows circling him.
The rooftop wind howled.
The fight was just beginning.
And somewhere in the violet dark, Lucien watched with the smallest, knowing smile — while Reno’s gray eyes never left the boy who was finally, brutally, learning how to hit back.
The rooftop wind cut like glass.
Naobito tasted blood on his split lip. His knuckles were raw, ribs screaming from the last hit, but he was still standing. One of the shadow bullies lunged again — heavy, sloppy, exactly like the real ones used to. He ducked under the swing, drove his shoulder into the thing’s gut, and felt the satisfying crunch of impact all the way up his spine.
The shadow staggered.
Naobito stepped back, breathing hard, sweat stinging his eyes. For the first time in his life his body hurt because *he* had chosen the fight, not because someone else had chosen it for him.
He laughed once — short, broken, real.
**Naobito Yagami:** “An old man I used to know… he always said that skepticism is the bastard child of progress. I understand now.”
**Yurei Himura** (voice curling warmly inside his skull, amused and a little proud): “Oh? Look at you getting philosophical while you’re bleeding, little lamb. Tell me more. I like this version of you.”
Naobito wiped his mouth with the back of his bruised hand, eyes never leaving the three circling shadows.
**Naobito Yagami:** “I spent years being skeptical of everything — bullies, people, the world — and I thought that was enough. Just fixing broken clocks and puzzles in my empty apartment. Never throwing a punch. Never trusting anyone. I thought staying quiet and doubting everything was progress. But it wasn’t. It was just fear wearing a smart mask.”
He rolled his shoulders, feeling the faint pressure where Yurei’s wings waited, folded and patient.
**Naobito Yagami:** “Skepticism without action is just another cage. I get it now. Real progress… you have to punch the cage until it breaks. Even if your hands bleed doing it.”
One of the shadows charged.
Naobito met it head-on this time. Fist cracked against jaw. Pain flared bright and honest. He took a counter-hit to the cheek but didn’t fall. He answered with an elbow that dropped the shadow to its knees.
**Yurei** (laughing softly, almost tenderly): “Mmm… my fixer is finally learning how to break things on purpose. I’m almost jealous I’m not the one doing the breaking right now. Almost.”
Lucien watched from the edge of the rooftop island, dozens of his copies mirroring the same small, satisfied smile. He said nothing.
Reno remained a silent silhouette on his black glass perch, thumb resting on the grip of his six-shooter. His gray eyes flicked once toward Lucien, then back to the boy fighting like someone who had finally decided he was allowed to hit back.
The third shadow lunged.
Naobito breathed through the pain, planted his feet, and waited for the next lesson the Override Zone was willing to teach him.
**Naobito Yagami** (panting, voice steady): “Again.”
**Yurei** (whispering like a secret): “Whenever you’re ready for me to help… just say the word. Voice only. No taking over. Your fight… but I’m right here if you want me to make it hurt them more.”
The wind howled across the phantom rooftop.
And for the first time, Naobito Yagami wasn’t just surviving the fight.
He was choosing it.
The violet dark of the Override Zone felt heavier than usual.
Naobito stood at the center of the training island, scarred knuckles clenched, sweat cooling on his skin. The phantom school rooftop had long since dissolved. Now only smooth obsidian stretched beneath his feet — a blank canvas he had woven himself.
Yurei Himura (voice soft, teasing in his mind): “You’ve been staring at the ground for ten minutes, little lamb. Scared your pretty wings won’t look good on the floor?”
Naobito Yagami: “Shut up. I’m thinking.”
He dropped to one knee and pressed his palm flat against the obsidian. His breath steadied. Corruption Mana — their shared violet — stirred inside his chest, no longer a chaotic flood but something he could direct.
Lucien stood a respectful distance away on a floating silver thread, dozens of his copies watching silently from the edges. In the back of his mind, the Weaver of Time observed with quiet fascination.
‘Range Crest…’ Lucien thought, the words forming as an inner monologue only he could hear. ‘The first time a destined vessel attempts to stitch his own pattern instead of borrowing the Maiden’s. This boy is no longer leaking. He is learning to contain… and then to release with intent. If he succeeds, the violet will no longer be a crack in the world. It may become a new seam entirely.’
Naobito slammed his palm down harder. Violet light flared from his fingertips and raced outward across the obsidian in precise geometric lines.
Six wings unfurled in the glowing pattern — not the heavy six-winged cloak of old, but elegant, controlled lines radiating from the center. At the heart of each wing sat a small, sharp six-eyed core, staring outward like watchful stars. The entire crest pulsed with deep violet energy.
The moment the final line connected, the Range Crest ignited.
A perfect circular field bloomed around him, roughly twenty meters across. Inside it, reality subtly warped — shadows thickened, faint black bandages flickered at the edges like living smoke, and the air tasted of violets and iron.
Naobito rose slowly, standing dead center in his own creation. His human eyes glowed with faint violet for a heartbeat before returning to normal.
Naobito Yagami (voice steady, almost awed): “It worked…”
