Yurei (laughing warmly inside his head): “Of course it worked. That’s our crest, my sweet fixer. Six wings. Six eyes. All yours to command now. Not bad for a boy who used to only fix broken clocks.”
Lucien’s inner voice continued, calm and measured:
‘Range Crest: Sixfold Eclipse. Unique pattern. Destined vessel signature. The boy has taken the first true step toward becoming something the Weavers have never stitched before.’
Naobito exhaled, letting the crest fade. The violet lines dissolved back into the obsidian like ink returning to a well.
Naobito Yagami: “Again. Stronger this time.”
Cutaway – Real World, Tokyo – Three Days After Takumi’s Death
The ruined hallway of the school had been cordoned off with both police tape and silver-threaded barriers invisible to normal eyes.
A woman in a long dark coat stood alone in the center of the bloodstained floor. Mid-twenties, sharp features softened by exhaustion, messy auburn hair tied back hastily. Her eyes were red-rimmed — not from crying, but from the emotional weight she carried far more than logic allowed.
Specialist Saya Kurogane — Rank 5 Neutralization Mage — knelt and pressed her palm to the dried blood. Silver-white mana flickered across the tiles as she activated a small detection Range Crest: a simple geometric circle with interlocking neutral lines.
The residual Corruption Mana hit her like a wave.
Violet. Thick. Ancient. Hungry.
Saya’s breath hitched. She didn’t flinch from the power — she flinched from the feeling behind it.
Saya (whispering to herself): “A kid… just a kid did this? All this violet… it’s not mindless destruction. There’s pain in it. Loneliness. Someone was screaming for help for years and no one listened…”
She stood up slowly, silver mana still glowing faintly around her fingers. Unlike most Specialists who approached leaks with cold calculation, Saya felt everything. The fear. The rage. The desperate need to be seen.
Saya: “If he’s really the source… I don’t want to cage him. I want to understand why the world broke him so badly that an ancient thing had to crawl inside just to protect him.”
Her Range Crest pulsed once, mapping the violet trail that led away from the school and then… vanished. Folded away.
Saya clenched her fist, silver mana flaring brighter.
Saya: “I’m not letting the others erase him without at least giving him a chance to speak. Not this time.”
Somewhere far above, unseen in the violet void of the Override Zone, Lucien’s copies smiled faintly.
The world outside was already moving.
And the boy who had just drawn his first Range Crest was still training — unaware that a woman with silver mana and far too much heart had just decided he might be worth saving instead of sealing.
The Override Zone had begun to feel less like a prison and more like a forge.
Naobito stood at the center of his freshly drawn Range Crest, the six-winged pattern still glowing faintly violet beneath his feet. The six-eyed cores in each wing stared outward like silent judges. Sweat clung to his brow, but his breathing was steady now. Controlled.
Yurei Himura (voice warm and teasing inside his mind): “Again. Push it wider this time, little lamb. Make the wings bite.”
Naobito didn’t argue. He dropped to one knee, slammed his palm against the obsidian floor, and poured more of their shared Corruption Mana into the crest. The violet lines flared brighter. The range expanded — thirty meters, then forty. Inside the circle, black bandages rose like living smoke, twisting and coiling with violent intent.
He stepped forward and threw a punch into empty air.
The bandages answered instantly, lashing outward like whips, cracking against the void with enough force to shatter stone. Naobito followed with a low sweep of his leg. The crest responded again — a wave of shadow rolled across the floor, tripping invisible opponents before they could even form.
For the first time, the power felt like his.
Naobito Yagami (panting, but grinning): “I can feel the difference. It’s not fighting me anymore.”
Yurei: “Because you stopped treating me like a parasite and started treating me like a partner, you stubborn boy. Keep going. I want to see how far my lamb can run before he asks me to carry him again~”
Lucien watched from his silver thread, inner voice calm and analytical:
‘He’s adapting faster than any vessel should. The destined one… Corruption Mana is no longer leaking. It is being woven. If this continues, the boy and the Maiden may truly become something the Mistress has never accounted for.’
Real World – Abandoned Subway Platform, Tokyo – Same Timeless Moment
Deep beneath the city, where normal trains no longer ran, Specialist Saya Kurogane stood alone on a cracked concrete platform.
Silver-white Neutralization Mana glowed softly around her hands as she traced a small detection Range Crest on the wall — a clean circle of interlocking neutral lines. The crest pulsed once, feeding her images and echoes.
Violet residue. Thick. Ancient. Still lingering in the air like perfume after a massacre.
Saya’s chest tightened.
She saw flashes: a quiet boy with bruises, walking through a hallway while others laughed. A golden boy named Takumi exploding into red mist. The faint, manic laughter that didn’t belong to any human throat.
Tears pricked at the corners of her eyes before she could stop them.
Saya Kurogane (voice cracking, emotional and raw): “He was just a kid… carrying something that heavy all alone. No one helped him. No one even saw him until it was too late.”
She pressed her forehead against the cold wall, silver mana flickering unsteadily.
Saya: “The others will want to seal him. They’ll call it containment. But I… I keep thinking what if someone had reached out to me when I was breaking? What if someone had tried to understand instead of just neutralizing the threat?”
A new voice cut through the darkness — calm, refined, and cold.
Lady Elara Vane: “Sentimentality is a luxury we cannot afford, Specialist Kurogane.”
Elara stepped out of a Folded seam, her designer goth attire immaculate even in the filth of the abandoned station. Reno followed a step behind, silent as ever, one hand resting near his six-shooter.
Saya straightened quickly, wiping her eyes, but her voice stayed passionate.
Saya: “You didn’t feel it, Lady Vane. That Corruption Mana… it wasn’t pure malice. It was pain given form. The boy didn’t ask for this. He solved a puzzle because he was lonely. Because the world chewed him up and spat him out. And now we’re hunting him like he’s the monster?”
Elara’s violet-gray eyes narrowed, but there was no mockery this time — only clinical assessment.
Lady Elara Vane: “Pain or not, a destined vessel bonded with an ancient fallen maiden is unprecedented. Their combined Corruption is already rewriting the Law of Containment. If we do not stitch them properly, the leak will become a tear. And tears become catastrophes.”
Reno leaned against a pillar, gray eyes flat.
Reno: “Kid’s getting stronger.”
Elara glanced at him sharply. “How much stronger?”
Reno: “He’s drawing Range Crests now. His own pattern. Six wings. Six eyes.”
Saya’s breath caught. A strange mix of fear and hope flashed across her face.
Saya: “Then he’s not just surviving her… he’s learning to stand with her. That changes everything.”
Elara was quiet for a long moment, silver rings clicking as she folded her arms.
Lady Elara Vane: “It changes nothing until we decide how to contain it. Or whether containment is still possible.”
She turned to leave, but paused.
Lady Elara Vane: “Find him before the other Specialists do, Kurogane. Your bleeding heart might be the only thing that keeps the boy from becoming exactly what we fear.”
The Fold swallowed Elara and Reno, leaving Saya alone once more on the cold platform.
She looked down at her trembling hands, silver mana still flickering.
Saya (whispering): “Hold on, Naobito Yagami… whoever you are now. Someone out here still believes you deserve a chance to be more than just a vessel.”
Back in the Override Zone, Naobito stood inside his glowing six-winged Range Crest, chest heaving, eyes bright with something new — not just survival, but ownership.
Yurei (soft, almost proud): “They’re already looking for us, little lamb. The silver woman with the soft heart… and the cold Weaver who wants to stitch us back into neat little boxes.”
Naobito clenched his fist. The crest pulsed in response.
Naobito Yagami: “Then let them look. When we step out of here, we won’t be running anymore.”
Yurei: “Good boy. Now… make the crest bigger. Show me how much my destined vessel has grown.”
The violet light flared brighter across the obsidian floor as Naobito slammed his palm down again.
Outside the timeless void, the world was already sharpening its knives.
Inside it, two broken fixers were quietly learning how to become something far more dangerous:
A partnership that might just rewrite the rules.
Real World – The Sanctum of Absolute Zero, Hidden beneath Mount Fuji
The air inside the sanctum was cold enough to make breath visible. Perfectly neutral. Not a single stray emotion, not a single uncontrolled flicker of mana was allowed to exist here. Towering pillars of flawless white marble rose into a domed ceiling etched with countless silver Range Crests — each one a perfect, symmetrical pattern designed to neutralize anything that dared disturb the balance.
At the center of the sanctum stood Master Kael Voss, the Weaver of Neutrality.
He was a tall man in his late fifties, silver hair cropped military-short, wearing a pristine white-and-gray uniform that seemed to absorb light rather than reflect it. His eyes were pale gray, almost colorless, as if every emotion had been carefully stitched out of them over decades. A silver mantle embroidered with moving neutral runes rested on his shoulders — the mark of one of the twelve true Weavers.
Saya Kurogane knelt before him, her dark coat dusted with subway grime, her hands still faintly trembling from the residual Corruption she had felt earlier.
Master Kael Voss (voice calm, measured, like a perfectly balanced blade): “Rise, Saya. And speak plainly. I can feel your turbulence from across the sanctum.”
Saya stood, fists clenched at her sides. Even though she tried to control it, her voice came out passionate and unsteady.
Saya Kurogane: “Master… I was at the school. The violet residue is unlike anything I’ve ever felt. It’s not just Corruption Mana. It’s alive. It carries pain. Loneliness. Rage. The boy — Naobito Yagami — he didn’t choose this. He solved a destiny-locked puzzle. He’s the first vessel the Dark Maiden has ever successfully bonded with. Doesn’t that mean something? Shouldn’t we at least try to reach him before the other Specialists decide to erase him?”
Kael remained completely still. Only the silver runes on his mantle shifted slightly, analyzing her emotional state.
Master Kael Voss: “You are still a novice, Saya. Rank 5. Your heart is loud. That is both your greatest strength and your most dangerous flaw.” He slowly walked around her in a circle, hands clasped behind his back. “Tell me what you know of the boy.”
Saya: “He’s… just a kid. Bullied. Orphaned. Empty apartment. Spent his life fixing broken things because no one fixed him. Then he solved her puzzle. Now he carries Corruption Mana measured in the tens of thousands of Seams. He’s already learning to draw his own Range Crests — a six-winged, six-eyed pattern. He’s not leaking anymore, Master. He’s… evolving with her.”
Kael stopped walking. For the first time, a faint crease appeared between his brows.
Master Kael Voss: “Tens of thousands of Seams… and he is stabilizing it. That should be impossible. A destined vessel and a fallen maiden achieving symbiosis is something that has never occurred in recorded history. Not once. The Dark Maiden — Yurei Himura — was always considered uncontainable. Her Corruption is born from betrayal and broken salvation. It feeds on attachment. On ownership.”
He raised one hand. A small, perfect silver Range Crest appeared on the floor beneath him — simple, elegant, and terrifyingly efficient. The air around them grew even colder.
Master Kael Voss: “This is why the Mistress is watching. A vessel who can stand beside an ancient entity instead of being consumed by her… this could rewrite the Law of Containment itself. Or it could tear it apart. The other Weavers are divided. Elara wants to tailor him. Lucien wants to let him evolve. Some want him sealed immediately.”
Saya stepped forward, eyes shining with conviction.
Saya: “Then let me go to him first. Let me talk to him. He’s not a monster, Master. He’s a broken boy who finally found someone who refused to abandon him. If we show him that containment doesn’t have to mean imprisonment… maybe he won’t have to become what they fear.”
Kael looked at her for a long moment. His colorless eyes held neither warmth nor cruelty — only centuries of neutrality.
Master Kael Voss: “Emotion clouds judgment, Saya. But… it can also reveal truths logic alone cannot see.” He exhaled slowly. “You have my permission to observe. Do not engage until I give the word. Report everything. If the boy truly begins weaving his own fate instead of letting the Maiden weave it for him… then we may have to reconsider what ‘containment’ even means anymore.”
Saya bowed deeply, silver mana flickering around her with barely contained hope.
Saya: “Thank you, Master.”
As she turned to leave, Kael’s voice followed her, quiet and heavy.
Master Kael Voss: “Be careful, novice. The Dark Maiden does not share. If that boy has truly earned her loyalty… she will burn the world before she lets anyone take him from her.”
The silver sanctum fell silent once more.
Back in the Override Zone
Naobito stood inside his glowing six-winged Range Crest, breathing hard after another successful activation. A faint, satisfied smile played on his lips.
Yurei (soft, almost purring in his mind): “They’re talking about us, little lamb. The silver girl with the bleeding heart… and her cold master. They’re scared of what we’re becoming.”
Naobito clenched his fist. The crest pulsed brighter in response.
Naobito Yagami: “Good. Let them be scared.”
Time skip:
The Override Zone had no clocks, no sunrises, no endings.
But change still happened.
When the violet dark finally settled again, Naobito Yagami stood on a brand-new island of polished obsidian and silver thread — an arena he had woven himself after countless unseen battles. His school uniform was gone, replaced by a simple black jacket and worn jeans that looked like they had survived a war. His knuckles were scarred, not bruised. His posture was straight, shoulders loose but ready, the quiet confidence of someone who had finally learned how to throw a punch and mean it.
He was still the same boy on the outside. No aging. No gray hairs. Just… sharper.
**Yurei Himura** (voice in his mind, softer now, almost affectionate): “Look at you, my sweet lamb. All grown-up on the inside. I barely recognize my favorite toy anymore.”
Naobito allowed himself a small, tired smile.
Off-screen, in the endless stretch of timeless training, everything had shifted.
He had fought a thousand shadow versions of his old bullies until his body moved on pure instinct. He had lost, bled, broken bones, then stood back up and won. Yurei had stayed true to her word — only a voice, never taking the reins — but she had whispered tactics, warnings, and the occasional teasing praise that kept him going when the pain wanted to quit.
Together they had woven new skills from the raw threads of the Zone.  
Naobito could now summon a single pair of shadow wings at will — not the crushing six-winged cloak of old, but controlled, precise tools he could fold away in a heartbeat.  
He could open two extra eyes on command, not for her to see through, but for him to read lies and intentions like open books.  
He had learned to weave simple Geometric Seals with nothing but a flick of his fingers and a drop of his own blood — tiny catalysts that let him fold small pockets of space for a second or two.  
Most importantly, he had learned how to let Yurei lend him her power without surrendering his body. A partnership, not a possession.
Their dynamic had changed too.
The toxic hunger was still there — she was still the Dark Maiden — but it had been… tempered. She teased less. Listened more. There were long stretches where she stayed completely silent just because he needed space. In return, he had started inviting her in voluntarily during the hardest moments, letting her ride his voice for a single sentence when the fight demanded it. Trust, fragile and dangerous, had begun to bloom between the fixer and the monster who once owned him.
Lucien had watched it all from the edges, dozens of copies nodding in quiet approval.  
Reno had never left his shadowed perch, but even the pawn had started leaving small gifts — a fresh obsidian coin here, a loaded six-shooter round there — like silent acknowledgments that the boy was no longer just cargo.
Naobito rolled his shoulders, feeling the faint, familiar pressure of folded wings between his blades. He looked out across the drifting memory islands and spoke aloud, knowing both Weavers could hear him.
**Naobito Yagami:** “I’m ready.”
**Yurei** (warm, almost proud): “Ready for what, little lamb? To go back and make the world bleed? Or to keep hiding in our pretty void forever?”
**Naobito Yagami:** “Neither. I want to test what we’ve become. For real.”
Lucien stepped forward from the violet dark, amber eyes gleaming.
**Lucien Moreau:** “Then the next weave begins. The Specialists have started circling the edges of this Zone. Elara is growing impatient. Even I cannot hold time back forever.”
From his black glass island, Reno finally spoke — three flat words that cut through the quiet like a bullet.
**Reno:** “Time’s up.”
Naobito cracked his scarred knuckles once. The faint violet glint in his human eyes wasn’t hers anymore. It was theirs.
**Naobito Yagami:** “Good. I’ve spent my whole life waiting for someone else to move first. Not anymore.”
He looked straight into the endless dark, voice steady.
**Naobito Yagami:** “Open the way back. We’re going home.”
Yurei’s soft, manic giggle echoed inside his skull — not loud, not controlling… just excited.
**Yurei:** “That’s my boy. Let’s go show them what two broken fixers can do when they finally stop fighting each other.”
The Override Zone began to fold around them, silver threads pulling tight, ready to stitch them back into the real world.
Naobito stood taller than he ever had before.
The quiet, bruised puzzle-solver was gone.  
In his place was something new — something that had learned to fight, to weave, to share power without losing himself.
And the Dark Maiden rode beside him now… not on his spine, but in his mind, as an equal.
The real test was about to begin.
The Override Zone released them without ceremony.
One moment, Naobito stood inside his glowing six-winged Range Crest. The next, reality folded with a soft, almost polite stitch, and he stepped out into the cool night air on the roof of an abandoned building on the outskirts of Tokyo.
The city lights sprawled beneath him like scattered jewels. The wind smelled of rain and distant exhaust. For the first time in what felt like forever, real time moved around him again.
Yurei Himura (soft, alert in his mind): “We’re back, little lamb. Stay sharp. I can already taste silver nearby.”
Naobito rolled his shoulders, feeling the faint pressure of folded wings between his blades. He didn’t summon them. Not yet.
A figure stepped out from behind an old water tank, hands raised peacefully. It was the woman from the visions Lucien had shown him — auburn hair slightly messy, dark coat, silver mana flickering nervously around her fingers.
Saya Kurogane stopped ten meters away, eyes wide with a mixture of caution and something dangerously close to hope.
Saya: “Naobito Yagami… I’ve been looking for you.”
Naobito studied her for a long moment. No immediate hostility. No drawn weapons. Just raw, unguarded emotion in her eyes.
Naobito Yagami: “You’re the one with the silver mana. The one who feels too much.”
Saya gave a weak, self-conscious laugh.
Saya: “Guilty. Specialist Saya Kurogane, Rank 5. I… I felt what happened at the school. I’ve been tracking the violet ever since. I’m not here to fight you.”
Naobito slowly walked closer until only a few meters separated them. He stopped, hands loose at his sides.
Naobito Yagami: “Before anything else… tell me about yourself. Why does a woman with Neutralization Mana look at me like I’m not a monster?”
Saya blinked, surprised by the directness. She lowered her hands.
Saya: “Because I know what it feels like when the world decides you’re broken and tries to fix you by force. I was a loud, overly emotional girl in a family of strict mages. My mana kept going wild because I felt everything too strongly. They wanted to seal part of me. My master — Kael Voss — was the only one who gave me a chance instead. So when I felt your Corruption Mana… I didn’t just sense power. I sensed a boy who was drowning and finally found someone who refused to let him sink alone.”
She smiled sadly.
Saya: “I know I’m probably being naïve. But I wanted to meet you before the others decided your fate for you.”
Naobito was quiet for a moment. Yurei’s soft, almost approving hum vibrated in the back of his mind.
Naobito Yagami: “The 12 True Weavers… tell me about them. What is their real purpose? Who are they? And most importantly — which ones are on my side, and which ones want me erased?”
Saya exhaled slowly, gathering her thoughts.
Saya: “The 12 True Weavers are the Mistress of Threads’ direct enforcers and interpreters of the Law of Containment. They’re the ones who keep the world from falling apart when too much free mana starts breaking reality. Each of them represents a fundamental aspect of stitching and balance.”
She began counting on her fingers, voice steady but emotional.
Saya:
“Master Kael Voss — Weaver of Neutrality. My master. He believes in absolute balance. He’s not against you personally, but he’s dangerous because he’ll erase anything that threatens the Law.
Lady Elara Vane — Weaver of Threads. You’ve already met her. She wants to ‘tailor’ you. She sees you as a magnificent but flawed project.
Lucien Moreau — Weaver of Time. He’s the one who took you to the Override Zone, right? He’s probably the closest thing you have to an ally among the Twelve. He wants to see what you can become.
The others… Weaver of Souls, Weaver of Flames, Weaver of Silence, Weaver of Chains… most of them are leaning toward containment or destruction. Especially the Weaver of Chains — she believes any vessel that willingly bonds with Corruption this deeply should be sealed permanently.”
Saya looked him straight in the eyes, her voice softening.
Saya: “Right now, only Lucien is clearly on your side. Elara is undecided. The rest… they’re scared of what you and the Dark Maiden are becoming. A destined vessel and an ancient fallen maiden working together instead of one consuming the other… it’s never happened before. They don’t know how to handle it.”
Yurei (whispering with dark amusement in his head): “They should be scared, my sweet lamb. Ask her which ones we should break first when the time comes~”
Naobito ignored Yurei’s comment for now and kept his gaze on Saya.
Naobito Yagami: “And you? Where do you stand, Saya Kurogane?”
Saya smiled — small, nervous, but genuine.
Saya: “I stand with the boy who was drowning… and the girl who chose to drown with him rather than let him die alone.”
The night wind howled between them on the rooftop as the first real conversation with the outside world finally began.
The rooftop conversation had stretched too long and grown too heavy. Eventually, Saya rubbed the back of her neck and gave a tired, almost shy smile.
Saya: “Look… I know this is weird. A Specialist hunting you one minute, then asking if you’re hungry the next. But I haven’t eaten since yesterday, and you look like you haven’t had real food in… however long you were gone. There’s a late-night ramen place two blocks from here. No seals. No mana. Just broth and noodles. My treat.”
Naobito stared at her for a long second. His stomach growled traitorously.
Naobito Yagami: “…Fine.”
Twenty minutes later, they sat across from each other in a tiny, steaming ramen shop tucked down a narrow alley. The place was almost empty — just an old man behind the counter and faint jazz playing from a cheap speaker. Two steaming bowls of tonkotsu ramen sat between them, rich, fatty, and smelling like normal life.
Naobito lifted his chopsticks, then paused.
Yurei (teasing, but with a possessive edge in his mind): “She’s trying to humanize you, little lamb. Feeding you like a stray cat she wants to adopt. How cute. Tell her I don’t share.”
Naobito ignored her and took his first bite. The warmth hit him like a memory he’d forgotten. Real food. Real steam on his face. For a moment, he was just a tired teenage boy again.
Saya watched him eat with soft eyes, blowing gently on her own spoonful.
Saya: “You know… when I first felt your Corruption Mana at the school, I cried. Not because it was scary. Because it felt so lonely. Like someone had been screaming into the dark for years and finally got an answer.” She smiled sadly. “Most people with strong mana are born into families that teach them control. You got thrown into the deep end with an ancient fallen maiden. That’s not fair.”
Naobito lowered his chopsticks slightly.
Naobito Yagami: “Fair doesn’t exist. I spent years fixing broken things in an empty apartment because it was the only thing that felt like control.” He looked up at her. “Now I’m the broken thing… and she’s the one who fixed me. In her own way.”
Yurei (purring, pleased): “That’s my boy.”
Saya leaned forward, her expression earnest and intense.
Saya: “But you’re not broken anymore, are you? I saw the Range Crest you drew. Six wings. Six eyes. That pattern… it’s yours. Not just hers. You’re changing the rules.” She hesitated, then added quietly, “I told my master about you. He didn’t order me to capture you. That’s… almost unheard of.”
Naobito took another bite, chewing slowly as if tasting more than just ramen.
Naobito Yagami: “Your master. The Weaver of Neutrality. He wants balance, right? What happens when balance says I shouldn’t exist?”
Saya’s grip on her chopsticks tightened.
Saya: “Then I’ll remind him that balance without mercy is just another form of tyranny.” She suddenly reached across the table and gently touched the back of his scarred hand for a second before pulling away, embarrassed. “Sorry. I just… I keep thinking if someone had reached out to me when my mana was going wild, maybe I wouldn’t have hurt so many people trying to control it.”
A long silence stretched between them, broken only by the quiet slurping of noodles and the old man humming in the kitchen.
Yurei (voice turning slightly sharper): “She’s touching what’s mine now? How bold. Should I wrap her pretty fingers in bandages, lamb?”
Naobito didn’t reply to Yurei. Instead, he looked at Saya with something almost gentle in his eyes.
Naobito Yagami: “You’re strange, Saya Kurogane. Most people who feel the violet either run… or try to kill me.”
Saya (smiling softly, eyes a little wet): “Maybe I’m just tired of watching broken kids get erased because they became something the world doesn’t understand.”
She raised her bowl and took a big, ungraceful slurp — the most human thing she’d done all night.
Saya: “Also, this ramen is really good. Eat up. Even destined vessels need calories.”
Naobito let out a small, rare chuckle. The sound surprised even him.
For a few minutes, in a tiny ramen shop glowing with warm yellow light, the vessel and the Specialist simply ate together — two people carrying far too much weight, pretending for a little while that they were just tired kids sharing a late-night meal.
But both of them knew the quiet wouldn’t last.
Scene: The Obsidian Penthouse – Top Floor, Hart Spire, Central Tokyo
The penthouse was a temple of arrogance and blood.
Floor-to-ceiling windows overlooked the glittering city like a conquered kingdom. The interior was minimalist luxury — black marble floors, blood-red accents, and subtle crimson lighting that made every shadow look like it was bleeding. In the center of the massive living room, a single grand piano stood untouched for years.
Niro Hart stood by the windows, one hand in the pocket of his perfectly tailored black slacks, the other swirling a glass of red wine that may or may not have been actual wine.
He was 27 now, but looked dangerously younger. Tall, lean, and devastatingly handsome in a cruel way. A crisp white buttoned shirt clung to his frame, sleeves rolled once, with a sleek black vest over it. His dark hair was styled back, a few rebellious strands falling over sharp crimson eyes that glowed faintly with Malevolence Mana.
He carried the grace of a demon who had already won the war and was simply deciding how to enjoy the peace.
A soft click of heels approached from behind.
Mireille Vale, his personal secretary, 25, stepped into the room carrying a black tablet. She was strikingly beautiful — long silver-white hair, sharp features, and eyes that burned with dangerous obsession whenever they landed on him. Her fitted black dress hugged her figure like a second skin. To everyone else, she was coldly professional. To Niro, she was a devoted blade.
Mireille (voice soft, almost reverent): “Master Niro… the quarterly reports are in. Hart Industries now controls 47% of the entire Asian mana-tech market. Three more rival companies dissolved this week. Their CEOs… met with unfortunate accidents.”
She stopped a respectful distance away, but her fingers tightened around the tablet, knuckles white. Her cheeks were faintly flushed — the usual reaction when she was near him.
Niro didn’t turn around immediately. He simply smirked, that lazy, demonic curl of the lips that had ended twelve lives in his own family before he turned eighteen.
Niro Hart: “Forty-seven percent? How boring. I expected fifty by now.” His voice was smooth velvet soaked in venom. “And my dear older sister’s former loyalists?”
Mireille (leaning in slightly, eyes shining with yandere adoration): “All gone. I made sure their deaths were… poetic. Just like you taught me. One of them begged for his life while crying about your sister. I recorded it for you.”
Niro finally turned. The Malevolence Mana around him flared for a brief second — deep, bloody red energy that made the air feel heavier, like the weight of every sin he’d ever committed.
He stepped closer to Mireille, lifting her chin with two fingers. She trembled, not from fear, but from pure, unhinged delight at his touch.
Niro Hart: “Good girl. You’ve always been so eager to please me. Tell me, Mireille… why do you serve a man who slaughtered his entire family without hesitation?”
Mireille’s eyes glazed over with fanatical love. Her voice dropped into an almost whisper.
Mireille: “Because you’re perfect. At eighteen you killed the people who tried to control you, took everything from them, and made the world kneel. You’re not just strong… you’re beautiful in your cruelty. I want to be the blade at your side when you decide the world still isn’t enough for you.”
Niro chuckled lowly, releasing her chin. He walked over to the grand piano and sat down, running his fingers across the keys without playing a note.
Niro Hart: “I’m twenty-seven now. I’ve dominated the market. I’ve erased every enemy. I’ve tasted every pleasure this rotting world has to offer.” His crimson eyes narrowed with dark amusement. “And yet… I’m bored, Mireille. The 12 True Weavers still cower in their little sanctums, stitching the world together like scared tailors. Especially that neutrality fool, Kael Voss, and the thread bitch, Elara Vane.”
He smiled — slow, vicious, and full of demonic grace.
Niro Hart: “Perhaps it’s time I gave them something new to fear. A little violet has appeared recently… a boy bonded with an ancient fallen maiden. Corruption Mana in the tens of thousands of Seams. A destined vessel.”
Mireille’s expression shifted into something hungry and jealous.
Mireille: “Should I prepare a team to eliminate him, Master?”
Niro Hart: “No.” He stood up gracefully, red mana flickering like flames around his shoulders. “I want to meet this boy. A vessel who didn’t get consumed… but learned to dance with his monster? How interesting.”
He turned toward the window, looking out over the city like it was already his personal playground.
Niro Hart: “Prepare the private jet and my best bottle of ‘97. We’re going to pay our respects to this Naobito Yagami. Who knows… maybe I’ll offer him a job. Or maybe I’ll simply watch him burn the Weavers’ precious Law of Containment to the ground.”
Mireille bowed deeply, her yandere smile widening with dangerous affection.
Mireille: “As you wish, my beloved Master. Everything I do… is for you.”
Niro Hart’s demonic smirk deepened as red Malevolence Mana pulsed once through the entire penthouse.
The game was no longer about money or revenge.
At 27, the prodigy who had killed his entire family and conquered an empire was finally looking for something worthy of his boredom.
And a certain violet-eyed boy had just caught his interest.
The penthouse was silent except for the faint hum of the city far below.
Niro Hart stood by the massive window, swirling his glass of blood-red wine, his white buttoned shirt and black vest perfectly fitted against his lean frame. The crimson glow of his Malevolence Mana flickered lazily around his fingers like dying embers.
Mireille Vale approached from behind, her heels clicking sharply against the marble. She stopped closer than professional distance allowed, clutching the black tablet to her chest like a shield. Her silver-white hair framed a face flushed with barely contained obsession.
Mireille: “Master Niro… the reports can wait if you’d prefer.”
She took another step closer, voice dropping into something softer, almost pleading.
Mireille: “You’ve been standing there for twenty minutes. You seem… restless. I could help ease that. I canceled all your remaining meetings tonight. I even prepared the private onsen on the 91st floor. Just you and me. No interruptions. I know how you like silence when you’re thinking about your next move.”
Niro didn’t turn around. A slow, cruel smirk curved his lips.
Niro Hart: “How thoughtful of you, Mireille.”
He finally glanced over his shoulder, crimson eyes glinting with amusement. The look he gave her was the same one a cat gives a mouse — entertained, but utterly detached.
Mireille’s breath hitched. She pressed on, desperate to hold his attention.
Mireille: “The boy with the violet mana… Naobito. I’ve already gathered everything on him. His school records, the police reports, even the leaked footage from the hallway where he killed that golden idiot. I can bring it all to you right now. Or… if you’d rather not talk business, I could—”
She reached out hesitantly, fingers brushing the sleeve of his vest.
Niro turned fully now. In one smooth motion, he caught her wrist, holding it gently but firmly. Not painful. Just enough to remind her who owned whom.
Niro Hart (voice low, mocking, and velvet-smooth): “Look at you. So eager. So desperate to be useful. You killed three men last week just because they spoke my sister’s name. And yet here you are, still begging for scraps of my attention.”
He leaned in slightly, close enough that she could feel his breath against her ear, but far enough that their lips would never meet.
Niro Hart: “Tell me, Mireille… do you really think spreading your legs or drawing my bath will make me see you as anything more than a very devoted tool?”
Mireille trembled. Not with fear — with sick, twisted adoration. Her voice came out breathy and shaky.
Mireille: “I don’t care what I am to you… as long as I’m something. I live for you. I kill for you. I would die for you. Just… let me stay close tonight. Please.”
Niro chuckled darkly, releasing her wrist like he was discarding a toy. He turned back toward the window, sipping his wine as if the conversation had already bored him.
Niro Hart: “You may stay in the room. You may even kneel at my feet while I think about this interesting new violet toy that’s appeared. But that’s all you’ll get tonight.”
He glanced back at her with that signature demonic smirk — graceful, arrogant, and completely devoid of warmth.
Niro Hart: “After all… a good dog should be satisfied with the privilege of watching its master.”
Mireille’s eyes glistened with obsessive devotion as she slowly lowered herself to her knees on the cold marble floor without being told twice.
Mireille (whispering reverently): “Yes, Master…”
Niro Hart looked out over his conquered city once more, the faint red glow of Malevolence Mana dancing across his sharp features.
At twenty-seven, the man who had slaughtered his entire family and built an empire was no longer amused by power alone.
He was looking for something new to break.
And a certain destined vessel had just become far more interesting than his yandere secretary could ever hope to be.
The late-night ramen shop’s bell chimed softly as Naobito and Saya stepped out into the narrow alley. Steam still clung to their clothes, and the warm broth had left a temporary illusion of peace.
Saya zipped up her dark coat, silver mana flickering faintly around her fingertips like nervous fireflies.
Saya: “I know it’s stupid, but… that was nice. Just two people eating ramen like the world isn’t trying to decide whether to seal them or study them.”
Naobito allowed himself a small smirk, hands in his pockets. The faint pressure of folded wings rested between his shoulder blades — ready, but not yet needed.
Naobito Yagami: “Don’t get used to it. Peace never lasts long when you’re carrying an ancient fallen maiden in your head.”
Yurei (amused, purring inside his mind): “Smart boy. She’s growing attached already. How adorable. Shall I wrap her in bandages if she tries to hug you?”
Before Naobito could reply, the air changed.
The alley’s temperature dropped several degrees. A heavy, suffocating crimson aura rolled in like blood mist — thick, regal, and dripping with pure Malevolence Mana. The streetlights flickered. Somewhere in the distance, a car alarm died mid-wail.
A black limousine with blood-red accents silently pulled up at the mouth of the alley, blocking the exit. The door opened without a sound.
Niro Hart stepped out.
He moved with the effortless grace of a demon who had long ago stopped pretending to be human. Crisp white button-down shirt, tailored black vest, hands casually in his pockets. Crimson eyes glowed faintly under the flickering lights as he adjusted his cuffs with aristocratic precision. A slow, devilish smirk played on his lips.
Behind him, Mireille Vale emerged like a shadow, silver-white hair catching the red glow, her obsessive gaze locked onto Niro’s back with terrifying devotion.
Niro didn’t speak at first. He simply observed Naobito like a collector examining a rare new specimen.
Niro Hart (voice smooth, cultured, and laced with amusement): “So this is the fabled destined vessel. The boy who tamed — or was tamed by — the Dark Maiden herself. I must say… the violet suits you better than I expected.”
Saya instinctively stepped in front of Naobito, silver Neutralization Mana flaring around her hands as she formed the beginning lines of a defensive Range Crest on the ground.
Saya: “Niro Hart… You have no business here.”
Niro’s smirk widened, elegant and cruel.
Niro Hart: “On the contrary, little silver dove. When something this interesting appears in my city, I make it my business.” He tilted his head slightly, crimson eyes locking onto Naobito. “Tens of thousands of Seams of Corruption Mana, and you’re still sane enough to eat ramen with a Specialist. Fascinating. Most vessels would have been devoured long ago.”
He took one graceful step forward. Malevolence Mana rippled outward in controlled waves — not attacking, but announcing dominance.
Niro Hart: “Allow me to introduce myself properly. Niro Hart. Twenty-seven. Current ruler of Hart Industries. Slayer of my entire bloodline before I even came of age. And currently… very bored.”
His gaze slid lazily to Saya, then back to Naobito.
Niro Hart: “The 12 True Weavers are already circling you like frightened tailors. Elara wants to stitch you. Kael wants to neutralize you. Lucien wants to watch you evolve.” He chuckled softly. “Me? I want to see what happens when a boy who was once nothing decides the world is no longer allowed to ignore him.”
Naobito met his stare without flinching. The faint violet glow behind his human eyes answered the crimson challenge.
Naobito Yagami: “And what exactly do you want from me, Hart?”
Niro’s smirk turned sharper, almost affectionate in its cruelty.
Niro Hart: “Nothing yet. Consider this a formal introduction… and a warning. When you eventually grow tired of the Weavers trying to contain you, come find me. I could use a partner who understands what it means to burn everything down and build something better from the ashes.”
Mireille shifted behind him, her yandere gaze flashing with barely concealed jealousy toward Naobito, but she remained perfectly silent.
Niro gave a small, theatrical bow — graceful, mocking, and full of demonic charm.
Niro Hart: “Until then, little vessel… try not to die too quickly. It would bore me.”
The limousine door opened again. Niro turned and slid back inside with fluid elegance, Mireille following like an obedient shadow. The car pulled away silently into the night, leaving only the fading scent of expensive cologne and Malevolence Mana behind.
Saya let out a shaky breath, her Range Crest dissolving.
Saya: “That man… he’s worse than most Specialists. He doesn’t follow the Law of Containment. He breaks it for fun.”
Yurei (laughing softly in Naobito’s mind): “I like him. He has the scent of a proper monster. We should keep him… or kill him. Whichever is more entertaining.”
Naobito stared down the empty alley where the limousine had vanished, violet energy flickering once across his knuckles.
Naobito Yagami: “He’s not wrong about one thing. The Weavers aren’t the only ones watching anymore.”
The night felt heavier now.
A new player had entered the game — stylish, logical, and dangerously bored.
And Niro Hart had just decided that Naobito Yagami was worth paying attention to.
The Alley – Moments After the Limousine Departed
The limousine had only gone thirty meters before it stopped.
The rear door opened again. Niro Hart stepped out alone this time, one hand adjusting his vest with casual elegance. Mireille remained inside, watching with jealous intensity through the tinted window.
Niro walked back toward them slowly, crimson Malevolence Mana trailing behind him like a royal cape made of sin. His smirk was gone. In its place was something colder — the face of a man recounting history he had written in blood.
Niro Hart: “You’re wondering why a man like me would bother introducing himself to a boy who still smells like school cafeteria and ramen. Fair question.”
He stopped a respectful distance away, hands in his pockets, posture perfect.
Niro Hart: “Since we’re being honest tonight… let me tell you exactly who I am, Naobito Yagami.”
Naobito didn’t move. Saya tensed beside him, silver mana flickering nervously.
Niro Hart: “I was born into the Hart family. Twelve siblings. All older. All stronger. All crueler. From the moment I drew my first breath, they reminded me I was the mistake. The thirteenth. The spare that should never have existed. Beatings. Humiliation. Locked in rooms for days. My father watched and laughed. My mother… did nothing.”
His crimson eyes gleamed with dark nostalgia.
Niro Hart: “My eldest sister, Seraphine, was the golden child. She was being groomed to become CEO of Hart Industries the day she turned twenty-five. The board had already approved it. I was sixteen when I overheard the conversation. She was laughing about how she would finally ‘put the little bastard in his proper place’ — as her personal toy.”
Niro’s smirk returned, sharp and demonic.
Niro Hart: “So on my sixteenth birthday, I threw a party. Just for them. I walked into the family manor with nothing but a smile and the first true awakening of my Malevolence Mana. I killed them all. Every single sibling. Every guard. Every sycophant who had ever laughed at my suffering. I made it slow for most of them. Artistic, even. The only one I left alive was my mother. I wanted her to live with the knowledge that the weakest child she ignored had become the monster that devoured her entire world.”
He spread his hands elegantly, as if presenting a masterpiece.
Niro Hart: “By sunrise, I owned everything. No board. No opposition. Complete control. Today, at twenty-seven, my mobility division alone — autonomous vehicles infused with controlled Malevolence Mana — nets me eighteen million dollars a year. That’s just one small division. Overall? I am a quintillionaire. I could buy countries and burn them for fun.”
Niro took one step closer, his voice dropping into something almost intimate.
Niro Hart: “I like killing, Naobito. Not because I’m broken. But because the world finally learned it was better to kneel than to humiliate me. The 12 True Weavers fear me because I don’t follow their precious Law of Containment. I break it. Willingly. Joyfully.”
His crimson eyes locked onto Naobito’s with genuine interest for the first time.
Niro Hart: “You and I are not so different, little vessel. You solved a puzzle because the world left you with nothing. I solved my family because they made the same mistake. The only real question is… how long will you let the Weavers treat you like a problem to be stitched away before you decide to become the tear instead?”
Yurei (whispering with dark delight in Naobito’s mind): “He’s delicious. A proper monster who earned his crown in blood. I approve.”
Saya looked visibly shaken, her silver mana flickering unsteadily.
Niro gave a small, graceful bow — theatrical and mocking.
Niro Hart: “Think about my offer. When the Weavers come for you… and they will… you’ll know where to find someone who understands what it means to stop being the victim.”
He turned and walked back toward the limousine without another word. The car door closed with a soft, expensive click, and the vehicle glided away into the neon night like a predator returning to its den.
The alley fell silent once more.
Saya (voice quiet, horrified): “He’s not just a villain… he’s a natural disaster wearing a suit.”
Naobito Yagami (staring after the taillights, violet energy flickering across his eyes): “Maybe. But he’s right about one thing.”
He clenched his scarred fist.
Naobito Yagami: “I’m done being the boy everyone ignores.”
The limousine’s taillights had long since disappeared into the neon haze, but the weight of Niro Hart’s presence still lingered in the alley like smoke after a fire.
Naobito stood motionless for a long moment, staring at the spot where the devil in the white shirt had stood. Then he turned to Saya, his voice low and serious.
Naobito Yagami: “Tell me about him. Everything. Who the hell is Niro Hart, really?”
Saya exhaled shakily, rubbing her arms as if trying to wipe away the lingering Malevolence Mana in the air. She leaned against the alley wall, silver mana flickering weakly around her.
Saya: “Niro Hart is… a walking catastrophe wearing a designer suit. He’s not just rich. He’s not just powerful. He’s a monster who rewrote the rules of power itself.”
She glanced around the alley, then pointed at a discarded soda can lying near a dumpster. The word HART was printed clearly on it.
Saya: “See that? HART. Look around.”
She gestured across the alley and toward the main street visible at the end. A glowing HART TECH billboard. A plastic delivery bag with the HART logo. Even the security camera mounted on the wall had a small HART engraving on its casing.
Saya: “He doesn’t just own companies. He makes things. Everything you can think of — phones, cars, canned food, clothing, medical supplies, mana tools… if it exists in the market, there’s a high chance it has his name on it somewhere. He dominates supply chains most people don’t even know exist.”
Naobito looked down at his own jacket — the one he had woven himself in the Override Zone. A tiny, elegant HART thread was stitched into the inner collar.
Naobito Yagami (frowning): “…Even this?”
Saya: “Even that. He probably didn’t make your jacket personally, but his factories did. That’s how deep his reach is.”
She lowered her voice, becoming more serious.
Saya: “As for his power… Niro can absorb, generate, and convert energy however he pleases. Any kind. Physical, thermal, kinetic, even mana itself. I saw him once — during a raid two years ago — let a live grenade go off in his bare hands. He just stood there smiling while the explosion happened. Then he absorbed it completely. Turned the entire blast into pure mana to replenish his reserves.”
Saya swallowed hard.
Saya: “They measured it at roughly 1.7 million Seams. That’s almost as much as you and Yurei combined right now. And he did it casually. Like it was nothing.”
Yurei (whispering with dark intrigue in Naobito’s mind): “Mmm… a man who eats explosions for breakfast. I’m starting to like him more and more, little lamb.”
Naobito’s expression hardened.
Naobito Yagami: “And the Weavers are scared of him?”
Saya: “Terrified. Not because he’s the strongest in raw power — though he’s close — but because he doesn’t play by any rules. He doesn’t leak. He doesn’t need containment. He just takes what he wants and converts it. The 12 True Weavers have tried to monitor him for years, but he treats them like annoying flies. He killed his entire family at sixteen and built an empire on their corpses. At twenty-seven, he’s bored. And now he’s interested in you.”
Saya looked at Naobito with genuine worry in her eyes.
Saya: “Men like Niro don’t offer partnerships out of kindness. He sees you as either a potential equal… or a new toy to break when he gets bored again. You need to be extremely careful.”
Naobito clenched his fist. Violet energy flickered across his knuckles for a brief second before vanishing.
Naobito Yagami: “He thinks we’re the same. Two people who got tired of being stepped on.”
Yurei (soft, dangerous laughter in his head): “The difference is… you have me. He only has himself and that obsessed little secretary. We’re going to be so much more fun than him.”
The alley felt smaller now. The casual ramen dinner felt like it had happened in another lifetime.
Naobito looked down the street where Niro’s limousine had vanished, his voice quiet but resolute.
Naobito Yagami: “He wants to see what I’ll become. Fine. Let’s give him a show.”
The alley was still heavy with the scent of Niro Hart’s Malevolence Mana when the air folded again — this time with elegant, silver-threaded precision.
Lucien Moreau stepped out of nowhere, hands in his coat pockets, chestnut hair slightly tousled as if he had simply walked through time itself to get here. His amber eyes held their usual calm, ancient patience.
Lucien Moreau: “It’s time, Naobito Yagami. The Twelve True Weavers are gathered. They have decided they can no longer simply watch from the edges. Will you come willingly?”
Yurei (whispering sharply in Naobito’s mind): “Careful, little lamb. This is their court. They want to judge us.”
Naobito looked at Saya once. She gave him a small, worried nod.
Naobito Yagami: “Lead the way.”
Lucien smiled faintly and placed a hand on Naobito’s shoulder. The world stitched itself sideways in a smooth, almost gentle Fold.
They arrived in The Loom Sanctum — a vast, circular chamber floating in a pocket dimension of pure silver and midnight threads. Twelve towering obsidian thrones formed a perfect ring beneath a domed ceiling where countless Range Crest patterns slowly rotated like living constellations.
Eleven Weavers were already seated. Reno stood silently behind Elara’s throne like a loaded weapon.
Lucien guided Naobito to the center of the circle before taking his own seat.
Lucien Moreau: “Brothers and sisters of the Loom… the destined vessel has arrived.”
One by one, the Twelve True Weavers regarded him.
Lady Elara Vane (Weaver of Threads, elegant British-French): sat with perfect posture, silver-streaked hair in a flawless chignon, her violet-gray eyes measuring him like fabric. “We meet again, little project.”
Master Kael Voss (Weaver of Neutrality, stern German): The silver-haired man Naobito had heard about from Saya. His colorless eyes held no emotion. “You reek of Corruption… yet you stand stable. Interesting.”
Lucien Moreau (Weaver of Time, French): Already known. He simply nodded from his throne.
Reno stood guard behind Elara, gray eyes flat, one thumb resting on his six-shooter. He gave the smallest nod of recognition.
Amara Khalil (Weaver of Souls, Egyptian, gentle and regal): A graceful woman in flowing white-and-gold robes, dark skin glowing with faint aqua Pure Mana. Her voice was soft, almost motherly. “So this is the boy bonded with the Dark Maiden. I sense great pain in you… and great potential for healing.”
Viktor Drago (Weaver of Chains, Russian, massive and imposing): A towering man with a thick black beard and cold steel eyes, wrapped in heavy iron-gray chains that moved on their own. His voice was deep and guttural. “A vessel that does not break. I have crushed stronger things for less.”
Mei Ling Zhao (Weaver of Silence, Chinese, serene and deadly): A petite woman in dark silks, eyes closed as if listening to something no one else could hear. She said nothing — only offered a small, unreadable smile.
Isabella Cortez (Weaver of Flames, Mexican-Brazilian, fiery): Passionate, with wild dark curls and eyes like burning embers. Red-orange mana flickered around her fingers. “Finally, someone interesting. I was getting tired of sealing weak leaks.”
Omar al-Sayed (Weaver of Sands, Egyptian-Arab, calm and ancient): A middle-aged man with a neatly trimmed beard, dressed in flowing desert tones. “The desert has seen many storms. You may become the greatest one yet.”
Asha Nkosi (Weaver of Beasts, South African, powerful and wild): A tall, muscular woman with intricate tribal patterns glowing on her dark skin. Her presence felt primal. “The beast inside you is strong. Will it consume you, or will you ride it?”
Dr. Elias Thorn (Weaver of Flesh, British, cold and clinical): A gaunt man in a tailored suit, fingers twitching like scalpels. “Fascinating biology. A perfect symbiosis between mortal and fallen goddess. I would love to study your seams.”
Freya Haldorsdottir (Weaver of Storms, Icelandic, fierce and elemental): A pale woman with white-blonde hair braided with silver runes, lightning crackling faintly in her eyes. “The storm approaches. Will you be its eye… or its ruin?”
Soren Blackwood (Weaver of Shadows, Scottish, mysterious and grim): Cloaked in living darkness, his face partially obscured. “The shadows whisper your name, boy. They are hungry.”
The final Weaver, seated at the highest throne, was a quiet older woman with pure white hair and a gentle but terrifying presence — High Weaver Selene Arkwright (Weaver of Fate, American, the unofficial leader among them).
High Weaver Selene Arkwright: “You stand before the Twelve True Weavers, Naobito Yagami — the only living soul to ever successfully bond with Yurei Himura, the Dark Maiden. The Mistress of Threads watches through us. Speak. Why should we not seal you where you stand?”
The entire sanctum fell into heavy silence. All twelve gazes — from different cultures, eras, and philosophies — weighed upon the boy in the center.
Yurei (laughing softly but dangerously in his mind): “Look at them, little lamb. So many powerful tailors… and not one of them understands what we are becoming.”
Naobito stood tall in the center of the circle, six potential wings folded invisibly against his back, and met their collective stare without flinching.
The heavy silence in the Loom Sanctum was absolute. Twelve ancient powers stared down at the boy standing alone in the center of their circle.
Naobito didn’t look at High Weaver Selene Arkwright when she posed her question. Instead, he slowly turned his head toward the one Weaver he actually trusted in this room.
Naobito Yagami: “Lucien.”
Lucien Moreau tilted his head slightly, amber eyes gleaming with faint amusement.
Naobito Yagami: “You told me it was time. But if I’m going to stand trial in your court, I’d prefer to know exactly who is judging me. I know you. I know Lady Elara still views me as an interesting sewing project.” His gaze flicked briefly to the stern German Weaver. “And Master Kael Voss’s reputation for neutrality precedes him.”
He turned slowly, letting his eyes sweep across the obsidian thrones with calm, calculated defiance.
Naobito Yagami: “I can already tell the Weaver of Chains would rather wrap me in iron before I finish speaking.” His eyes softened just a fraction as they landed on the gentle Egyptian woman. “And the Weaver of Souls seems to be the only one here offering any grace. But the rest of you? I’m at a disadvantage, Lucien. Introduce us properly.”
A low, dangerous growl rumbled through the chamber. Viktor Drago’s iron-gray chains clinked and tightened as the massive Russian leaned forward, clearly insulted by the boy’s audacity.
Yurei Himura (laughing delightedly inside Naobito’s mind): “That’s my lamb. Make them acknowledge you. Make them look at what we’ve become.”
High Weaver Selene Arkwright narrowed her eyes, but after a long pause, she raised one elegant hand, silencing the room. She gave Lucien a single nod.
Lucien Moreau stood slowly, brushing invisible dust from his coat with that timeless grace.
Lucien Moreau: “Very well, Naobito. A fair request from a guest of the Loom.”
He gestured gracefully around the circle, his ancient voice carrying through the sanctum with calm authority.
Lucien Moreau: “To Amara’s left sits Mei Ling Zhao, Weaver of Silence.” The petite Chinese woman offered the faintest, unreadable smile, her eyes still closed. “Beside her is Isabella Cortez, Weaver of Flames.” The fiery Brazilian-Mexican woman grinned, embers dancing between her fingers. “Then Omar al-Sayed, Weaver of Sands.” The calm Arab man inclined his head respectfully. “Asha Nkosi, Weaver of Beasts.” The powerful South African woman watched him with primal intensity. “Dr. Elias Thorn, Weaver of Flesh.” The gaunt British man studied Naobito like a fascinating dissection. “Freya Haldorsdottir, Weaver of Storms.” The fierce Icelandic woman’s eyes crackled with lightning. “And finally, Soren Blackwood, Weaver of Shadows.” The cloaked Scottish man simply inclined his head, darkness shifting around him.
Lucien finished the circle and sat back down.
Lucien Moreau: “You now stand before all Twelve True Weavers, Naobito Yagami. The question remains.”
High Weaver Selene Arkwright leaned forward, her voice carrying the weight of centuries.
High Weaver Selene Arkwright: “Indeed. Why should we not seal you where you stand? You carry an ancient fallen maiden whose Corruption Mana threatens the very Law of Containment. You are unprecedented. Dangerous. Explain yourself, destined vessel.”
All twelve Weavers — and Reno standing silently behind Elara — waited.
The spotlight was now fully on Naobito.
Yurei (voice low and excited in his head): “This is it, little lamb. Show them. Make them understand that sealing us would be the biggest mistake these tailors have ever made.”
Naobito stood tall in the center of the circle, scarred hands loose at his sides, violet energy flickering faintly behind his human eyes. He took a slow breath, then spoke with quiet, unshakable confidence.
The Loom Sanctum was thick with tension. Twelve ancient gazes bore down on the boy in the center like needles ready to stitch him into oblivion.
Naobito stood motionless for a long moment, then his eyes drifted to High Weaver Selene Arkwright’s left shoulder. There, glowing faintly in an ancient geometric script — not letters, but elegant, ever-shifting patterns that moved like living threads — was a single complex sigil.
His head tilted slightly.
Naobito Yagami: “High Weaver? What does that rank signify?”
A ripple of shock passed through the chamber.
Master Kael Voss’s colorless eyes widened a fraction. Isabella Cortez’s flames flickered unsteadily. Viktor Drago’s chains rattled once. Even the serene Mei Ling Zhao opened her eyes. Expressions ranged from open worry to stunned awe. Only Lucien remained perfectly calm, a faint knowing smile playing at the corner of his lips.
Omar al-Sayed, the Weaver of Sands, leaned forward, his voice filled with genuine astonishment.
Omar al-Sayed: “You can read this?”
Naobito nodded calmly, as if it were the most natural thing in the world.
Naobito Yagami: “Hai. Hers says ‘High Weaver’… while the rest of you have ‘True Weaver’ written across your shoulders.”
The sanctum fell into dead silence.
Several Weavers instinctively glanced at their own shoulders or wrists, where the same ancient geometric language was subtly inscribed. Freya Haldorsdottir’s lightning crackled louder. Dr. Elias Thorn’s fingers twitched violently.
High Weaver Selene Arkwright’s eyes narrowed sharply.
Lucien Moreau finally stood once more, his voice carrying calm authority through the stunned chamber.
Lucien Moreau: “They are surprised, Naobito, because only the Mistress of Threads herself is supposed to be able to read this geometric language. It is older than any written tongue. Older than the Law of Containment. It is the language in which reality was first stitched together.”
He glanced around at his fellow Weavers, a small, cryptic smile on his face.
Lucien Moreau: “I know exactly why you can read it… but I will not tell them. The Mistress has asked that this particular surprise remain hers for now.”
The tension in the room skyrocketed. Viktor Drago slammed a massive fist on the arm of his throne, chains exploding outward with metallic fury.
Viktor Drago: “This boy reads the Mistress’s own script and you speak of surprises, Time Weaver? He is an anomaly that must be contained before—”
Amara Khalil (Weaver of Souls, gentle but firm): “Enough, Viktor. The boy is not attacking us. He is simply… seeing more than we expected.”
Naobito remained standing calmly in the center, violet energy flickering faintly behind his eyes. Yurei’s soft, delighted laughter echoed in his mind.
Yurei: “They’re scared now, little lamb. Good. Let them feel how small their needles are compared to us.”
High Weaver Selene Arkwright raised her hand again, silencing the growing murmurs. Her voice was measured, but there was a new edge of caution in it.
High Weaver Selene Arkwright: “You continue to surprise us, Naobito Yagami. Reading the Mistress’s script is… unprecedented. But that does not answer my original question.”
She leaned forward, the geometric sigil on her shoulder glowing brighter.
High Weaver Selene Arkwright: “Why should we not seal you and the Dark Maiden where you stand?”
All twelve Weavers waited once more, the atmosphere electric with unease and fascination.
The Loom Sanctum was thick with unease after the revelation about the geometric script.
Naobito stood calm in the center, then spoke with quiet, unshakable confidence.
Naobito Yagami: “Niro Hart. You never sealed him. I met him tonight. I know.”
A visible shiver ran through several Weavers.
Viktor Drago’s chains rattled violently. Isabella Cortez’s flames dimmed. Even the usually serene Mei Ling Zhao frowned deeply. Some looked away. Master Kael Voss’s colorless eyes narrowed. High Weaver Selene’s expression tightened with clear displeasure.
While they reacted, Naobito focused inward — and for the first time, he felt the connection flip. He could read Yurei’s thoughts just as clearly as she read his. But even she couldn’t decipher the deeper geometric language flowing through his mind. It was hidden from both of them, yet somehow accessible to him.
Yurei (internally, surprised): ‘…I can’t read it either. What are you becoming, my lamb?’
Naobito’s lips curved into a small, knowing smile.
Naobito Yagami: “The puzzle just clicked. Here it is. I got it.”
Freya Haldorsdottir (Weaver of Storms, leaning forward with lightning in her eyes): “Puzzle…? What puzzle?”
Naobito ignored her. He turned his head and looked directly at Lucien Moreau.
Naobito Yagami: “Lucien… am I…?”
Lucien Moreau slowly rose from his throne. For the first time since Naobito had known him, the Weaver of Time wore the widest, most genuine smirk he had ever seen — ancient, delighted, and full of quiet triumph.
Lucien Moreau: “You are.”
The sanctum erupted into murmurs. Chains rattled. Flames sparked. Shadows deepened. Even High Weaver Selene looked momentarily unsettled.
High Weaver Selene Arkwright: “Explain yourself, Time Weaver. What is he?”
Lucien didn’t break eye contact with Naobito as he answered, his voice carrying through the entire chamber.
Lucien Moreau: “He is reading the Mistress’s geometric script naturally. He is forming a true symbiotic bond with a fallen maiden instead of being consumed. And now… he is beginning to understand the deeper weave behind reality itself. The puzzle he solved wasn’t just the one that contained Yurei Himura.”
Lucien’s smirk widened further.
Lucien Moreau: “It was the first lock on something much older.”
Yurei (inside Naobito’s head, equal parts thrilled and wary): “They’re terrified now. Good. Let them tremble, my destined one.”
The Twelve True Weavers stared at the boy in the center with a mixture of awe, fear, and calculation. For the first time, it felt like they were no longer looking at a vessel to be judged.
They were looking at something that might one day judge them.
The Loom Sanctum fell into a stunned hush after Lucien’s words.
Naobito stood at the center, calm and composed, as if the weight of twelve ancient powers meant nothing to him. He looked slowly around the circle, then spoke with quiet certainty.
Naobito Yagami: “The six-winged puzzle I solved… it wasn’t just a prison for Yurei. It was one key. Out of six. And the bigger puzzle… is me.”
A ripple of shock passed through the Weavers.
High Weaver Selene Arkwright leaned forward sharply, her geometric sigil glowing brighter.
Selene: “Explain.”
Naobito continued, his voice steady.
Naobito Yagami: “The Mistress of Threads made it. All of it. The puzzle, the bond, the destiny. I was never just a random boy who found a cursed box. I was designed to become the vessel. The six keys were always meant to lead here — to me becoming something more than either a broken boy or a fallen maiden.”
He tapped two fingers against his own chest.
Naobito Yagami: “I am the puzzle. And that’s why you can’t seal me. That’s why the Mistress wants me alive.”
The sanctum erupted.
Viktor Drago shot to his feet, chains exploding outward with metallic fury. Viktor Drago: “Preposterous! The boy dares claim the Mistress herself crafted him as some grand experiment?!”
Isabella Cortez laughed sharply, flames roaring around her. Isabella: “This just got interesting.”
Amara Khalil, the gentle Weaver of Souls, looked at Naobito with deep sorrow and wonder. Amara: “If this is true… then you are not an anomaly. You are intentional. A living design by the one who stitched reality itself.”
Lucien Moreau remained seated, but his smirk had grown into something almost reverent.
Lucien Moreau: “The Mistress has been silent on this matter for a reason. She wants to see how the puzzle solves itself. Whether the vessel and the Maiden will complete the remaining keys… or break the board entirely.”
High Weaver Selene Arkwright raised her hand, restoring silence. Her expression was grave.
Selene: “If what you say is true, Naobito Yagami, then you are no longer just a threat to the Law of Containment. You are potentially the next evolution of it. Or its destruction.”
She studied him carefully, the geometric script on her shoulder shifting rapidly.
Selene: “The Mistress rarely plays games. When she does… entire eras are rewritten. So I will ask you one final time.”
Her voice echoed through the sanctum like a judge delivering sentence.
Selene: “Do you understand what it means to be one of six keys in a puzzle crafted by the Mistress of Threads herself? And are you willing to bear whatever comes when the remaining keys turn?”
Yurei (inside Naobito’s mind, voice laced with dark excitement and rare respect): “They finally get it. We were never an accident, little lamb. We were made for this.”
All twelve Weavers — and Reno standing silently in the background — waited for his answer.
The boy who had once solved a single cursed puzzle in a lonely apartment now stood at the center of a far greater design.
The Loom Sanctum was frozen in anticipation, every Weaver hanging on the answer that would decide whether Naobito would be sealed or allowed to walk free.
Naobito stood motionless in the center of the circle for a long second. Then, without looking at High Weaver Selene, he turned his head toward the silent figure standing behind Elara’s throne.
Naobito Yagami: “Reno… take me to Niro Hart.”
A stunned silence blanketed the chamber.
Reno’s gray eyes flicked toward Elara, his hand resting on the grip of one six-shooter. His voice was flat, almost bored.
Reno: “My lady?”
Lady Elara Vane sat perfectly still on her obsidian throne, silver rings glinting as she slowly drummed her fingers once. Her violet-gray eyes studied Naobito with a mixture of surprise and dark amusement. A small, elegant smile curved her lips.
Lady Elara Vane: “Do as he says.”
The sanctum erupted.
Viktor Drago slammed his fist down, chains exploding outward. Viktor: “You dare dismiss this council and run to that blood-soaked devil?!”
Isabella Cortez laughed loudly, flames roaring. Isabella: “Oh, I like this boy!”
Master Kael Voss remained deathly still, but his silver mana flared in clear disapproval.
Even High Weaver Selene Arkwright looked momentarily taken aback by the sheer audacity.
Reno gave a single, small nod. He stepped forward from behind Elara’s throne and placed a hand on Naobito’s shoulder. The obsidian coin appeared between his fingers as he prepared the Fold.
Reno: “Hold still.”
Before anyone could protest further, the Range Crest ignited beneath them — nine concentric rings spinning rapidly. Space stitched violently.
In a single, brutal snap, both Naobito and Reno vanished from the Loom Sanctum.
Hart Spire – Private Penthouse Observatory
The Fold deposited them directly onto the sleek black marble balcony overlooking the glittering Tokyo night.
Niro Hart was already waiting.
He stood with one hand in his pocket, white shirt and black vest immaculate, crimson Malevolence Mana flickering lazily around him like a crown of sin. Mireille stood slightly behind him, silver-white hair framing her obsessive expression.
Niro’s demonic smirk deepened the moment he saw them.
Niro Hart: “Well, well… the destined vessel comes to me so soon. And with one of Elara’s attack dogs as an escort, no less.” He chuckled softly. “I’m flattered.”
Reno released Naobito’s shoulder and stepped back, silent as ever.
Naobito met Niro’s crimson gaze without flinching, violet energy flickering behind his own eyes.
Naobito Yagami: “You said you were bored. I figured I’d give you something interesting to watch.”
Niro Hart laughed — low, elegant, and genuinely entertained.
Niro Hart: “Oh, Naobito… I think you and I are going to get along splendidly.”
Behind him, Mireille’s yandere stare burned with dangerous jealousy, but she remained perfectly still.
The game had officially begun.
The end, for now.
===== 2_0 The 'Hart' that shall beat.docx (36588 chars) =====
The 'Hart' that shall beat
Origin of Niro Hart – Age 8
The rain fell like it was mourning someone important.
The Hart family cemetery was crowded with black umbrellas and colder hearts. Rows of polished gravestones stretched under the gray sky. Today they buried Grandfather Elias Hart — the ruthless founder who had built the empire from nothing.
Eight-year-old Niro Hart stood at the very back of the family line, small and easily overlooked. His black suit was slightly too big. His hands were clasped tightly in front of him so no one would see them shaking.
His father, Victor Hart, stepped up to the podium. Tall, imposing, voice like polished steel.
Victor Hart: “My father taught us one thing above all else: weakness is a disease. The Hart name does not tolerate it. We cut it out. We burn it away. Strength is the only currency that matters in this world.”
The words landed on young Niro like cold raindrops.
His siblings — twelve of them, all older, all taller, all favored — whispered behind their hands.
Oldest sister Seraphine (quietly, cruelly): “Look at the little mistake shaking. Pathetic.”
Second brother (snickering): “Grandfather always said the thirteenth was a waste of blood.”
Niro’s mother stood beside Victor, elegant and silent. Her eyes never once drifted toward her youngest son. She had learned long ago that acknowledging him only brought more pain.
Inside Niro’s small chest, something dark and sharp began to twist.
‘Weakness is a disease…’
The thought repeated in his young mind, over and over, like a curse taking root.
‘They all think I’m the weakness. The mistake. The thirteenth child who should never have been born.’
His tiny hands clenched harder. A faint red spark — the very first flicker of Malevolence Mana — ignited deep in his soul, invisible to everyone.
Victor Hart (continuing, voice booming): “We honor Grandfather by continuing his legacy. Only the strong survive in this family. Only the strong inherit.”
The words burned into eight-year-old Niro Hart like a brand.
He looked up at his father. At his silent mother. At his twelve siblings who had spent his entire short life reminding him he was nothing.
And for the first time, the boy who would one day slaughter them all smiled — small, cold, and already broken in the most dangerous way.
Niro Hart (inner thought, soft and venomous): ‘If weakness is a disease… then I will become the cure. I will cut every single one of you out. One day.’
The rain kept falling.
No one noticed the tiny spark of red in the youngest Hart’s eyes.
But the future had already begun to bleed.
The rain had eased into a miserable drizzle by the time the funeral procession ended.
The black limousine waited like a sleek predator at the edge of the cemetery road. The Hart family filed in one by one — tall, elegant, and cruel. Niro, small and silent at eight years old, was the last to climb inside. He sat in the far corner, knees drawn up, staring at his wet shoes.
The door clicked shut. The engine purred to life.
Inside the dim, leather-scented cabin, the atmosphere was colder than the grave they had just left. Victor Hart sat at the head like a king, Mother beside him, staring out the window with empty eyes. The twelve older siblings filled the rest of the seats, whispering and laughing softly among themselves.
Only one brother slid into the seat next to Niro.
Thaddeus Hart — seventeen, tall and broad-shouldered, with the same dark hair as the rest but kinder eyes that hadn’t yet been fully poisoned by the family name. Everyone called him Thad. He was the only one who ever looked at Niro like he was a person instead of a mistake.
Thad leaned in close, voice low enough that only Niro could hear over the rain tapping on the roof.
Thad (whispering, gentle but urgent): “Hey, little brother… you okay? I know today was rough. Grandfather was hard on everyone, but especially on you. Just… stay small for now. Keep your head down. I’ll talk to Father later. Maybe I can get you out of some of the lessons.”
Niro didn’t look up. His small hands were still clenched in his lap, the faint red spark deep in his chest burning hotter.
Niro (inner thought, cold and quiet): ‘Stay small… Keep my head down… They all say the same thing. Even you, Thad. But staying small is what got me here. Weakness is a disease.’
Thad ruffled Niro’s wet hair gently, trying to offer comfort the only way he knew how.
Thad: “You’re not alone, okay? I’ve got your back. We’ll get through this family together.”
The rest of the siblings snickered from the other side of the limo. Seraphine’s voice carried over mockingly.
Seraphine: “Look at Thad playing hero for the mistake again. How noble.”
Thad shot her a warning glare but said nothing more. He stayed close to Niro for the rest of the ride, a silent shield in a car full of knives.
Niro stared straight ahead, the red spark in his soul growing brighter with every cruel whisper, every pitying look, every empty promise.
Even the only brother who cared… would one day learn what happened to those who stood too close to a disease that had decided to become the cure.

The "Lessons" – Age 8 to 12
The Hart family did not believe in gentle upbringing.
After Grandfather’s funeral, the “lessons” began in earnest.
They happened in the private training hall beneath the manor — a cold, marble chamber lined with mirrors so the child could watch his own humiliation from every angle. Victor Hart oversaw them personally, Mother watching silently from the side like a ghost.
Four of the older siblings were chosen as instructors. The ones who enjoyed it most.
Thaddeus “Thad” Hart (17 → 21) The only one who still pretended it was for Niro’s own good.
Thad stood in the center of the hall with a wooden training sword, demonstrating the proper stance for the third time.
Thad: “Again, Niro. Shoulders back. Grip firm. You’re too small — you have to be faster to make up for it. Come on, little brother. I know you can do this.”
Niro, eight years old and already bruised, lunged forward with the practice blade. Thad parried effortlessly and tapped him on the ribs — hard enough to hurt, gentle enough not to break anything.
Thad (quietly, while the others weren’t listening): “I’m sorry. Father says it builds character. Just… endure it. I’ll talk to him tonight.”
But he never did.
Edward Hart (19 → 23) The sadist with a smile.
Edward preferred psychological lessons. He would lock Niro in a small, dark storage room for hours with nothing but a single candle.
Edward (through the door, cheerful): “Think about how weak you are, little mistake. Think about how easy it would be for any of us to snuff you out. When the candle dies, you can come out… if you can find the door in the dark.”
Niro learned to stop crying. He learned to hate the dark. He learned to smile while planning.
Nigel Hart (18 → 22) The brute.
Physical punishment was Nigel’s specialty. He would make Niro run laps around the estate until he collapsed, then drag him up by the collar and make him continue.
Nigel (laughing as Niro fell again): “Pathetic. Even the servants last longer than you. Maybe we should just throw you out with the trash like Father wanted.”
Every bruise was a lesson. Every broken bone a reminder.
Seraphine Hart (21 → 25) The cruelest of them all.
Seraphine’s lessons were elegant and personal. She would sit Niro down in front of the family portrait and make him recite why he was worthless.
Seraphine (smiling sweetly, stroking his hair like a doll): “Say it, little brother. ‘I am the thirteenth mistake. I will never inherit. I exist only because Mother failed to prevent me.’ Good boy. One day I’ll be CEO… and you’ll be whatever I decide you deserve to be.”
She was the one who first told him, at age ten, that she was the designated heir.
That night, the red spark in Niro’s chest burned brighter than ever.
By the time Niro turned twelve, the “lessons” had done their work.
He no longer cried. He no longer begged. He no longer hoped Thad would save him.
He simply watched.
