And in the quiet of his room, the boy who would one day kill them all began to plan exactly how each lesson would be repaid — with interest.
Thad still tried, sometimes. Edward still laughed. Nigel still beat him. Seraphine still smiled while sharpening the knife.
None of them realized the mistake they were making.
The thirteenth child was learning.
And one day, the teacher would become the lesson.
Mother's Choice — Age 10, The Portrait Room
Her name was Elena Hart before she learned not to answer to it.
She stood behind the one-way glass of the training hall, watching Seraphine make ten-year-old Niro recite in front of the family portrait.
Seraphine (muffled through glass, sweet): “Say it, little brother. 'I am the thirteenth mistake.'”
Niro (small, bleeding lip): “I am the thirteenth mistake.”
A maid beside Elena whispered.
Maid: “Madam... shall I bring ice for Young Master Niro after?”
Elena didn't blink.
Elena: “No. If Victor sees you favor him, he'll add an extra lesson to correct it. You know that.”
Maid: “Yes, madam. Sorry.”
Elena watched her youngest son's hands clench. She remembered holding those hands when he was three, before Victor told her: acknowledge him and you paint a target on both of you.
She had chosen survival. Thirteen children, one husband who measured love in obedience, and she had chosen to go silent to stay alive.
Victor entered beside her, smelling of rain.
Victor: “Good. Seraphine is teaching him his place. You look pale, Elena.”
Elena: “I'm tired.”
Victor: “Then rest. Don't go to him tonight. Comfort makes weak boys weaker. Let Thad play the kind brother if he must - Thad can afford one flaw. You cannot.”
He left.
Elena stayed until the lesson ended. Niro walked past the glass without knowing she was there. His eyes were already red at the edges - not crying. Burning.
Elena pressed her fingers to the glass for one second after he was gone.
Elena (whisper, to no one): “Forgive me. I thought if I didn't look, they'd forget you. I didn't know they'd try to erase you.”
She wiped her eyes, fixed her posture, and went to dinner where she would not look at him again.
It was the choice that would let her live to kneel in blood six years later.
The Override Zone – Niro Hart, Age 14
The air tore open like silk ripped by a careless hand.
One moment Niro was in the Hart family manor, locked in the punishment room again after failing another “lesson” — bruised ribs, split lip, and the taste of blood in his mouth. The next, reality folded violently around him and spat him out into endless violet-black nothing.
He landed hard on a floating obsidian island, skidding across the smooth surface before catching himself. Fourteen years old, thin, angry, and already radiating the first dangerous flickers of deep red Malevolence Mana.
Lucien Moreau stood a short distance away, hands in his coat pockets, amber eyes blinking once in genuine surprise.
Lucien Moreau: “That’s odd.”
Niro pushed himself up, crimson eyes narrowing at the strange man who looked like he had stepped out of time itself. The Zone’s weightless dark pressed against him, but instead of fear, only cold calculation filled the boy’s mind.
Niro Hart (voice already carrying the arrogance of someone who had survived too much): “Where the hell am I? Did one of my siblings finally manage to kill me, or is this another one of their sick games?”
Lucien tilted his head, studying the boy with faint fascination. Dozens of his copies appeared at the edges of the island, all watching silently.
Lucien Moreau: “Neither. You somehow tore a hole into the Override Zone — a dimension outside of time. No one has ever entered it accidentally before. Especially not a fourteen-year-old radiating this much raw Malevolence Mana.”
Niro wiped blood from his lip, standing straighter. The red energy around him flared brighter, unstable but hungry.
Niro Hart: “Good. Then it’s mine now.”
Lucien’s smirk was small and knowing.
Lucien Moreau: “Bold claim for a child who just escaped a punishment room. The Zone doesn’t belong to anyone… but it does respond to those with enough will to break it open. Tell me, boy — what exactly were you thinking about when you ripped your way in here?”
Niro’s crimson eyes darkened with pure, unfiltered hatred.
Niro Hart: “I was thinking about how I’m going to kill every single one of them. Slowly. My father. My mother who watched. My twelve perfect siblings who spent every day reminding me I was the mistake. Especially Seraphine… the golden child who gets everything while I get bruises and locked rooms.”
The red Mana around him surged, forming faint, violent patterns in the air.
Niro Hart: “If this place is outside of time… then maybe I can practice. Maybe I can become strong enough here that when I go back, none of them will ever touch me again.”
Lucien watched the boy with quiet interest, dozens of his copies nodding in unison.
Lucien Moreau: “Interesting. Most who stumble into the Zone break under the weight of it. You… you see it as a weapon.”
He stepped closer, offering a hand that Niro didn’t take.
Lucien Moreau: “Very well, little Hart. The Zone is yours to use… for now. Train. Break. Become whatever monster you need to be. But remember — time may not move here, but consequences always find their way back.”
Niro’s smirk was small, cold, and already full of demonic promise.
Niro Hart: “Good. I’m counting on it.”
The violet-black void around them seemed to pulse in response to the boy’s growing Malevolence.
The Override Zone had welcomed its newest guest.
How To Hold Hate — Override Zone Training, Age 14
The violet-black void had no floor, but the obsidian island held.
Lucien Moreau clapped once. Dozens of his copies clapped half a beat late.
Lucien: “Again. Don't throw it. Hold it.”
Niro, fourteen, bruised from the punishment room still fresh on his skin back on Earth, glared. Red Malevolence coiled around his fist, wild, spitting.
Niro: “Holding it does nothing. I want to break things.”
Lucien: “You already break things. That's why you ripped a hole here by accident. I'm teaching you to not die doing it.”
One Lucien stepped forward, held out his palm. A perfect sphere of amber time-mana hovered there, silent.
Lucien: “Your mana is Malevolence. Raw will to dominate. Right now yours is a scream. A scream wastes air. Compress it.”
Niro snarled and squeezed. The red flare shuddered, cracked, exploded outward and threw him on his back.
Nose bleeding. Hands shaking.
Lucien crouched, all copies crouching.
Lucien (softer): “What were you thinking about when you came here, little Hart?”
Niro wiped blood.
Niro: “Seraphine smiling while she tells me I'm nothing. Edward laughing through the door. Nigel's boot. My mother looking away.”
Lucien: “Good. Don't let it go. Fold it smaller. Hate is heavy - make it dense.”
Niro stood. Breathed. Thought of Thad saying 'stay small' and hating that too.
He pressed both hands together. The red scream shrank - fist-sized, then coin-sized, then marble-sized, humming like a trapped hornet.
It held for three seconds. Then it popped, singeing his eyebrows.
Niro laughed - surprised, real, fourteen for one second.
Niro: “Did you see that? Three seconds.”
Lucien smiled, genuine.
Lucien: “Three seconds today. Three minutes next month. One day you'll hold a sun in your palm and decide who gets to stand in its light. That's when you'll go back and cure your disease.”
Dozens of Luciens nodded.
In the void outside time, the boy trained until his hands stopped shaking. Not because the pain stopped. Because he learned to carry it.
And the future — the one where a fourteen-year-old would one day slaughter his entire family and build an empire on their graves — had just taken its first real step.
Thad's Last Night — Age 14, Two Weeks Before The Mountain Road
The west study smelled of cigar smoke and old money. Victor Hart didn't look up from his desk.
Thad stood at attention, seventeen going on tired.
Thad: “Stop the lessons for Niro. He's fourteen, Father. Edward locked him in the box for six hours yesterday. Nigel cracked two of his ribs last week.”
Victor Hart: “And he lived. That's the point.”
Thad: “The point is you're making him hate us. All of us.”
Victor finally looked up.
Victor Hart: “He is the thirteenth. He was born hateable. Seraphine understands succession. Edward understands discipline. You - you understand sentiment. That's why you'll never lead.”
Thad's hands shook, but he didn't leave.
Thad: “Then let me take him away. Boarding school. Off-world academy. Anywhere not here. I'll sign off my share of the inheritance if you want. Just let him live somewhere he isn't a target.”
Victor laughed once, short.
Victor Hart: “You'd throw away Hart blood for a mistake? Your grandfather would have shot you for that sentence.”
Seraphine was in the doorway, listening. She smiled sweetly.
Seraphine: “Oh, Thaddy. Still playing hero for the little mistake? Father's starting to listen, you know. People might think you care more about Niro than about the family.”
Thad turned on her.
Thad: “I care about not raising a monster in our basement, Sera.”
Seraphine: “Careful. Monsters hear things.”
That night Thad went to Niro's room. The boy was awake, icing his ribs in the dark, red flicker faint under his skin that Thad couldn't see.
Thad ruffled his wet hair, forced a grin.
Thad (quiet): “Hey, little brother. I talked to him. Didn't work yet. But I'm going to get you out. Mountain road meeting next week with the Kael suppliers - if I close it, he'll owe me a favor. I'll use it on you. Promise.”
Niro didn't say thank you. He just stared.
Niro (flat): “Don't promise things this family won't let you keep.”
Thad left. The hallway was empty except Seraphine's shadow at the far end, phone to her ear.

Niro Hart – Age 14 (Two Years Before the Massacre)
The manor hallway was quiet except for the distant sound of servants whispering.
Niro stood frozen in the corridor, still wearing the bruises from that morning’s “lesson.” Edward had been particularly creative today.
One of the older maids — the only servant who had ever shown him a sliver of pity — stopped beside him, voice low and nervous.
Maid: “Young Master… I’m sorry. Master Thaddeus… he’s dead. Car accident on the mountain road last night. They say it was sudden.”
Niro didn’t move.
His small hands slowly clenched at his sides. The faint red spark of Malevolence Mana that had been growing inside him for years flared brighter — hotter — sharper.
Niro Hart (inner voice, cold and empty): ‘Thad… the only one who ever pretended to care. The only one who said he had my back. Dead. Just like that.’
A slow, terrifyingly calm smile spread across the fourteen-year-old boy’s face.
He looked up at the maid, crimson eyes already losing the last traces of childhood.
Niro Hart (softly, almost gently): “Thank you for telling me.”
The maid hurried away, unnerved by the smile that didn’t belong on a child’s face.
Alone in the hallway, Niro leaned against the wall and let the red energy coil around his fingers like living blood.
Niro Hart (inner thought, darker now): ‘Even the one who was kind to me couldn’t survive this family. If Thad couldn’t protect himself… then none of them deserve to live.’
The spark inside him no longer flickered.
It burned.
And two years later, on his sixteenth birthday, the Hart family would finally understand exactly what they had created.
The Night Before His 16th Birthday
The Hart manor was never truly quiet.
Niro Hart, fourteen years old and already far too sharp for his age, moved like a shadow through the west wing corridors. Sleep had abandoned him again — another night of bruises from the day’s “lessons,” another night of the red spark in his chest burning hotter.
He stopped outside the half-open door of Seraphine’s private study.
Inside, low voices drifted into the hallway.
Seraphine Hart (cold, amused): “Thad was becoming a problem. Always defending the little mistake. Talking about ‘protecting’ him. Father was starting to listen.”
Edward Hart (laughing softly): “So we solved it. Mountain road at night. A little push on the wheel, a little cut in the brake line. Clean. Everyone thinks it was an accident.”
Nigel Hart (grunting in approval): “Good riddance. Thad was soft. Always trying to play hero for that worthless thirteenth child. Now the line of succession is cleaner. Seraphine takes over when Father steps down. No more annoying ‘brotherly love’ getting in the way.”
Seraphine’s voice dripped with satisfaction.
Seraphine: “Exactly. The mistake stays a mistake. And with Thad gone, no one will protect him anymore. By the time he turns sixteen, we’ll have him properly broken. Or gone.”
Niro stood perfectly still in the dark hallway.
The red spark inside his chest didn’t just flare — it ignited into a roaring flame.
Niro Hart (inner voice, ice-cold and final): ‘They killed the only one who ever tried. For power. For the company. For the precious Hart name.’
A slow, terrifying smile spread across the boy’s face in the darkness.
He turned and walked back to his room without a sound.
That night, the last fragile thread of mercy in Niro Hart snapped completely.
Tomorrow was his sixteenth birthday.
And the Hart family had just signed their own death warrants.
The grand dining hall of the Hart manor was lit by crystal chandeliers and the cold glow of wealth.
The long mahogany table was set for thirteen. Victor Hart sat at the head like a king on his throne, Mother beside him, silent and elegant as always. The eleven remaining siblings filled the rest of the seats, laughing and whispering among themselves.
It was the night before Niro’s sixteenth birthday.
Victor raised his glass, voice booming with pride.
Victor Hart: “Tonight, we celebrate the future. Seraphine, my eldest and most capable child, will officially be named my successor as CEO of Hart Industries. The board has already approved it. She will lead us into a new era of strength and dominance. The Hart name will never again be weakened by—”
A scream tore through the hallway outside the dining room.
Edward Hart stumbled through the grand double doors, blood pouring from a massive shotgun wound to the side of his head. His face was half-destroyed, one eye missing, jaw shattered. He collapsed onto the polished floor in a broken, bloody heap, twitching once before going still.
The entire family froze.
Niro Hart stepped calmly into the doorway behind him, the smoking shotgun held loosely in one hand. At sixteen, he was no longer the small, trembling boy they had tormented for years. He was taller now, lean and sharp, with cold crimson eyes that glowed with rising Malevolence Mana. Red energy coiled around him like living blood, thick and hungry.
He tossed the shotgun aside with a clatter. It skidded across the marble and stopped at his father’s feet.
Victor Hart shot to his feet, face twisting with rage and disbelief.
Victor: “What the hell have you done, you worthless little—”
Niro didn’t let him finish.
He raised his hand. Malevolence Mana surged forward in a violent red wave. It slammed into Victor like a freight train, crushing his ribs and pinning him against the wall. The head of the Hart family gasped, blood spraying from his mouth as bones snapped.
Niro Hart (voice calm, almost gentle): “You always said weakness is a disease, Father. I finally agree. I’m curing it tonight.”
The rest of the siblings erupted — chairs scraping, screams, some trying to run. Nigel charged at him with a roar, fists raised.
Niro flicked his wrist. Red energy compressed into a dense, crackling ball in his palm. He hurled it at Nigel with casual precision. The ball exploded against the older brother’s chest, blowing a hole straight through him and taking out half the dining table behind him in a shower of wood and blood.
Seraphine screamed and tried to flee toward the side door, her perfect face twisted in terror for the first time in her life.
Seraphine: “You freak! You insane little mistake—!”
Niro raised his hand again. This time he compressed the Malevolence Mana into a much smaller, denser orb — glowing like a tiny red sun. He flicked it toward her with almost lazy grace.
The orb shot across the room like a bullet. It hit Seraphine in the back and detonated with catastrophic force.
The entire corridor behind her vanished in a roaring explosion of red energy. Walls crumbled. Chandeliers shattered. Seraphine’s scream was cut short as her body was torn apart in the blast, leaving nothing but a smoking crater and scattered pieces.
The remaining siblings tried to fight, to run, to beg.
Niro moved through them like a reaper.
One by one they fell — some to raw blasts of Malevolence, some to precisely compressed orbs that punched through bodies like cannon fire, some to the sheer crushing pressure of red energy that wrapped around them and squeezed until bones and organs gave way.
The dining hall became a slaughterhouse.
Blood painted the walls. The long table was reduced to splinters. The air reeked of ozone, iron, and charred flesh.
Only one person remained alive.
Mother sat on the floor where she had fallen from her chair, back pressed against the wall, eyes wide with pure terror. She was shaking violently, unable to even scream anymore.
Niro walked slowly through the carnage until he stood directly in front of her. His white shirt and vest were soaked red, but his face was calm — almost peaceful.
He knelt down so they were eye level.
Niro Hart (softly, almost tenderly): “You watched, Mother. Every lesson. Every bruise. Every time they called me the mistake. You never said a word. So I’ll let you live with it.”
He stood up, turning his back on her without fear.
Niro Hart: “Watch the empire burn from the ashes I leave you. That’s my final gift.”
The boy who had just murdered his entire family except one walked out of the dining hall, red Malevolence Mana trailing behind him like a royal cape.
Behind him, Mother’s broken sobs echoed through the blood-soaked room.
The Hart family was no more.
And Niro Hart, at sixteen years old, had just taken his first true step into the monster he was always meant to become.
The Quiet After Blood — Age 16, 3 AM After The Massacre
The hallway outside the dining hall was quiet except for Mother sobbing behind him.
Niro walked barefoot - he'd lost one shoe somewhere between Nigel and Seraphine - leaving red footprints on marble his ancestors had imported from Italy.
He stopped at Thad's old room. Still kept as a shrine. Trophies. Soccer medal. Photo of seventeen-year-old Thad holding eight-year-old Niro on his shoulders, both squinting in sun.
Niro stared a long time.
Niro (quiet, to photo): “You said you'd talk to him tonight. You never did. They made sure you never could.”
Red mana curled off his shoulders, tired now, flickering.
Niro: “I didn't save a seat for you at the table tonight, Thad. You'd have tried to stop me. And I'd have had to...”
He didn't finish. He closed the door gently.
In his own room - tiny, punishment-room-adjacent, no trophies - he changed out of the blood-soaked white shirt and black vest into a clean white shirt and black vest. Same uniform. New man.
He looked in the mirror. Sixteen. Crimson eyes. No tears.
Niro (to mirror): “Weakness is a disease. I cut it out.”
His hands still shook, just like at eight at the funeral. Only now no one was left to see it.
He went to his father's study to steal an empire.
What Happens Next – The Immediate Aftermath
The Hart manor was silent except for the soft, broken sobs of the only survivor.
Niro Hart walked out of the blood-soaked dining hall without looking back. His white shirt and black vest were drenched crimson, but he moved with the same graceful, demonic elegance he would carry for the rest of his life. The red Malevolence Mana around him pulsed like a living heartbeat — satisfied, but far from sated.
He didn’t run. He didn’t panic.
He simply went to his father’s study, sat down in the large leather chair behind the desk, and opened the family safe with the code he had memorized years ago.
Inside were documents, keys, and the master passwords to every Hart Industries account and system.
Niro spent the next two hours calmly transferring ownership, erasing traces, and locking down the empire under his own name. By sunrise, the legal and financial work was done. The Hart family no longer existed on paper.
Only then did he deal with the “obvious.”
He walked back to the dining hall. His mother was still on the floor, curled up among the corpses of her children and husband, shaking uncontrollably.
Niro knelt in front of her again. His voice was gentle, almost kind.
Niro Hart: “You will tell the police it was a home invasion. Masked men. You barely survived. You saw nothing. If you ever speak the truth… I will come back and make what happened tonight feel like mercy.”
He pressed a single kiss to her forehead — cold, mocking, and final.
Niro Hart: “Live with it, Mother. That’s your punishment.”
She never spoke a word against him. The official story was accepted: a brutal home invasion that wiped out the entire Hart family except the grieving widow and the youngest son who had been “miraculously” away at a friend’s house that night.
Niro inherited everything at sixteen.
No one questioned it. No one dared.
What Mother Told Police — Immediate Aftermath
Two detectives. Hospital blanket. Beeping.
Detective: “Mrs. Hart, take your time. How many men?”
Elena Hart stared at her bandaged hands. Behind her eyes: Niro kneeling eye-level, tender. “You watched, Mother.”
Elena (hoarse): “Masked. Four... five men. They... they wanted the safe. My husband fought. My children...”
She cried on cue. She'd practiced silence for sixteen years. Lying was easy now.
Detective: “And Niro? Your youngest? Where was he?”
Elena: “At a friend's. Thank God. Thank God one was spared.”
Outside the hospital room, sixteen-year-old Niro in a borrowed school uniform ate vending-machine crackers, eyes empty, the tragic sole survivor for the cameras.
Their eyes met through the glass for one second.
He smiled that old, demonic, small smile.
She looked away first. As always.
That was her punishment. To live, to know, and to never once look directly at what she helped create.

One Last Origin Scene – The Secretary
Age 19 – Hart Industries Headquarters, Private Executive Floor
The boardroom was empty except for Niro Hart.
He sat at the head of the long table, nineteen years old, already the undisputed owner of everything the Hart name touched. The massacre was three years behind him. The world knew him as the tragic sole survivor who had risen from the ashes. Only a few people knew the truth — and those people were either dead or too terrified to speak.
A soft knock sounded at the door.
Niro Hart: “Enter.”
The door opened. A young woman stepped inside — twenty-one years old, silver-white hair tied in a neat ponytail, sharp features, and eyes that burned with something dangerous the moment they landed on him.
She was dressed in a crisp black secretary outfit, tablet in hand, posture perfect.
Mireille Vale: “Mr. Hart. I’m here for the executive assistant position. My name is Mireille Vale.”
Niro leaned back in his chair, studying her with lazy crimson eyes.
Niro Hart: “Your résumé says you graduated top of your class in corporate law and strategic management. Impressive. But I don’t hire based on paper. Why should I let you work for me?”
Mireille didn’t flinch. She stepped closer, placing the tablet on the table, and looked him straight in the eyes.
Mireille: “Because I’ve been watching you for two years, Mr. Hart. Not because of your money or your power — though both are magnificent. I watched how you took a ruined family empire and turned it into something unstoppable. How you smile while the world calls you a miracle survivor, knowing you’re the one who painted the walls red. How you carry yourself like the world already belongs to you… and it just hasn’t realized it yet.”
She smiled — small, obsessive, and completely genuine.
Mireille: “I like your personality, Mr. Hart. Your unmatched ego. Your cold elegance. The way you look at people like they’re already dead and just don’t know it. I want to be the one who stands closest to that. I want to watch you conquer everything. And if you ever need someone to get their hands dirty for you… I’ll do it with a smile.”
Niro stared at her for a long moment.
Then he laughed — low, dark, and genuinely entertained.
Niro Hart: “You’re insane.”
Mireille (softly, almost lovingly): “Only for you.”
He leaned forward, resting his chin on his interlaced fingers, crimson eyes gleaming.
Niro Hart: “You’re hired. Try not to bore me, Mireille.”
She bowed deeply, the obsessive smile never leaving her face.
Mireille: “I won’t, Master. Ever.”
From that day on, Mireille Vale became his shadow — his secretary, his blade, his most devoted yandere. She killed for him without hesitation. She managed his empire with terrifying efficiency. And she never asked for anything in return except the privilege of staying close to the man whose unmatched ego and cold beauty had stolen her completely.
Niro Hart had found the perfect tool.
And Mireille had found her god.
First Boardroom Kill — Age 19
Hart Industries, 47th floor. Rain against glass.
Director Cho, sixty, loyal to Victor Hart for twenty years, slammed his folder.
Cho: “You are sixteen - sorry, nineteen - playing CEO with your father's company. The Malevolence engine division is dangerous, illegal in six realms. I move to freeze it and call a no-confidence vote.”
Six other directors shifted. Niro at the head of the table didn't move. Mireille stood behind him, tablet to chest, silver-white ponytail perfect.
Niro: “Cho. You billed three mistresses to the company last quarter. You sold engine schematics to the Ivory Board's shell corp. You called my mother last week and told her I was unstable. Did I miss anything?”
Cho went white.
Cho: “You - you hacked -”
Niro: “I own the servers you used to do it.”
Niro nodded once at Mireille.
Mireille stepped forward, smiled like a secretary, broke Cho's wrist in two places before security could blink, then pressed a red-hot Malevolence needle - Niro's compressed orb, thimble-sized, given to her that morning - to his neck.
Mireille (sweet): “Mr. Hart doesn't like no-confidence votes during rain. He finds them... noisy.”
Cho whimpered. The other directors froze.
Niro stood, straightened his vest, walked around the table, knelt by Cho.
Niro (gentle): “You served my father. My father is dead. You have two options. Retire tonight with a pension and a story about a home invasion survivor who was merciful. Or be the example that teaches this table how succession works now.”
Cho: “R-retire. Please. Retire.”
Niro: “Good choice.”
He took the needle from Mireille, dissolved it with a flick. Red mist curled away.
Niro to the room: “Anyone else want to vote?”
Silence.
Mireille's eyes shone with open worship as she opened the door for him. In the elevator down, alone with her god, she whispered.
Mireille: “That was perfect, Master. The way you let him choose. So elegant.”
Niro checked his cuff for blood. There was none. That disappointed him a little.
Niro: “He chose boring. Next one won't. Try not to look so excited, Mireille. People will think we're dating.”
Mireille (soft, loving): “Only for you.”
Doors closed. Empire secured.
The Years That Followed (19 to 27)
He didn’t kill anyone else immediately. He didn’t need to.
The empire was already his. The servants who knew too much were paid off or quietly disappeared over the next few months. The board members who might have resisted were bought, blackmailed, or broken.
By eighteen, Niro Hart was the undisputed ruler of Hart Industries. He expanded aggressively — buying competitors, developing mana-infused technology, and turning the company into a global powerhouse. His mobility division alone (autonomous vehicles powered by controlled Malevolence Mana) became legendary.
He killed when necessary — rivals, threats, anyone who looked at him the wrong way — but never with the same raw fury as the night of the massacre. Those deaths were clean, elegant, and efficient. He had learned to enjoy the art of it.
At twenty-seven, he was a quintillionaire who could buy countries and burn them for fun.
And he was still bored.
Until the day he heard rumors of a boy in Tokyo who had bonded with an ancient fallen maiden and was leaking violet Corruption Mana in the tens of thousands of Seams.
Niro Hart smiled that old, demonic smile.
Niro Hart: “Finally… something interesting.”
===== 3_0Override Zone the blood tournament.docx (170648 chars) =====
Override Zone: "The Blood Tournament"
Two Years Later
The underground arena known only as The Crimson Veil was a cathedral of violence hidden beneath the bones of three continents.
It was not on any map. It was not reachable by normal means. Only those with enough mana, connections, or sheer audacity could step through the Folded gates and enter the colossal stadium carved from black stone and living shadow.
Tonight, the stands were packed with thousands — mages, vessels, pawns, demons, ancient spirits, and beings from realms far beyond Earth. The air hummed with every color of mana imaginable: violet corruption, red malevolence, silver neutralization, green nature, and flashes of pure aqua from the rare divine observers.
A massive holographic screen hovered above the central fighting pit, broadcasting across the hidden mana society’s underground television network — VeilNet.
The anchor, a sharp-dressed incubus with glowing red eyes, grinned into the camera with predatory excitement.
VeilNet Anchor: “Ladies, gentlemen, monsters, and everything in between — welcome to the greatest spectacle in the known universe! Hosted by the Elite Councils of the Nine Realms, not by any single Weaver or Mistress… we present to you…”
The arena lights exploded into crimson and violet.
VeilNet Anchor (voice rising to a roar): “THE BLOOD TOURNAMENT!”
The crowd erupted. Thousands of mana signatures flared in excitement.
In the opulent VIP section suspended high above the arena, the 12 True Weavers sat in a grand arc of thrones. Elara Vane crossed her legs elegantly. Lucien Moreau watched with calm amusement. Master Kael Voss remained stoic. Asha Nkosi, the Weaver of Beasts, grinned wildly, already eager for violence.
Reno (now 20, still quiet and deadly) stood guard behind Elara, dressed in a sharper black tactical suit. Saya Kurogane sat beside her master, silver mana flickering nervously around her hands. Asha Nkosi had personally insisted on fighting for the Weavers — her primal energy barely contained.
Scattered among the Weavers’ entourage were other pawns, allies, and lesser Weavers who had not yet been named.
In a private luxury box on the opposite side of the arena, away from the Weavers, sat Niro Hart (29). He lounged in a blood-red throne-like chair, white shirt and black vest immaculate, a glass of crimson liquid in his hand. Mireille Vale knelt beside him, silver-white hair cascading down, her yandere gaze fixed on him with obsessive adoration.
Niro Hart (smirking as he watched the screen): “A Blood Tournament hosted by the Councils… how quaint. Let’s see if any of these insects can entertain me tonight.”
Mireille leaned closer, voice dripping with devotion. Mireille: “Shall I eliminate anyone who dares look at you too long, Master?”
Niro chuckled and stroked her hair once, like petting a dangerous animal.
Niro Hart: “Not yet, my dear. I’m more interested in seeing if a certain violet-eyed boy shows up…”
Back in the VIP section, High Weaver Selene Arkwright frowned at the holographic participant list floating before them.
On it, two names glowed in violet:
Naobito Yagami & Yurei Himura — Listed as one entry. Status: Not fighting for the Weavers. Current location: Unknown (reported in deep space).
Reno glanced at Elara.
Reno: “They’re not here.”
Elara Vane (smiling thinly): “Of course they aren’t. They’re probably watching from somewhere far more interesting. Let the games begin.”
The arena lights dimmed. The first fighters were announced. The crowd roared as the Blood Tournament officially began.
Two hours until the real chaos started.
And somewhere in the cold void of deep space, Naobito and Yurei floated together, watching the broadcast through a small woven window of violet energy, their bond stronger — and far more dangerous — than ever before.
The Crimson Veil Arena thrummed with raw energy as the holographic screen above the fighting pit flared to life once more. The incubus anchor grinned, his red eyes gleaming as he spread his arms dramatically.
VeilNet Anchor: “Before the first blood is spilled, let us honor our distinguished guests of honor — the powers that shape entire realms!”
The arena lights swept across the VIP sections as massive glowing sigils appeared beside each box.
VeilNet Anchor: “Representing the Twelve True Weavers — the sacred enforcers of the Law of Containment — we have: High Weaver Selene Arkwright, Weaver of Fate! Lady Elara Vane, Weaver of Threads! Lucien Moreau, Weaver of Time! Master Kael Voss, Weaver of Neutrality! Amara Khalil, Weaver of Souls! Viktor Drago, Weaver of Chains! Mei Ling Zhao, Weaver of Silence! Isabella Cortez, Weaver of Flames! Omar al-Sayed, Weaver of Sands! Asha Nkosi, Weaver of Beasts! Dr. Elias Thorn, Weaver of Flesh! Freya Haldorsdottir, Weaver of Storms! And Soren Blackwood, Weaver of Shadows!”
The crowd roared in a mix of awe and fear as each Weaver’s sigil flared.
The anchor’s grin widened as the camera panned to the opposite luxury box.
VeilNet Anchor: “And from the mortal empires, the undisputed ruler of Hart Industries and one of the most feared Malevolence users in existence — Niro Hart and his loyal shadow, Mireille Vale!”
Niro raised his glass lazily toward the camera, his demonic smirk visible even on the giant screen. Mireille leaned possessively against his throne, eyes locked only on him.
The anchor continued, sweeping across other VIP sections filled with beings from across the universe.
VeilNet Anchor: “We also welcome representatives from the Eternal Court of Pure Light (aqua mana radiating from angelic delegates), the Abyssal Dominion (red-skinned demon lords pulsing with Malevolence), the Verdant Conclave (green-natured spirit elders), the Silver Veil Enclave (neutralization masters in flowing white robes), and the Fractured Realms Coalition — outcasts, rogue vessels, and independent powers who answer to no council!”
The crowd’s roar reached a fever pitch as thousands of unique mana signatures flared in the stands — every color and faction represented.
VeilNet Anchor: “This is not just a tournament. This is where the universe settles its debts in blood! The Blood Tournament begins… NOW!”
The arena lights exploded into chaos as the first two fighters were teleported into the pit.
High above, in their VIP box, Niro Hart chuckled softly, swirling his drink.
Niro Hart: “Let the games begin indeed.”
In the Weavers’ section, Elara leaned toward Lucien with a thin smile.
Elara: “He’s watching. They both are.”
Lucien’s eyes twinkled with quiet amusement.
Lucien: “Of course they are. The real show hasn’t even started yet.”
Deep in the cold silence of space, far from the roaring Crimson Veil Arena, a small pocket of violet energy floated like a private theater.
Naobito Yagami and Yurei Himura hovered together in the void, watching the massive holographic broadcast of the Blood Tournament through a woven window of Corruption Mana. The arena’s lights and cheers felt distant, almost unreal.
Yurei’s voice purred softly in his mind, warm and playful.
Yurei Himura: “Who do you think is going first, little lamb?”
Naobito crossed his arms, eyes fixed on the screen as the first fighters were announced — raw, untested vessels and low-rank mages stepping into the pit.
Naobito Yagami: “Asha Nkosi-san said the first few rounds are for the newcomers. But she also said we’ll be treated as a ‘Symbiote-Host’ relationship. Therefore, we’ll start after the singular beings.”
He glanced sideways, as if he could see her ethereal form beside him.
Naobito Yagami: “They’re scared of what we are together. They want to see how the rest of the board moves before they risk putting the real pieces on it.”
Yurei (laughing softly, almost affectionately): “Smart boy. They know we’re not just two fighters. We’re a living puzzle. One key out of six… and the board itself. Let the little ones bleed first. We’ll step in when the real game begins.”
On the broadcast, the first match started with a roar from the crowd. A green-natured mage against a red-tinged Malevolence user. The arena shook with the opening clash.
Naobito’s expression remained calm, but his fingers twitched faintly, violet energy flickering across his knuckles.
Naobito Yagami: “When our turn comes… we show them exactly what a destined vessel and his Maiden can do.”
Yurei: “That’s my lamb. Watch closely. Learn their weaknesses. And when we finally enter that pit…”
Her voice dropped into a dark, loving whisper.
Yurei: “We’ll paint it violet.”
The two of them continued watching in the quiet of deep space — partners, not possessor and possessed. Waiting for their moment.
The Blood Tournament had only just begun.
Crimson Veil Arena – VIP Observation Lounge (Neutral Faction Section)
High above the roaring crowd, in a luxurious lounge reserved for the neutral councils and independent factions, several powerful figures watched the opening matches with varying degrees of interest.
A tall, ethereal being from the Eternal Court of Pure Light — an angelic emissary in flowing aqua robes — sipped a crystalline drink and spoke with measured calm.
Aetherion, Emissary of Pure Light: “A Blood Tournament hosted by the Nine Realms Councils… bold. The rules are simple: no outside interference, no killing outside the pit, and the winner of each bracket earns a single favor from the collective councils. Yet the real prize is reputation — and the chance to challenge any guest of honor.”
Beside him, a hulking demon lord from the Abyssal Dominion, skin the color of dried blood and eyes burning with Malevolence, laughed deeply.
Lord Varak of the Red Abyss: “Reputation is for the weak. I came to spill blood and test myself against the so-called ‘Symbiote-Host’ pair everyone is whispering about. Naobito Yagami and his Dark Maiden. They say their Corruption Mana rivals even my strongest generals. I want that fight.”
A slender, intellectual-looking figure from the Fractured Realms Coalition — a hooded scholar with multiple glowing eyes and silver-white Neutralization accents — adjusted his spectacles and spoke in a precise, curious tone.
Archivist Veyl: “Fascinating. Most challengers are singular entities. The rules treat ‘Symbiote-Host’ relationships as a single entry, giving them a slight delay in the bracket to observe the field. Smart. It prevents early exploitation. But this Naobito… I have read the sealed reports. A destined vessel who didn’t break. The first in recorded history. I am far more interested in studying him than fighting him.”
The demon lord snorted.
Lord Varak: “Study him after I break him. The Weavers look nervous. That means he’s dangerous. Good.”
A graceful spirit elder from the Verdant Conclave, skin patterned with living green vines, shook her head.
Elder Lirael: “Violence for violence’s sake. The boy carries heavy loneliness. I sense it even from here. Whether he fights for the Weavers or as an independent… I hope the tournament does not break what little light remains in him.”
The Archivist Veyl leaned forward, eyes gleaming with intellectual hunger.
Archivist Veyl: “Regardless of outcome, this tournament will reveal much. The Councils are watching. The Weavers are watching. And Niro Hart is watching. The real question is not who wins… but what new balance — or imbalance — emerges when the final key turns.”
The group fell quiet as the first match reached its brutal conclusion below. The crowd roared.
High above, in their own box, Niro Hart smiled slowly, sensing the same undercurrents.
The Blood Tournament was no longer just entertainment.
It was a stage for the universe to decide what kind of power would define the next era.
The Crimson Veil Arena exploded into cheers as the first official match of the Blood Tournament began.
Two fighters materialized in the central pit: a lean, green-glowing Nature Mana user from the Verdant Conclave — a young woman named Lirael Thornvine, Rank 3 — and a hulking Malevolence user from the Abyssal Dominion, a red-skinned brute named Gorzak the Flayer.
The anchor’s voice boomed across the arena.
VeilNet Anchor: “First bout — Nature versus Malevolence! Begin!”
Gorzak roared and charged, red Malevolence Mana erupting around his fists like burning blood. He swung wildly, each punch carrying enough force to crater the reinforced floor.
Lirael moved like flowing vines — graceful, precise, almost dancing. She dodged the first two strikes, then countered with a sweeping kick infused with sharp green energy. Vines erupted from the ground, momentarily binding Gorzak’s legs.
The Malevolence user laughed and shattered the vines with raw power, lunging again.
They clashed in the center — fist against palm, red against green.
For a split second, it looked even.
Then Lirael’s eyes flashed. A razor-sharp leaf-blade of concentrated Nature Mana formed in her hand. She spun under Gorzak’s guard and slashed upward in one clean, devastating arc.
The blade cut straight through the Malevolence user’s torso.
Gorzak’s roar turned into a wet gurgle. His body split cleanly in half, red mana dissipating as he collapsed in two pieces on the arena floor.
The crowd went wild.
VeilNet Anchor: “First victory to Lirael Thornvine of the Verdant Conclave! Nature prevails in under thirty seconds!”
High above in the VIP box, Naobito watched the replay on the floating screen, arms crossed, violet eyes narrowed with genuine interest.
Naobito Yagami: “She’s lower rank… but that cut was perfect. No wasted movement. Pure efficiency.”
Yurei (voice intrigued in his mind): “Mmm. A little green flower who knows how to prune the weeds. She reminds me of what I could have been… before I became this. Interesting.”
Naobito leaned forward slightly, studying Lirael as she calmly bowed to the crowd and stepped back for the next match.
Naobito Yagami: “I want to see more of her. Lower rank doesn’t mean weak. It means she’s had to be smarter.”
In the Weavers’ section, Asha Nkosi grinned widely, while Elara Vane raised an elegant eyebrow.
The tournament was just getting started.
And Naobito’s attention had already been caught by the quiet, deadly grace of a Nature user who had ended a brute in one clean strike.
The Crimson Veil Arena pulsed like a living heart.
After Lirael Thornvine’s swift, almost surgical victory, the next few bouts unfolded in rapid, brutal succession. A Malevolence brute from the Abyssal Dominion was torn apart by a coordinated team of Nature users. A silver Neutralization enforcer neutralized a reckless green-rank mage in under a minute, turning the pit into a sterile, mana-dead zone. The crowd roared for blood, for technique, for the raw spectacle of power clashing against power.
High above, in their private pocket of deep space, Naobito and Yurei watched through the violet woven window. The faint glow of distant stars framed them like silent witnesses.
Yurei (voice soft, almost affectionate in his mind): “They fight like children throwing stones. So much noise… so little elegance. Who do you think will entertain us next, little lamb?”
Naobito leaned back against nothing, arms crossed, violet energy flickering lazily around his fingers as he formed and dismissed tiny Range Crest patterns in the void.
Naobito Yagami: “The ones who understand control. The ones who don’t waste movement. Like her.”
On the screen, the next fighter stepped into the pit — a tall, graceful woman with living green vines woven through her dark hair. Lirael Thornvine again. The crowd cheered louder this time.
Naobito Yagami (quietly, almost to himself): “She’s lower rank, but she moves like she already knows how the fight ends. That’s rare.”
Yurei (laughing lightly): “You’re interested in the little green flower? How sweet. Should I be jealous, or should I help you pluck her later?”
Naobito didn’t answer. His eyes stayed on the screen as Lirael faced a hulking red Malevolence warrior. The clash was fast — vines erupted, blades of sharpened nature mana flashed, and the red user was cut down in three precise strikes. Clean. Efficient. Almost merciful.
The arena erupted.
VeilNet Anchor: “Another decisive victory for Lirael Thornvine! The Verdant Conclave’s rising star continues her streak!”
Naobito’s expression remained thoughtful.
Naobito Yagami: “She reminds me of what I used to be. Fixing things quietly. Surviving. But she turned that into a weapon. I respect that.”
Yurei: “Then when our turn comes, we’ll show her what a real weapon looks like. Together.”
In the Weavers’ VIP box, Asha Nkosi grinned widely, leaning forward.
Asha Nkosi: “The green girl has spirit. I like her. She fights like a beast who learned patience.”
Elara Vane sipped her drink elegantly, but her eyes were sharp.
Elara: “Interesting. The boy is watching her closely. Even from wherever he’s hiding.”
Lucien Moreau smiled faintly, saying nothing.
Back in space, Naobito closed his eyes for a moment, feeling the distant roar of the crowd through the broadcast.
Naobito Yagami: “Two hours until we’re called. Let’s make sure we’re ready.”
Yurei (warm, possessive, but equal): “Always, my destined one. Always.”
The Blood Tournament raged on below — rich, chaotic, and full of promise.
And in the quiet void, the real stars of the show waited, their bond stronger, their power deeper, and their destiny unfolding one brutal match at a time.
Two hours had passed.
The lower-ranked singular fighters had bled and fallen. The Crimson Veil Arena was now a cauldron of raw hunger, the crowd roaring for something greater.
In the VIP boxes, the Twelve True Weavers watched with varying degrees of tension. Elara Vane’s fingers drummed once on her armrest. Asha Nkosi grinned like a predator scenting blood. Niro Hart lounged in his private box, red wine in hand, a demonic smirk playing on his lips as Mireille knelt beside him.
Deep in the cold void of space, a violet window of Corruption Mana floated like a private theater.
Naobito Yagami and Yurei Himura watched the arena in silence.
The VeilNet Anchor’s voice suddenly boomed across the universe.
VeilNet Anchor: “The singular bouts are over! The arena now opens the Symbiote-Host bracket! From the unknown depths of space… the destined vessel and his ancient fallen maiden — Naobito Yagami and Yurei Himura!”
The entire arena fell into a breathless, oppressive silence. Thousands of eyes turned upward.
In the void, Naobito’s lips curved into a small, dangerous smile.
Naobito Yagami: “Our turn.”
He raised his hand and shattered the violet viewing window with a single pulse of Corruption Mana. The shards dissolved into glowing particles around them.
Six massive, ethereal wings of pure violet energy erupted from his back — not the heavy, crushing cloak of old, but majestic, controlled, and radiant. Yurei’s presence surged through him, their bond seamless.
Yurei (voice thrilled and intimate in his mind): “Let them see us, little lamb. Let them remember why they fear us.”
Naobito launched forward.
They tore through the vacuum of space at staggering, light-bending speeds. The violet wings burned like a comet’s tail as they slammed into the planet’s atmosphere. Friction ignited the Corruption Mana, turning their descent into a blazing violet meteor that lit up the night sky like a second sun.
The arena’s protective barriers screamed in protest as the meteor streaked downward.
BOOM.
Naobito crashed directly into the center of the fighting pit.
The impact was catastrophic.
Indestructible black stone shattered outward in a perfect radial explosion. Shockwaves ripped through the arena, cracking seats and knocking lower-rank spectators off their feet. A massive cloud of debris and violet energy erupted upward, swallowing the pit in a swirling storm of shadow and Corruption Mana.
The crowd was dead silent.
Then the smoke began to clear.
Naobito rose slowly from the crater in a classic, god-tier landing posture — one knee down, the other leg bent, head bowed, six majestic violet wings spread wide behind him like a fallen angel’s coronation. Yurei’s ethereal human form manifested around him, arms wrapped possessively around his shoulders, her pitch-black kimono flowing like liquid night as violet energy radiated from them both.
The overwhelming aura of their combined Corruption Mana washed over the entire arena.
The silence broke.
The crowd erupted in a deafening roar — fear, awe, and bloodlust colliding in one massive wave.
In the Weavers’ VIP box, several thrones shifted. Elara’s eyes widened with dark fascination. Asha Nkosi laughed wildly. Master Kael’s expression turned grave.
In his private box, Niro Hart slowly lowered his wine glass, his demonic smirk deepening with genuine interest.
Niro Hart: “Finally… something worth watching.”
High above, the VeilNet Anchor’s voice cracked with excitement.
VeilNet Anchor: “They’re here! The Symbiote-Host has arrived! The Blood Tournament will never be the same!”
Naobito rose to his full height, six wings folding slightly as Yurei’s form melted back into him, leaving only the overwhelming violet aura and the quiet, confident boy who now carried the weight of destiny itself.
The real games had just begun.
The True Weavers’ Calculation
The impact of Naobito’s landing still echoed through the Crimson Veil Arena like a second heartbeat.
Violet dust and shattered black stone settled slowly as the six majestic wings of Corruption Mana folded behind him with deliberate grace. Yurei’s ethereal form briefly manifested around his shoulders — a pitch-black kimono-clad silhouette with long flowing hair and a manic yet tender smile — before melting back into him, leaving only the overwhelming violet aura and the quiet, confident young man who now stood at the center of the universe’s gaze.
The crowd was in absolute frenzy.
But high above, in the suspended VIP arc of obsidian thrones, the Twelve True Weavers were not cheering.
They were calculating.
Master Kael Voss, Weaver of Neutrality, leaned forward first, his colorless eyes narrowing as he studied the density of the six wings still faintly shimmering around Naobito.
Master Kael Voss: “Look at the density of those wings, Elara. A vessel should not be able to channel that volume of Corruption Mana without his body deteriorating. The seams should be tearing. Yet he stands… unbroken.”
Lady Elara Vane’s violet-gray eyes widened a fraction, her silver rings clicking once as she gripped the arm of her throne.
Lady Elara Vane: “A perfect vessel. Not cracked. Not leaking. Fully symbiotic. The Mistress’s design… it worked too well.”
The realization spread like silver threads through the circle.
Amara Khalil (Weaver of Souls, gentle but troubled): “His soul is no longer a cage for her. It is a home. I have never seen such harmony between mortal and fallen. It is… beautiful. And terrifying.”
Viktor Drago (Weaver of Chains, growling): “Beautiful? It is an abomination! If he can wield her power without breaking, what chance do our chains have? We should seal him now, before he grows stronger!”
Mei Ling Zhao (Weaver of Silence) said nothing. She simply watched with closed eyes, but the faint frown on her face spoke volumes.
Isabella Cortez (Weaver of Flames, grinning fiercely): “Oh, I like this. A boy who can dance with a goddess without burning alive? Let him fight. I want to see how hot that violet fire really burns.”
Omar al-Sayed (Weaver of Sands, calm but wary): “The desert has seen many storms. This one carries the weight of destiny itself. If he wins this tournament… the sands will shift in ways we cannot predict.”
Asha Nkosi (Weaver of Beasts, laughing with primal delight): “Finally! A worthy beast to test myself against. Let him come. I want to see if he can match my claws.”
Dr. Elias Thorn (Weaver of Flesh, clinical and hungry): “Fascinating physiology. The symbiosis is flawless. No rejection. No decay. I would give anything to study the exact nature of their bond.”
Freya Haldorsdottir (Weaver of Storms, lightning crackling in her eyes): “The storm approaches. If he wins, the balance we have maintained for centuries may be washed away. But storms also bring renewal. I am… curious.”
Soren Blackwood (Weaver of Shadows, voice like darkness itself): “The shadows whisper his name already. They hunger for what he carries. If he is not contained, they may consume us all.”
High Weaver Selene Arkwright (Weaver of Fate) remained the calmest, but even she could not hide the gravity in her expression.
High Weaver Selene: “The Mistress crafted him as one of six keys. And now the puzzle is waking up. If he wins this tournament… we may no longer have the power to seal him. He will become something beyond our Law.”
In the opposite luxury box, Niro Hart lowered his glass, his demonic smirk deepening with genuine intrigue.
Niro Hart: “Well played, little vessel. Now the real game begins.”
Back in the pit, Naobito stood tall, six wings slowly folding away as he looked up toward the Weavers’ section with calm, violet-flecked eyes.
The Blood Tournament had just become something far greater than a simple competition.
It had become the first move in a game that could rewrite reality itself.
The VeilNet Anchor’s voice boomed across the Crimson Veil Arena, ready to hype the next phase of the Blood Tournament.
VeilNet Anchor: “And now, for the first Symbiote-Host match of the night—”
He never finished.
A sudden, overwhelming wave of cyan mana flooded the entire arena.
It was not violent. It was graceful. Elegant. Like liquid starlight poured into the void.
The sound of heels — sharp, confident, and impossibly loud — echoed across the obsidian floor. Each step kissed the black stone with crystalline clarity, sending soft ripples of cyan energy outward like blooming lotuses.
Every single person in the arena froze.
Breaths caught in thousands of throats.
Eyes widened in shock and recognition.
In the VIP box, Niro Hart’s glass slipped from his fingers and shattered. His usual demonic smirk vanished, replaced by a rare, genuine flash of surprise.
Niro Hart (under his breath, a shipping-container-heavy insult): “That fucking bitch…”
Because it was her.
From the center of the fighting pit, where the cyan mana had coalesced into a perfect circle, a woman stepped forward.
She was breathtaking — tall, regal, with long flowing hair the color of midnight laced with starlight. Her presence alone commanded the entire arena. The cyan mana swirled around her like a living crown, pure and ancient.
The Mistress of Threads — Hana Yagami — had arrived.
The Twelve True Weavers rose from their thrones in unison, expressions ranging from reverence to barely contained shock.
High Weaver Selene Arkwright bowed her head deeply.
Selene: “Mistress…”
Hana Yagami’s gaze swept across the arena, calm and absolute. Her eyes — the same sharp, knowing eyes Naobito would one day recognize as his own blood — finally settled on the empty space where the Symbiote-Host pair was supposed to appear.
A small, enigmatic smile curved her lips.
Hana Yagami: “Let the real game begin.”
The arena, which had been roaring moments ago, was now dead silent — thousands of beings from across the universe holding their breath in the presence of the one who had stitched reality itself.
And somewhere in the cold void of deep space, Naobito and Yurei felt the pull.
Yurei (voice soft, almost reverent in his mind): “She’s here, little lamb. The one who made the puzzle… the one who made you.”
Naobito’s violet eyes narrowed as he felt the cyan mana calling to him across the stars.
The Blood Tournament had just been elevated to something far beyond blood.
It had become a family reunion.
The impact of Naobito’s meteor-like descent still echoed through the Crimson Veil Arena.
Shattered black stone and violet dust hung in the air as he knelt in the exact center of the fighting pit — one knee down, the other leg bent, six majestic wings of Corruption Mana slowly folding behind him. Yurei’s ethereal form briefly wrapped around his shoulders like a protective shroud before melting back into him, leaving only the overwhelming violet aura and the young man who had just announced his arrival with god-tier force.
The crowd was still roaring.
Then Hana Yagami stepped forward.
The cyan mana surged.
A head-tearing, soul-splitting headache slammed into Naobito without mercy. The same agony ripped through Yurei’s consciousness. Both of them — vessel and maiden — gripped their heads in unison.
Naobito Yagami: “AAAHH—!”
Yurei (screaming with him in his mind): “It burns—!”
The memory tore through them like violet lightning.
Memory — The Night of the First Key
A soft nursery bathed in gentle cyan light. Hana Yagami, the Mistress of Threads, sat in a flowing silk robe the color of starlit midnight, cradling an infant in her arms. The baby — tiny, perfect, with a faint violet spark already glowing in his eyes — cooed softly.
The door opened. A tall, dignified man stepped in — her husband.
Husband: “How is he?”
Hana looked up, her smile radiant and full of quiet, terrifying pride.
Hana Yagami: “He’s perfect.”
She gently traced a finger along the baby’s cheek, weaving a single invisible thread of destiny into his soul.
Hana Yagami: “One of six keys. My little puzzle… you will open doors even the Weavers cannot imagine.”
Back in the arena, Naobito screamed.
The sound tore out of him raw and broken — a sound no one in the Blood Tournament had expected from the god-tier arrival that had just cracked the indestructible floor.
He collapsed forward, hands clawing at his head, violet energy crackling wildly around him. The crowd’s roar died into stunned silence.
Black bandages erupted from nowhere, wrapping tightly around his own mouth and silencing the scream mid-shriek. His body trembled violently as the memory burned itself into his soul.
He remained kneeling in the center of the shattered pit — surrounded by debris, exposed under the lights of millions watching on VeilNet — with absolutely nowhere to hide.
The unstoppable Symbiote-Host who had just crashed into the arena like a violet meteor was now on his knees before the Mistress of Threads, shaking like a broken child.
The Twelve True Weavers rose from their thrones in unison, expressions ranging from shock to calculation.
High Weaver Selene Arkwright stared with wide eyes.
Master Kael Voss frowned deeply.
Elara Vane leaned forward, silver rings clicking.
Niro Hart in his private box lowered his glass, his demonic smirk replaced by genuine intrigue.
Hana Yagami stood at the edge of the pit, cyan mana swirling gracefully around her. Her gaze — ancient, knowing, and strangely maternal — settled on the kneeling boy with the black bandages sealing his mouth.
Hana Yagami (voice soft, carrying across the silent arena): “My little puzzle… you finally remember.”
Naobito slowly lifted his head, eyes wide and pained, meeting hers across the shattered stone.
The Blood Tournament had just become something far more personal.
Naobito remained kneeling for one long, agonizing heartbeat — black bandages still sealing his mouth, body trembling from the memory that had torn through him like violet lightning.
Then he slowly pushed himself up.
He rose to his full height in the center of the shattered pit, six ethereal wings of Corruption Mana flickering once before folding neatly against his back. A thin trail of blood dripped from the corner of his mouth. He spat it out — a single dark drop hitting the broken obsidian with a quiet plink.
His voice came out hoarse but steady, the bandages loosening just enough to let him speak.
Naobito Yagami: “I don’t care if you’re my mother, Mistress.”
The entire arena seemed to hold its breath.
Hana Yagami — the Mistress of Threads — stood at the edge of the pit, cyan mana swirling around her like living starlight. Her expression remained calm, almost gentle, but her voice carried the absolute authority of the one who had stitched reality itself.
Hana Yagami: “You will address me as ‘Ka-san’ if speaking directly to me. And if you cannot respect me as a mother… respect me as an elder. End of discussion, young man.”
The words hung in the air like a command woven into the fabric of existence.
Yurei Himura (from within Naobito, telepathically, with a rare note of genuine appreciation and dark amusement): ‘Heh… I like her. She has the same fire I once had — the kind that refuses to be denied. A true weaver who knows exactly what she made when she crafted you, little lamb. Respect her… for now. She might be the only one in this circus who actually understands what we are.’
Naobito wiped the remaining blood from his lip with the back of his hand, violet eyes meeting Hana’s without flinching.
The Weavers in their VIP box watched in stunned silence. Elara’s fingers had stopped drumming. Kael’s colorless eyes were narrowed. Asha Nkosi grinned like she was watching the best fight of the night.
In his private box, Niro Hart chuckled softly, raising his glass in a mocking toast.
Niro Hart: “Family reunions are always so touching.”
Hana Yagami’s gaze never left Naobito. A faint, enigmatic smile played on her lips — the smile of a mother who had waited years for her puzzle to finally wake up.
Hana Yagami: “Good. Now… shall we see what my little puzzle can do in this Blood Tournament?”
The arena lights pulsed violet and cyan in unison as the crowd — still recovering from the shock — began to roar once more.
Naobito stood tall in the center of the shattered pit, Yurei’s presence steady and appreciative in his mind, and the weight of destiny pressing down on him like never before.
The real fight was about to begin.
The arena lights pulsed in anticipation as Hana Yagami raised her hand to summon the first true test for her son.
Cyan mana swirled in the center of the pit, coalescing into something ancient and terrifying — a gate of starlit threads beginning to open.
Before it could fully manifest, a savage roar shattered the air.
Lord Varak of the Red Abyss exploded through the incomplete Folded gate, shattering it with raw Malevolence force. The towering demon lord landed heavily in the pit, red energy erupting around him like fresh blood. Horns curled like crowns, ritual scars glowing, eyes burning with arrogant fury.
Lord Varak (roaring at the Mistress): “Enough waiting! I will be the one to test this so-called destined vessel! Not some ancient summon — ME!”
Hana Yagami lowered her hand slowly. A faint, terrifyingly amused smile touched her lips as she regarded the interrupting demon lord like a child throwing a tantrum.
Hana Yagami: “Very well. Take out the trash before you face my real test, my son.”
The crowd went wild.
Naobito stood tall in the center of the shattered pit, six wings half-unfurled, violet energy crackling around him. Yurei’s presence surged in perfect sync.
Yurei (voice thrilled in his mind): “A loud, arrogant demon who thinks he can interrupt the Mistress herself? How delicious. Break him, little lamb. Show them all what a perfect vessel can do.”
Naobito Yagami (calm, eyes glowing violet): “Gladly.”
Lord Varak charged with a bellow, red Malevolence Mana exploding outward in a brutal wave. He swung a massive fist that could level buildings.
Naobito didn’t dodge.
He stepped forward and met the punch with an open palm.
The impact created a shockwave that cracked the already damaged floor further. But Naobito didn’t budge.
Violet Corruption Mana flared around his hand, perfectly containing and neutralizing the red energy on contact. The Malevolence Mana was absorbed, converted, and fed back into Naobito’s reserves in a seamless flow.
Naobito Yagami: “Your power is loud. But it’s empty.”
He twisted his wrist. Black bandages erupted from the ground, wrapping around Varak’s arm and yanking the demon lord off balance. Naobito followed with a precise elbow strike to the demon’s ribs, infused with controlled Corruption.
Varak staggered, roaring in pain and rage, but Naobito was already moving — graceful, efficient, and utterly dominant. Every strike was calculated. Every counter dismantled the demon’s raw power without wasting a single movement.
The fight wasn’t a brawl.
It was a systematic dismantling.
Within seconds, Varak was on his knees, red mana flickering weakly as violet bandages bound his arms and torso.
Naobito stood over him, six wings fully spread for a moment before folding away.
Naobito Yagami: “You’re nothing compared to what we are.”
The arena was silent for a heartbeat… then exploded into the loudest roar of the night.
In the Weavers’ box, the Twelve True Weavers watched with a mixture of awe and unease.
Elara Vane (softly): “A perfect vessel indeed…”
Niro Hart (in his private box, raising his glass with a dark smile): “Well played, little puzzle. You’re even more interesting than I thought.”
Hana Yagami simply watched her son with quiet, maternal pride.
The Blood Tournament had just witnessed its first true statement.
And the real test was still to come.
Niro Hart’s Private Luxury Box – Crimson Veil Arena
The roar of the crowd was a distant thunder.
Niro Hart lounged in his blood-red throne-like chair, one leg crossed over the other, crimson Malevolence Mana flickering lazily around his fingers like dying embers. His white shirt and black vest remained immaculate, untouched by the chaos below.
Mireille Vale knelt gracefully beside him, silver-white hair cascading down her back, her yandere gaze fixed on him with that familiar, dangerous adoration. She watched the holographic replay of Naobito’s swift, clinical dismantling of Lord Varak with a faint, possessive smile.
Mireille: “I see why you favor him, Master. He is remarkably like you. Though I confess, I am surprised he did not kill that ‘thing’… perhaps witnessing it would have cultivated a measure of forbearance within me.”
Niro chuckled lowly, the sound rich and demonic. He reached down and idly stroked her hair once, like one would a loyal but dangerous pet.
Niro Hart: “Forbearance? How quaint. The boy still clings to some scrap of humanity. He dismantled the demon with precision, not pleasure. That’s the difference between a tool and a craftsman.”
He took a slow sip of his crimson wine, eyes never leaving the arena pit where Naobito now stood, violet aura still radiating.
Niro Hart: “But you’re right. He is like me. A broken thing that learned to break the world instead. I wonder how long that forbearance will last when the real monsters come out to play.”
Mireille leaned into his touch, eyes half-lidded with obsessive delight.
Mireille: “If he ever becomes a threat to you, Master… I will personally ensure he never leaves the pit alive.”
Niro’s smirk deepened, cruel and amused.
Niro Hart: “Let’s hope it doesn’t come to that, my dear. I’d hate to lose such an entertaining new toy so soon.”
Below them, the arena lights shifted again, preparing for the next match.
The Blood Tournament continued.
But in the luxury box, Niro Hart’s interest in the destined vessel had just grown a little sharper.
Watchers from Earth's Clans, Organizations, and Corporations — Through VeilNet
The Crimson Veil Arena’s roar was a distant thunder, but on Earth, the real war was being fought in silence.
VeilNet — the underground broadcast network for the mana society — was streaming live across every hidden channel, encrypted server, and black-market feed. Thousands of powerful eyes on Earth were glued to their screens.
The Hidden Clans – A Traditional Japanese Estate
In a moonlit garden behind warded walls, the elders of the Kurogane Clan (Saya’s family) sat in a traditional room, holographic screens floating above a low table.
The clan head, Saya’s stern father, watched Naobito’s dominant display with narrowed eyes.
Kurogane Clan Head: “The boy who was once bullied in their school… now stands as a Symbiote-Host with an ancient fallen maiden. And he dismantled a demon lord like it was nothing.”
One elder whispered, “The Weavers are losing control. If he wins this tournament…”
The head slammed his fist down.
Kurogane Clan Head: “We watch. We wait. If the boy returns, Saya will be our bridge… or our weapon.”
Corporate Boardroom – Hart Industries Rival Tower
In a sleek, high-rise boardroom overlooking Tokyo, executives from a rival mega-corporation watched the feed in stunned silence.
The CEO, a sharp-suited woman with faint green Nature Mana flickering in her eyes, leaned forward.
CEO: “Niro Hart is already moving on him. If that boy allies with Hart Industries… we’re finished.”
An advisor whispered, “He’s not fighting for the Weavers. He’s independent. That makes him dangerous.”
The CEO smiled coldly.
CEO: “Then we make sure he never leaves the arena alive. Or we make him an offer he can’t refuse.”
Underground Organization – The Veil Wardens (Neutral Enforcers)
In a hidden bunker beneath the city, a group of rogue vessels and independent mages watched the broadcast on multiple screens.
Their leader, a scarred woman with silver Neutralization Mana, crossed her arms.
Leader: “The destined vessel and the Dark Maiden… working as one. The Weavers are panicking. Niro Hart is watching like a shark. This tournament is going to break the balance we’ve fought to maintain for decades.”
A younger member asked, “Do we intervene?”
Leader: “Not yet. But if the boy wins… we offer him sanctuary. The world needs someone who can stand between the Weavers and the monsters like Hart.”
Back in the Arena
The crowd was still buzzing from Naobito’s entrance and the memory flashback.
Hana Yagami stood calmly at the edge of the pit, cyan mana swirling around her.
Naobito stood tall in the center, six wings folded, the black bandages around his mouth slowly dissolving as he regained control.
Naobito Yagami (voice steady, eyes glowing violet): “I’m ready for the real fight.”
Yurei (soft, proud in his mind): “That’s my boy.”
The Blood Tournament continued.
But across Earth, clans, corporations, and organizations watched with bated breath — knowing that whatever happened in the arena tonight would reshape the world.
The arena was still buzzing from Naobito’s landing and the memory flash when the lights shifted again — this time to a brilliant, crackling cyan mixed with silver.
The VeilNet Anchor’s voice trembled with excitement.
VeilNet Anchor: “For the next Symbiote-Host bracket match… a legend returns from the grave! The previous Sword Saint of Lightning — thought dead for over a decade — Isolde Aster!”
The ground in the opposite side of the pit cracked open.
From the glowing cyan light stepped a tall, armored woman.
Isolde Aster — the former Sword Saint of Lightning.
She wore ornate, battle-worn silver-and-cyan plate armor that hummed with restrained power. A long, elegant lightning-shaped greatsword rested on her shoulder. Her silver hair flowed wildly down her back, and her eyes crackled with electric cyan energy. A faint, excited smile played on her lips as she looked straight at Naobito.
The crowd lost their minds.
In the Weavers’ VIP box, High Weaver Selene Arkwright shot to her feet.
High Weaver Selene Arkwright: “How…?”
Lucien Moreau leaned back in his throne, calm as ever.
Lucien Moreau: “Pure cyan mana… or Malevolence red mana… can revive the dead. Under the right conditions.”
Viktor Drago slammed his fist on the arm of his throne, chains rattling furiously.
Viktor Drago: “Why didn’t the Mistress— heck, WHY DIDN’T YOU TELL US, ублюдок?!”
Lucien gave a small, unapologetic shrug.
Lucien Moreau: “I only tell the eleven of you what you need to know.”
Isolde Aster stepped fully into the pit, her lightning-charged armor humming. She looked at Naobito with clear, excited interest.
Isolde Aster (voice strong, carrying across the arena, with a playful smile): “So you’re the famous destined vessel everyone’s whispering about. Handsome one, aren’t you? I’ve been dead for ten years… but fighting a legend like you feels like a good reason to come back to life.”
Naobito stood tall in the center, six wings half-unfurled, violet energy crackling around him. Yurei’s voice purred in his mind.
Yurei: “A revived Sword Saint of Lightning… and she’s flirting with you? How bold. Let’s show her what a real storm looks like, little lamb.”
Naobito cracked his knuckles, a small, confident smirk forming.
Naobito Yagami: “Let’s see if the dead can keep up with the living.”
The crowd roared as the two powerhouses faced each other in the shattered pit.
The real fight was about to begin
The arena lights flared.
Isolde Aster moved first.
In a single heartbeat, the revived Sword Saint of Lightning transformed into a literal bolt of cyan lightning — god-speed, blinding, unstoppable. Her massive greatsword trailed behind her like a comet’s tail as she streaked straight for Naobito’s neck, aiming to decapitate him before he could even register the attack.
Naobito’s own reaction time wasn’t enough.
But Yurei’s instincts were.
The six ethereal violet wings snapped forward in a blur, layering into an impenetrable shield of raw Corruption Mana. The cyan lightning bolt slammed into the wings with cataclysmic force.
BOOM.
The impact created a massive, blinding shockwave. A dome of crackling, warring energy — cyan lightning versus violet corruption — exploded outward and completely blanketed the fighting pit. The crowd, the cameras, and even the True Weavers in their VIP box lost all visibility. The entire arena was swallowed by the roaring, violet-cyan storm.
Inside the eye of the storm, the world narrowed to a single, crushing stalemate.
Isolde’s greatsword was locked against Naobito’s layered wings, sparks of cyan and violet exploding between them. Their eyes met through the chaos — hers electric blue, his glowing violet.
Isolde’s playful smirk vanished instantly.
She leaned in close, voice a urgent whisper only he could hear, barely audible over the raging energy dome.
Isolde Aster: “Your mother sends a message, boy. This tournament is not a game. It is a filter. The remaining keys are hidden among the fighters. Win them all… and you will unlock what she has been weaving since the day you were born. Fail… and the Mistress will have no choice but to erase you herself. She loves you in her own way. Don’t make her regret it.”
The pressure between them remained crushing, their weapons still locked in a show of overwhelming force for anyone watching from outside the dome.
Naobito’s eyes widened for a fraction of a second, then narrowed with dark understanding.
Naobito Yagami (voice low, steady): “Tell her… I’m not her puzzle anymore. I’m the one who solves it.”
Isolde’s smirk returned — sharp, excited, and full of lightning.
Isolde Aster: “Good. Now let’s give them a show they’ll never forget.”
The energy dome shattered outward in a violent explosion of cyan and violet light.
The crowd roared as the two powerhouses separated, both unharmed, both radiating overwhelming auras.
Inside the raging cyan-violet storm dome, everything changed in an instant.
Yurei felt herself pulled — violently, inexorably — deeper into Naobito’s consciousness. The world outside vanished. The roaring crowd, the clashing mana, even the pressure of Isolde’s greatsword… all of it faded.
She found herself standing in a dark, gritty, rain-soaked version of Naobito’s old apartment.
The walls were cracked and peeling, water dripping from the ceiling. Dim violet light flickered from a single hanging bulb. Broken puzzles and half-fixed clocks littered the floor like forgotten bones. The air smelled of dust, old blood, and loneliness.
Yurei’s ethereal form — pitch-black kimono flowing around her — looked around with wide, manic eyes.
Yurei Himura: “What is this…?”
A calm, ancient voice echoed through the dark apartment, soft yet absolute.
Hana Yagami (Mistress of Threads): “You have your key. Find the lock, Himura.”
Yurei’s head snapped up. Her lips curled into a dangerous, thrilled smile.
Yurei: “So that’s how you want to play it, Mother of Puzzles…”
Outside, in the physical world, Naobito’s body moved on its own — but it was only him now.
The six wings remained, but they were powered purely by his will and the Corruption Mana he had learned to control. No Yurei riding him. No extra eyes watching for him. Just him, his critical thinking, and the power she had willingly lent.
Isolde Aster’s greatsword came down again in a lightning-fast arc.
Naobito dodged with raw, trained precision — the kind he had hammered into his body during endless training in the Override Zone. He countered with a sharp elbow strike infused with violet energy, forcing the revived Sword Saint back a step.
Naobito Yagami (gritting his teeth, voice steady): “She’s testing us… separately.”
Yurei (from deep inside his mind, searching the dark apartment): “Then let’s pass her test, little lamb. I’m looking for the lock. You keep her busy. Make it look convincing.”
Naobito smirked, six wings flaring as he launched forward to meet Isolde’s next lightning strike.
The fight continued in the physical world — brutal, fast, and perfectly balanced — while inside Naobito’s mind, Yurei moved through the dark, gritty apartment, searching for the hidden lock that would complete the next key.
The Mistress’s game had just become far more personal.
The arena had become a storm of light and violence.
The Physical World: The Solo Craftsman
Naobito bled.
A shallow cut across his cheek wept warm blood down his jaw. His muscles burned. Without Yurei’s hyper-sensory instincts riding him, the world moved at terrifying speed.
Isolde Aster vanished in a crack of cyan lightning and reappeared directly above him, her greatsword surging downward like a guillotine of pure electricity.
Naobito didn’t have time to think.
He reacted on pure training — two of the six ethereal violet wings snapped upward, crossing above his head in a manual block. The blade slammed into them with cataclysmic force, sparks of cyan and violet exploding outward. The impact rattled his bones, but the wings held.
He shifted his weight instantly, sweeping a low kick at Isolde’s legs while simultaneously flaring Corruption Mana through the other four wings to push her back.
Isolde flipped mid-air, landing gracefully on the cracked obsidian, eyes wide with genuine surprise and delight.
Isolde Aster (laughing, lightning arcing between her teeth): “You’re fighting manually! The boy has teeth of his own!”
Naobito wiped the blood from his cheek, breathing heavy but controlled. The violet aura around him suddenly surged — sharper, brighter — as the first broken node in his mindscape was bridged.
Naobito Yagami (eyes flashing with violent, brilliant light): “I told you. I’m the one who solves the puzzle.”
He launched forward, six wings flaring behind him like a fallen angel’s wrath. He wasn’t just surviving anymore. He was buying time.
The Mindscape: Bridging the Broken System
Inside Naobito’s mind, the dark, rain-soaked apartment trembled.
Yurei stood at the old scarred workbench, her pitch-black kimono trailing through dust and shattered puzzle pieces. Before her lay the “lock” — a complex, physical manifestation of Naobito’s soul: a burned-out logic board of metallic pathways and geometric nodes, cyan threads of the Mistress’s magic having severed the connections long ago.
Yurei Himura (whispering to the empty room, manic smile softening into intense focus): “A severed connection. She wants to see if I can put him back together.”
