She raised her hand. Pure, liquid Corruption Mana dripped from her fingers like black mercury. With absolute precision, she pressed them to the board, acting as living solder.
Violet energy surged through the dead pathways, forcing connections where none had existed for years. The room shook. Rain fell harder from the cracked ceiling.
With every fused node, Naobito’s power outside grew sharper.
The Physical World
Naobito moved like a craftsman wielding the most dangerous tool in existence.
He didn’t rely on Yurei to auto-pilot. He manually directed the wings, the Corruption Mana, every ounce of power she had lent him. Isolde’s lightning blitzes were met with calculated counters — wings blocking, bandages lashing out to disrupt her footing, violet energy absorbing and redirecting her strikes.
The crowd was losing their minds.
The Mindscape
Yurei’s fingers burned as she bridged another major node. The board began to glow — faint at first, then brighter.
Yurei (gritting her teeth, laughing through the pain): “Come on… wake up for me, my destined one.”
The Physical World
Naobito’s eyes flashed with new, violent brilliance. He launched forward, six wings fully extended, and met Isolde’s next lightning strike head-on.
The clash sent a shockwave that cracked the arena floor even further.
He was fighting alone.
And he was winning.
The Mindscape: The Final Bypass
Yurei wiped a bead of sweat from her pale forehead, black kimono clinging to her skin in the humid, rain-soaked apartment.
The logic board of Naobito’s soul lay before her on the scarred workbench — almost whole, but the central power distribution node remained completely severed. The Mistress’s cyan magic had burned the original traces away entirely.
She couldn’t follow the old paths.
She had to create something new.
Yurei raised her hand. A dense, hyper-focused bead of liquid Corruption Mana formed at her fingertips — pure, black mercury. With absolute precision, she pressed it across the void, acting as living solder.
She bridged the gap.
The black mercury of her power fused the dead substrate, soldering the final geometric node directly to the core. A perfect, unbreakable joint.
The moment the connection cooled, the entire board surged to life.
It wasn’t just a lock clicking open.
It was an entire system booting up.
The gritty, rain-soaked apartment flooded with brilliant violet light as the dormant architecture of Naobito’s soul finally registered the completed circuit. The “Key” had never been a physical object.
It was administrative access.
Yurei exhaled a breathless, triumphant laugh.
Yurei Himura: “System online, little lamb. Give her hell.”
The Physical World: System Override
Naobito felt the exact millisecond the circuit closed.
A cataclysmic surge of energy tore through him. The newly completed pathways in his soul stepped up his mana output exponentially — a flawless voltage converter driving his reserves into overdrive.
The six ethereal wings on his back violently shifted. Their feathered, ghostly edges snapped into sharp focus, solidifying into rigid, perfectly structured geometric blades of pure violet light.
Isolde Aster felt the atmospheric pressure in the arena drop. She skidded back, cyan greatsword humming with lethal intensity. Her eyes widened as she sensed the sudden, terrifying optimization of his aura.
Isolde Aster (raising her blade, lightning blinding the arena): “Show me, little craftsman! Show me the storm!”
She launched her ultimate technique — a blinding, arena-spanning wave of pure cyan electricity designed to vaporize anything in its path. A god-tier execution strike.
Naobito didn’t brace. He didn’t cross his wings to block.
He simply reached out.
With the internal architecture of his soul perfectly connected, his control over the Corruption Mana was absolute. He stepped directly into the lightning and caught the glowing tip of Isolde’s greatsword between his bare palms.
The kinetic force shattered the obsidian beneath his feet, but Naobito didn’t budge an inch. The cyan lightning tried to shred his arms, but he grounded the energy perfectly. He routed her raw electrical mana through his newly completed internal circuits, neutralizing the lethal charge and seamlessly absorbing it into his own system.
Isolde’s eyes widened in utter shock as the light died from her blade.
Before she could pull back, Naobito flicked his wrists, violently twisting the greatsword out of her grip. The massive weapon embedded itself into the arena wall with a deafening crash.
In the same breath, Naobito closed the distance. He stopped moving exactly one inch away from her, bringing one of his rigid, razor-sharp violet wings to rest gently against the side of her throat.
The roaring crowd fell into a stunned, breathless silence.
Naobito stood tall, the violet energy radiating from him now perfectly calm, humming with the quiet, terrifying efficiency of a completed machine.
Isolde looked at the wing at her throat, then up into Naobito’s glowing violet eyes. She didn’t look defeated.
She looked absolutely thrilled.
Isolde Aster (whispering, a proud smirk on her lips): “Pass.”
The arena remained dead silent for one heartbeat.
Then it exploded into the loudest roar of the night.
The Crash
The competitor’s lounge was dim and quiet, concrete walls scarred from decades of mana-fueled violence. The roar of the arena felt like a distant storm.
Naobito Yagami stumbled through the door, adrenaline crashing hard. His body burned — muscles screaming, a shallow cut across his cheek still bleeding, ribs aching from the final exchange with Isolde Aster. The six wings had long since folded away, but the weight of the fight lingered in every breath.
He slumped onto a cold metal bench, elbows on his knees, head bowed. Sweat and blood dripped onto the floor.
Yurei Himura (soft, concerned in his mind): “You did well, little lamb. Breathe. I’m right here.”
From the shadows near the far wall, a flat, emotionless voice cut through the silence.
Reno: “Power output increased by 47%. Mana reserves stabilized at 2.1 million Seams. Corruption Mana leakage reduced to 0.8%. Tactical assessment: optimized vessel. High threat level to containment protocols.”
Reno stepped forward, gray eyes cold and clinical, as if Naobito were nothing more than a weapon to be measured.
Reno: “You are no longer a leaking anomaly. You are an efficient weapon. The Weavers will take note.”
Naobito didn’t look up. He just breathed, slow and heavy, the sting of the cut on his cheek a reminder that he was still human.
Then softer footsteps approached.
Saya Kurogane knelt in front of him, silver Neutralization Mana glowing gently around her hands. Her eyes were full of genuine sorrow — not pity, but understanding.
She reached out slowly, offering a damp, clean towel.
Saya: “Here. For your cheek.”
Naobito lifted his head. Their eyes met. He took the towel with a tired hand and pressed it to the cut.
Saya (voice gentle, almost breaking): “You don’t have to be a weapon in this room, Naobito. Not right now. You can just… breathe. You’re allowed to be tired. You’re allowed to be human for a minute.”
Yurei (quiet in his mind, watching with a strange mix of jealousy and reluctant respect): “She sees you. Not the vessel. Not the power. Just… you. How annoying. And how… nice.”
Naobito closed his eyes for a long second, the towel pressed to his face. Reno’s cold analysis still hung in the air like a blade. Saya’s warmth felt like the first real breath he’d taken since stepping into the pit.
He lowered the towel, looking between the emotionless pawn and the compassionate woman.
Naobito Yagami (voice hoarse, but steady): “One of you sees a weapon. The other sees a person. I’m starting to wonder which one is right.”
Saya offered a small, sad smile.
Saya: “You get to decide that. Every single day.”
Reno remained silent, watching like a loaded gun.
In the background, the roar of the arena continued — the Blood Tournament waiting for its next monster.
But in this quiet, dim lounge, Naobito sat between cold calculation and gentle compassion, trying to remember what it felt like to just be himself.
Deep Space – The Void Observatory
Naobito floated in the quiet violet pocket, eyes closed, still processing the memory of his mother holding him as an infant. The headache had faded, but the emotional aftershock lingered.
Then the void around him shifted.
A new presence folded in — not cyan like Hana, not violet like them. It was something warmer, older, laced with quiet amusement.
A man stepped out of a soft, golden Fold, hands in the pockets of a tailored black coat. He looked to be in his early thirties, but his eyes were ancient. Tall, strikingly handsome in a way that felt almost unfair — sharp jawline, messy dark hair with silver streaks, and a lazy, charming smile that could disarm both gods and monsters. There was something magnetic about him, something that made the void itself seem to lean in.
Kiritaka Yagami — the Mistress’s husband, Naobito’s father, and quite possibly the most immortal pain in the universe.
He looked at his son with a smug, affectionate grin.
Kiritaka: “Well, well… look at you. All grown up and cracking arenas like it’s a Tuesday. Your mother always said you’d be dramatic.”
Naobito’s eyes snapped open. Yurei’s presence sharpened instantly inside him.
Naobito Yagami: “…Father.”
Kiritaka (chuckling, spreading his arms): “In the flesh. Or what counts as flesh after the 47th time I’ve died and come back. Immortality’s a bitch, but the revival process does wonders for the skin.”
He stepped closer, studying Naobito with that same smug-but-caring gaze — the look of a man who had watched his wife weave their son into a cosmic puzzle and was proud of the result.
Kiritaka: “You’ve got her eyes when you’re angry, you know. And my stubborn jaw. Dangerous combination.”
Yurei (inside Naobito’s mind, intrigued and slightly wary): “He’s handsome… in that ‘I’ve survived everything and still flirt with death’ kind of way. I see where you get it from, little lamb.”
Naobito remained tense, fists clenched at his sides.
Naobito Yagami: “You knew. Both of you. You let her turn me into a key.”
Kiritaka’s smile softened, but didn’t disappear. He reached out and gently placed a hand on Naobito’s shoulder — warm, paternal, and annoyingly comforting.
Kiritaka: “We didn’t ‘let’ her do anything. Your mother doesn’t ask permission when she weaves fate. But yes… I knew. And I agreed. Because the alternative was watching this universe slowly rot under bad stitches. You were always going to be special, Naobito. We just made sure you’d be strong enough to survive it.”
He gave Naobito’s shoulder a light, caring squeeze.
Kiritaka: “Proud of you, by the way. Taking on a demon lord without letting Yurei pilot the whole fight? That’s my boy. Though next time, maybe don’t let the revived lightning saint call you ‘handsome one’ quite so openly. Your mother gets territorial.”
Yurei (snorting in Naobito’s head): “I like him. He’s smug. I respect smug.”
Naobito exhaled slowly, the tension in his shoulders easing just a fraction.
Naobito Yagami: “Why are you here now?”
Kiritaka’s expression turned more serious, though the smug smile never fully left.
Kiritaka: “Because the remaining keys are waking up. And because your mother is watching you very closely right now. She loves you, in her own terrifying, reality-bending way. Try not to make her cry. She gets creative when she cries.”
He stepped back, the Fold already beginning to pull him away.
Kiritaka: “Oh, and tell Yurei I said hello. She’s exactly the kind of chaos your mother hoped you’d find.”
With a final wink and that annoyingly handsome grin, Kiritaka vanished back into the Fold, leaving only a faint golden thread lingering in the void.
Naobito stood there in silence for a long moment.
Yurei (soft, almost fond): “Your father is insufferable… and I think I like him.”
Naobito let out a tired, bitter laugh.
The Blood Tournament was still raging far below.
But now, another piece of the puzzle had just walked into his life — alive, immortal, and far too smug about it.
The roar of the Crimson Veil Arena slowly died down as Hana Yagami stepped forward to the edge of the shattered pit. Her presence alone commanded silence — cyan mana swirling around her like living constellations.
She raised one elegant hand, and the entire arena’s lights dimmed to a deep, reverent violet and cyan glow.
Hana Yagami (voice calm, absolute, and carrying to every corner of the stadium and VeilNet broadcast): “Listen well, warriors of the Nine Realms. The Blood Tournament has always been a filter. Tonight, that filter becomes clearer.”
She turned her gaze toward Naobito, who stood tall in the center of the destroyed pit, six wings half-folded behind him.
Hana Yagami: “My son has already qualified for the finals. He will not fight in the qualification rounds. Every fighter from this moment forward will be competing for one thing only — the right to face him in the final showdown.”
A stunned wave rippled through the crowd.
Then it exploded into chaos.
Thousands of voices roared — some in excitement, some in rage, some in pure bloodlust. The holographic screens above the arena flashed with updated brackets. Naobito’s name was already locked at the very top, glowing in violet.
VeilNet Anchor (voice cracking with hype): “You heard the Mistress herself! Naobito Yagami and Yurei Himura are straight into the finals! Everyone else fights for the honor — and the nightmare — of facing the destined vessel!”
In the Weavers’ VIP box, reactions were immediate.
Viktor Drago snarled, chains rattling. Master Kael Voss remained deathly still. Elara Vane smiled thinly, intrigued. Asha Nkosi laughed loudly, slamming her fist on her throne. Lucien Moreau simply watched with that knowing, ancient smile.
In his private luxury box, Niro Hart let out a low, delighted chuckle.
Niro Hart: “Straight to the finals… how generous of Mommy. This just got delicious.”
Mireille leaned closer to him, eyes shining with obsession.
Mireille: “Shall I prepare something special for the finals, Master?”
Niro Hart: “Not yet. I want to see how many of these fools die trying to earn the right to face him.”
Back in the pit, Naobito stood motionless, processing the announcement.
Yurei (inside his mind, purring with dark amusement): “They’re all going to fight just for the privilege of losing to us. How flattering. I almost feel bad for them… almost.”
Naobito exhaled slowly, violet energy settling around him like a second skin. He looked up toward his mother, then across the arena at the roaring crowd.
Naobito Yagami (voice low, but amplified by Corruption Mana so the entire stadium could hear): “Then let them come. I’ll be waiting in the finals.”
Hana Yagami’s smile deepened — proud, terrifying, and full of ancient affection.
Hana Yagami: “Good. Prove to them why I chose you.”
The arena lights flared once more as the next qualification round fighters were announced. The Blood Tournament had officially entered its most savage phase.
Everyone else was now fighting for the right to challenge the final boss.
And the final boss was already standing in the center of the arena, wings folded, eyes glowing violet, ready for whatever came next.
Hana Yagami stood at the edge of the shattered pit, cyan mana swirling gently around her like living threads of fate.
Without warning, she snapped her fingers.
Snap.
Naobito vanished from the center of the arena in a smooth, elegant Fold — no violence, no meteor impact, just a clean stitch of space.
He reappeared directly in the center of the Weavers’ VIP box, standing among the Twelve True Weavers themselves.
The entire box fell into stunned silence.
Elara Vane’s silver rings froze mid-click. Master Kael Voss’s colorless eyes widened a fraction. Viktor Drago’s chains rattled once in shock. Asha Nkosi’s wild grin faltered into open surprise.
They had not expected this.
Before anyone could speak, Lucien Moreau — who had been calmly observing from his throne — abruptly shifted.
His usual ancient, knowing smirk disappeared.
For the first time any of them could remember, Lucien’s expression softened completely. The perpetual weight of centuries seemed to lift from his shoulders. His amber eyes filled with something gentle, almost paternal, as he looked at Naobito.
He stood slowly and gave the boy the softest, warmest smile any of the Weavers had ever seen on his face.
Lucien Moreau: “Welcome, Naobito. I’m glad you’re here.”
The silence in the box became suffocating.
High Weaver Selene Arkwright stared at Lucien, visibly stunned. Her hand tightened on the arm of her throne.
Master Kael Voss (quiet, almost disbelieving): “…Lucien?”
Elara Vane leaned forward, her violet-gray eyes narrowing in sharp surprise. She had never seen the Weaver of Time look at anyone with such open, unguarded affection.
Lady Elara Vane: “What… is this? Lucien, you’ve never —”
Asha Nkosi blinked rapidly, her primal energy flickering in confusion. She tilted her head like a confused predator.
Amara Khalil (Weaver of Souls, soft and emotional): “Lucien… your heart is glowing. I’ve never felt you this warm toward anyone before.”
Viktor Drago grunted, chains clinking as he shifted uncomfortably, clearly unsettled by the unfamiliar gentleness on Lucien’s face.
Freya Haldorsdottir’s lightning crackled erratically in her eyes as she stared.
Dr. Elias Thorn’s fingers twitched like scalpels, analytical but visibly thrown off.
Isabella Cortez whistled low, flames dancing across her fingertips. Isabella: “Well I’ll be damned… the Time Weaver actually has a soft spot.”
Mei Ling Zhao remained silent, but her closed eyes opened slightly, a rare flicker of surprise crossing her serene features.
Omar al-Sayed stroked his beard slowly, watching Lucien with deep curiosity.
Soren Blackwood’s shadows rippled uneasily around him.
Lucien ignored them all. His gentle smile remained fixed on Naobito, warm and sincere.
Lucien Moreau: “You’ve come far, little tinkerer. Further than even I expected in some threads.”
Naobito stood among the Twelve True Weavers, feeling the weight of their collective shock and Lucien’s unexpected warmth.
Yurei (quietly in his mind, intrigued): “Well… this is new.”
The Mistress had just placed her son directly in the heart of the Weavers’ inner circle.
And for the first time, the Twelve were seeing a side of Lucien Moreau none of them had ever witnessed before.
Naobito blinked, the residual violet energy of the arena still crackling faintly around his knuckles. He looked at the stunned faces of the most powerful beings in the multiverse, and then back to Lucien’s warm, amber eyes.
He slowly lowered his guard, the ethereal wings vanishing completely.
Naobito Yagami (deadpan, looking around the suffocatingly silent room): “I was expecting to get executed, not adopted.”
Isabella Cortez choked on a laugh, quickly turning it into a cough as smoke billowed from her lips. Elara Vane shot her a withering glare.
Yurei (laughing darkly in his mind): “Take the adoption, little lamb. The benefits package looks phenomenal.”
Lucien simply chuckled, the sound rich and echoing with centuries of amusement, completely ignoring the bewildered stares of his peers.
Lucien Moreau: “We can discuss the adoption papers later. For now, you need a seat. The real show is about to begin.”
A throne of polished obsidian and silver thread smoothly materialized beside Lucien’s own. Naobito hesitated for half a second, then sat down.
The silence in the Weavers’ box became almost unbearable.
High Weaver Selene Arkwright stared at Lucien with visible disbelief, her hand tightening on the arm of her throne until her knuckles paled.
Master Kael Voss (quiet, almost disbelieving): “Lucien… this is highly irregular.”
Elara Vane leaned forward sharply, violet-gray eyes narrowing with intense curiosity and a hint of unease.
Lady Elara Vane: “You’ve never looked at anyone like that. Not in all the centuries I’ve known you.”
Amara Khalil (Weaver of Souls, soft and emotional): “Your heart… it’s glowing, Lucien. I’ve never felt you this warm toward anyone before. It’s almost… paternal.”
Viktor Drago grunted loudly, chains rattling aggressively as he shifted in his seat, clearly unsettled.
Viktor Drago: “This is madness. The boy should be in chains, not sitting among us like an equal!”
Asha Nkosi tilted her head like a curious predator, a wild grin slowly spreading across her face, though her eyes showed genuine surprise.
Freya Haldorsdottir’s lightning crackled erratically around her braids as she stared, speechless for once.
Dr. Elias Thorn’s fingers twitched violently, analytical hunger mixed with visible confusion.
Isabella Cortez (still recovering from her laugh, grinning): “Damn… the Time Weaver has a favorite. Never thought I’d see the day.”
Mei Ling Zhao remained silent, but her closed eyes opened slightly, a rare flicker of surprise crossing her serene features as she studied Lucien.
Omar al-Sayed stroked his beard slowly, watching with deep, quiet curiosity.
Soren Blackwood’s shadows rippled uneasily around him, the darkness shifting as if uncertain.
Lucien ignored them all. His gentle, warm smile remained fixed on Naobito, soft and sincere in a way none of the Eleven had ever witnessed from him.
Lucien Moreau: “You’ve come far, little tinkerer. Further than even I expected in some threads.”
Naobito sat among the Twelve True Weavers, feeling the weight of their collective shock and Lucien’s unexpected warmth. For the first time since stepping into this world, he didn’t feel like a threat to be judged.
He felt seen.
Yurei (whispering with dark amusement in his mind): “They’re all looking at you like you just grew a second head. I’m enjoying this far too much.”
The Blood Tournament raged on below them, but up here, in the heart of the Weavers’ inner circle, the balance had quietly, irreversibly shifted.
The holographic displays spanning the VIP box flared to life, casting harsh, flickering light across the Weavers’ faces. Down in the pit, Veil Wardens had rapidly erected a temporary, reinforced fighting surface over the obsidian crater Naobito and Isolde had left behind.
The first qualification match of the newly modified bracket was about to begin.
Naobito leaned forward in his new throne. He didn't look at Elara Vane, who was still staring at him like a mathematical equation that defied logic, nor at Viktor Drago, who was practically vibrating with insulted pride. He ignored the suffocating tension in the room completely.
His violet eyes locked onto the projection of the fighters stepping into the arena.
Naobito Yagami (keeping his voice low, addressing Lucien without turning his head): "You said I came further than you expected in some threads. That means you've seen the ones where I fail."
Lucien steepled his fingers. The soft, fatherly smile faded into something more ancient, burdened by the weight of infinite possibilities.
Lucien Moreau: "I have seen timelines where the Corruption consumes you. I have seen timelines where you shatter the arena, only to be executed by the Wardens before your mother could intervene. And I have seen timelines where you find the other Keys... and they kill you."
Elara Vane finally snapped, entirely unable to tolerate being kept out of the loop.
Lady Elara Vane (voice cutting like ice): "Keys? Lucien, you are overstepping. If there is a cosmic variable in play, the Council of Weavers is entitled to the data."
Lucien didn't even look at her. He merely raised a single finger. The ambient chronal energy in the VIP box thickened instantly, freezing Elara’s spinning silver rings in mid-air. It was a gentle, absolute reminder of his domain—and a polite warning to shut up.
Lucien Moreau (voice perfectly calm): "The Council watches the tournament, Elara. That is your current function. So, watch."
He gestured gracefully toward the holograms. Down in the pit, the VeilNet announcer roared the names of the first two combatants. One was a towering, heavily armored Goliath from the Outer Rim, swinging a massive gravity-hammer that warped the air around it.
But Naobito wasn't looking at him.
His eyes snapped to the second fighter. It was a slender, ragged-looking girl wearing scavenged, mismatched tech-gear. She looked completely terrified, physically shivering as the bloodthirsty crowd screamed for her death.
But to Naobito’s newly bridged internal architecture, she didn't look terrified at all.
Her mana wasn't reacting like a normal fighter's. It was entirely suppressed by a fractured, geometric node hovering just beneath the surface of her soul. A broken circuit.
Yurei (her voice instantly dropping its playful tone, turning razor-sharp and hungry): "There. Second quadrant of her chest. Do you see it, little craftsman?"
Naobito’s eyes narrowed, his knuckles whitening as he gripped the silver threads of his throne.
Naobito Yagami: "A broken logic board. Just like mine."
Lucien Moreau (leaning in slightly, his voice dropping to a whisper only Naobito could hear): "Exactly. Your mother is a brilliant architect, but she leaves a mess when she builds. That girl doesn't know she holds a piece of the puzzle. She doesn't even know what she is. Watch how she fights. You are going to need her."
High above the arena floor, across the stadium in the darkest luxury suite, Niro Hart slowly lowered his opera glasses. A vicious, utterly thrilled grin split his face as he traced Naobito’s line of sight straight down to the shivering girl in the pit.
Niro Hart (chuckling, pouring himself another glass of wine): "Oh, look at that... the final boss has found a side quest. Mireille, be a dear and pull her file. I want to know exactly what our boy is looking at."
Down in the pit, the Goliath roared and brought his hammer down with devastating force. The girl barely dodged, rolling desperately across the cracked stone. She was clearly outmatched in raw power… yet something about the way she moved felt familiar. Survival instinct honed by years of being overlooked.
Yurei (voice sharp and focused in his mind): “She’s like you were. Broken on purpose. The Mistress left her unfinished… just like she left you.”
Back in the Weavers’ box, the tension was thick enough to cut.
Elara Vane was still visibly seething from being silenced earlier, her silver rings spinning rapidly on her fingers. Master Kael Voss watched the fight with cold neutrality, but his jaw was clenched. Asha Nkosi grinned like a beast scenting blood, clearly enjoying the chaos. Viktor Drago muttered curses under his breath, chains rattling restlessly.
Naobito remained silent, eyes never leaving the girl in the pit as she narrowly avoided another crushing blow.
Naobito Yagami (quietly, almost to himself): “She’s not just fighting to survive. She’s fighting like someone who was never given a choice… just like me.”
Lucien’s gentle smile returned, soft and knowing.
Lucien Moreau: “Exactly. The second key has awakened. Whether she knows it or not.”
The Goliath’s hammer came down again. This time, the girl barely rolled out of the way, scraping her arm badly on the jagged stone. Blood stained the obsidian.
Naobito’s expression darkened.
The qualification rounds had just become far more personal.
The tension in the Weavers’ VIP box was suffocating.
Down in the pit, the ragged girl in scavenged tech-gear was barely surviving. The Goliath’s gravity hammer came down again, cracking the reinforced floor as she rolled desperately out of the way, blood trailing from a fresh gash on her arm.
Lucien Moreau leaned forward slightly in his throne, his ancient amber eyes fixed on the holographic feed. His voice was low, calm, but carried the weight of infinite timelines.
Lucien Moreau: “The Key of Duality is sitting in the most secure, elite box in the multiverse… but the girl holding the second Key is about to be crushed to death by a Goliath.”
He gestured toward the screen, where the terrified young woman barely dodged another devastating swing.
Lucien Moreau: “If she dies, a piece of the puzzle is lost forever. That’s the dilemma.”
Naobito’s head snapped toward Lucien, violet energy flaring around him.
Naobito Yagami: “Who is she?”
Lucien’s expression remained gentle, but his eyes were heavy with the burden of foresight.
Lucien Moreau: “Her name is Sylvara ‘Syl’ Kane. She comes from the Fractured Realms — a collapsed dimension where reality itself was torn apart centuries ago. She’s a scavenger, a survivor who grew up picking through the bones of dead worlds, repairing broken mana artifacts just to stay alive. She doesn’t know what she carries. She doesn’t even know she’s a Key. But the Mistress designed her as the Key of Echoes — the counterpart to your Key of Duality. Without her, the remaining keys cannot align.”
In the box, several Weavers reacted sharply.
Elara Vane (voice sharp): “Another Key? Lucien, you’ve been sitting on this—”
Viktor Drago growled, chains rattling. Viktor: “Then why are we watching her die like a rat in a pit?!”
Amara Khalil looked genuinely pained, her gentle soul-sensing picking up the girl’s fear.
Asha Nkosi grinned, but there was a predatory edge to it now.
Lucien ignored them, eyes still on Naobito.
Lucien Moreau: “Your mother is testing whether you will act. Whether the Key of Duality will reach for the Key of Echoes… or let the puzzle remain incomplete.”
Down in the arena, Syl Kane was on her last legs. The Goliath raised his hammer for a finishing blow.
Naobito stood up from his throne without a word, violet energy surging around him.
Naobito Yagami: “Then I’m not waiting for the finals.”
Yurei (voice sharp and excited in his mind): “That’s my boy. Let’s go save our missing piece.”
The entire Weavers’ box watched in stunned silence as Naobito prepared to intervene.
The Mistress’s game had just become a rescue mission.
Naobito stood motionless in the Weavers’ VIP box, violet eyes locked onto the holographic feed of the girl fighting for her life below.
Then the realization hit him like a circuit finally completing.
Naobito (mentally, the pieces snapping together in milliseconds): “Echoes…? Echoes……!!”
The newly bridged pathways in his soul lit up. The radio stations of understanding clicked into perfect alignment. He saw it — her unknown ability, hidden just beneath the surface, waiting to be triggered.
Naobito (still inside his own mind, voice urgent and sharp): “Yurei. Tell her to scream. Make her scream. Her life depends on it.”
Yurei (instantly understanding, her voice cutting across the distance like a blade): “Sylvara Kane. SCREAM.”
Down in the blood-soaked pit, Sylvara Kane — battered, bleeding, and seconds from death — suddenly heard a voice that wasn’t her own echo inside her head.
Her eyes widened in terror and confusion.
Then she screamed.
It wasn’t a normal scream.
It was apocalyptic.
A raw, piercing, impossibly loud sonic explosion tore out of her throat — her Echo ability finally awakening under extreme duress. The sound wave was so violently loud that it visibly warped the air, shattering nearby stone and sending a deafening shockwave across the entire arena. The Goliath staggered backward as the sonic blast slammed into him like a physical hammer, cracking his armor and rupturing his eardrums.
The crowd clutched their ears. Some screamed in pain. The VeilNet cameras distorted for a split second from the sheer volume.
Sylvara kept screaming — loud, desperate, terrified, but now weaponized. The Echo mana fed on her fear and amplified it into a devastating, arena-shaking force.
Back in the VIP box, Naobito’s eyes glowed with violent brilliance.
Naobito Yagami (low, focused): “That’s it…”
Yurei (proud and hungry in his mind): “Beautiful. She doesn’t even know what she is… but she’s perfect for us.”
Lucien watched with quiet approval.
Lucien Moreau (softly, only to Naobito): “The Key of Echoes has awakened. Well done.”
The rest of the Weavers stared in stunned silence as the ragged, previously overlooked girl suddenly became a sonic nightmare in the center of the pit.
The Blood Tournament had just gained a new, deafening variable.
The scream tore through the Crimson Veil Arena like a sonic apocalypse.
Sylvara Kane’s voice — raw, terrified, and now fully awakened — shattered the air. The Echo ability exploded outward in a devastating wave. The Goliath roared in agony as the sound slammed into him, cracking his heavy armor and sending him staggering back several steps. The reinforced fighting surface beneath their feet fractured further under the pressure.
The crowd screamed, many covering their ears. Even the VIP boxes felt the vibrations.
In the Weavers’ box, several thrones rattled.
Viktor Drago cursed loudly. Isabella Cortez laughed in delight, flames flaring. Asha Nkosi grinned like a predator watching prey evolve.
Naobito’s eyes burned with violet intensity. He could feel the connection — the Key of Echoes responding to his call through Yurei’s bridge.
Naobito Yagami (low, decisive): “She’s awake. I’m going down there.”
Before anyone could stop him, he stepped forward and dropped from the VIP box. Six violet wings snapped open, carrying him in a controlled descent straight into the pit.
He landed beside Sylvara with a soft impact, wings folding neatly behind him. The girl was on her knees, gasping, blood trickling from her ears, eyes wide with shock at what she had just done.
Sylvara Kane (hoarse, terrified): “W-what… what was that…? I didn’t… I can’t…”
Naobito extended a hand toward her, violet energy gently stabilizing the air around them.
Naobito Yagami: “You’re not alone anymore. That power… it’s part of something bigger. Part of us.”
Yurei (soft, almost kind in Sylvara’s mind as well): “Breathe, little echo. We’ve got you.”
The Goliath recovered, roaring in fury as he charged again, gravity hammer raised high.
Naobito stepped in front of Sylvara protectively, six wings flaring.
Naobito Yagami: “Stay behind me. We’ll finish this together.”
The arena erupted in chaos as the destined vessel and the newly awakened Key of Echoes stood side by side for the first time.
Hana Yagami watched from the edge of the pit, her enigmatic smile deepening with quiet satisfaction.
The puzzle was beginning to align.
The Weavers’ Box – Moments Later
The holographic displays showed Naobito standing protectively in front of the ragged girl as the Goliath charged once more.
High Weaver Selene Arkwright (quietly): “He’s already moving to protect the second Key…”
Elara Vane (voice sharp with calculation): “This is dangerous. If he starts collecting the remaining Keys himself…”
Lucien Moreau simply smiled softly, watching the feed with that same gentle warmth.
Lucien Moreau: “He’s doing exactly what he was made to do.”
In his luxury box, Niro Hart lowered his glass, eyes gleaming with dark delight.
Niro Hart: “Two pieces already together… This tournament just became a family affair.”
The Blood Tournament raged on.
But the real game — the one woven by the Mistress herself — was accelerating faster than anyone had anticipated.
The Goliath’s roar still echoed in the physical world as the gravity hammer came down again.
But inside Naobito’s mind, everything was silent.
A vast, empty black space stretched endlessly. No walls. No floor. Just soft violet light glowing faintly from unseen sources, like distant stars in an endless night.
Sylvara Kane suddenly found herself standing there, no longer in the blood-soaked pit. Her scavenged tech-gear was still torn and bloody, but the pain felt distant, muted. She looked around in wide-eyed confusion, then spotted Naobito standing a few meters away — calm, six ethereal wings folded behind him, violet energy gently radiating from his form.
Sylvara Kane (voice shaky, looking around): “Where… where are we? What is this place?”
Naobito stepped closer, his expression gentle but steady.
Naobito Yagami: “We’re inside my mind. I brought you here for a second. Just to tell you… you’re going to be okay. We’re connected in some way. I don’t fully understand it yet, but I know you’re not alone anymore.”
Sylvara stared at him, her breathing still ragged from the fight. There was something deeply familiar in his eyes, in the way his presence felt — like a missing piece she had been searching for her entire life without knowing it.
Sylvara Kane (softly, almost whispering): “You feel… like family. Like I’ve known you before. Are you… my brother or something?”
Naobito gave her a small, tired smile.
Naobito Yagami: “Not biologically. But I’ll tell you everything after we defeat this dumb giant idiot out there.”
Sylvara blinked, then let out a shaky, relieved laugh — the first real sound of hope she had made in years.
Sylvara Kane: “Deal.”
The black space around them began to fade as Naobito gently guided her consciousness back to the physical world.
Yurei (warm and approving in his mind): “Good boy. She’s scared, but she trusts you already. Let’s finish this together.”
Back in the arena, Naobito’s eyes snapped open. The Goliath was charging again, hammer raised for a killing blow.
Naobito stepped forward, six wings flaring protectively in front of Sylvara as she staggered to her feet behind him.
Naobito Yagami: “Stay behind me. We end this now.”
The Key of Duality and the Key of Echoes stood together for the first time.
The fight was far from over.
The Goliath charged with a thunderous roar, gravity hammer raised high for a killing blow.
Sylvara instinctively grabbed onto Naobito’s arm to brace herself, her body still shaking from the earlier sonic outburst. The Key of Echoes and the Key of Duality stood together in the shattered pit — two broken pieces finally aligning.
Then it happened.
A new Range Crest ignited beneath their feet — not violet, not cyan, but a perfect fusion of both. The pattern was breathtaking: six wings interwoven with echoing sonic spirals, six eyes glowing at the center of each wing, surrounded by flowing geometric threads that pulsed with combined Corruption and Echo mana. It was something entirely new — a crest born from their synced bond, not from either of them alone.
The crest flared to life with overwhelming power.
BOOM.
A cataclysmic wave of violet-cyan energy erupted outward. The Goliath didn’t even have time to scream. The fused Range Crest caught him mid-charge and launched him skyward with impossible force. He shot through the arena’s reinforced ceiling like a red comet, shattering barriers and vanishing into the upper atmosphere — then further.
Several light-years away.
If he was smart, he wouldn’t come back.
The arena fell into stunned silence for a heartbeat.
Then the crowd erupted in the loudest roar of the night.
Naobito stood tall in the center of the pit, breathing steadily, six wings slowly folding away. Sylvara still held onto his arm, eyes wide with shock and awe as she looked at the glowing remnants of their combined Range Crest fading beneath them.
Sylvara Kane (whispering, voice trembling): “What… what was that? We did that… together?”
Naobito Yagami (calm, but with a small, tired smile): “Yeah. We did.”
Yurei (laughing softly in his mind, proud and thrilled): “Beautiful. Our first real creation. The Goliath won’t be coming back anytime soon. Smart boy.”
In the Weavers’ VIP box, the Twelve True Weavers were on their feet.
Elara Vane (eyes wide): “A fused Range Crest… they created something new together.”
Asha Nkosi laughed wildly, slamming her fist on her throne. Asha: “Now that’s a fight worth watching!”
Master Kael Voss remained silent, but his expression was grave.
High above in his luxury box, Niro Hart lowered his glass, his demonic smirk deepening with genuine fascination.
Niro Hart: “Two Keys working as one… and they just sent a demon lord into deep space. This night keeps getting better.”
Hana Yagami stood at the edge of the pit, cyan mana swirling around her. Her smile was soft, proud, and full of ancient satisfaction.
Hana Yagami: “Well done, my son. The puzzle is beginning to solve itself.”
Naobito looked up toward his mother, then down at Sylvara, who was still holding his arm, breathing hard but alive.
The Blood Tournament had just witnessed its first true miracle.
And the remaining Keys were waiting to be found.
Kenshi Kashimo – Watching the Blood Tournament (Neo-Haven, Nevada)
In a dimly lit apartment on the edge of Neo-Haven, a silver-faux-hawk caught the glow of a cracked laptop screen.
Kenshi Kashimo (20) lounged on a beat-up couch in his default blue hoodie and black jeans, one leg slung over the armrest, a half-eaten cup of instant ramen forgotten on the coffee table. His blue eyes were glued to the illegal VeilNet stream playing on the laptop.
The Blood Tournament.
He had tuned in out of boredom — underground mana fights were usually good for a laugh or a quick bet. But the moment the camera panned to the violet comet crashing into the pit, his smirk froze.
Kenshi (leaning forward slowly, voice low): “No fucking way…”
The boy who landed in the center of the shattered arena — six violet wings, calm eyes, radiating power like a walking apocalypse — was unmistakably Naobito Yagami.
His childhood friend. The quiet kid he used to share broken puzzles and cheap snacks with before everything went to hell.
Kenshi’s usual smug grin slowly returned, but this time it was sharper, mixed with genuine disbelief and a spark of excitement.
Kenshi: “You crazy bastard… you actually became something.”
On the screen, Naobito dismantled a demon lord with cold efficiency. The crowd roared. Kenshi laughed once — short, sharp, and proud.
Kenshi: “Look at you, man. From getting your ass kicked behind the school to dropping meteors on demon lords. I always knew you had it in you… just didn’t think you’d go full god-mode.”
He leaned back, running a hand through his silver faux-hawk, blue eyes narrowing as the camera caught a glimpse of Yurei’s ethereal form briefly wrapping around Naobito.
Kenshi (muttering to himself): “And you’ve got a goddess riding shotgun now? Damn… the quiet ones really do snap the hardest.”
His expression turned more serious for a moment — the rare look he only showed when shit got real.
Kenshi: “Whatever happened to you… it better be worth it. If you need backup, you know where to find me, old friend.”
He grabbed his jacket, already reaching for his keys.
The Blood Tournament was still raging on screen.
But in a small apartment in Neo-Haven, Nevada, an old friend had just decided the quiet days were over.
Kenshi stepped out of his apartment, the heavy metal door clicking shut behind him. The neon glow of Neo-Haven buzzed through the humid Nevada night. He tossed his keys in the air, catching them cleanly, his mind already racing through his underground contacts.
He didn’t have a spaceship. He didn’t have a VIP pass to the Weavers’ private dimension.
But Neo-Haven was a notoriously dirty city. The Veil here was thin, and where the Veil was thin, smugglers built Gates.
Kenshi Kashimo (dialing a burner phone, slipping on a pair of aviators despite the dark): “Hey, darling. It’s Kenshi. Yeah, I know it’s late. Listen, I need to buy a ticket on the next transit-smuggling run out of the desert. Where to?”
He smirked, glancing back up at the starless sky.
Kenshi Kashimo: “The Crimson Veil Arena. Yeah, I’m serious, babe. Charge it to my tab. My best friend just picked a fight with the multiverse, and I need to go make sure he doesn’t do anything stupid without me.”
The line went quiet for a second, then a gruff voice answered with a sigh.
Smuggler Contact: “You’re insane, Kashimo. The VeilNet feed is blowing up. That kid in the pit… is that really your old buddy from the orphanage days?”
Kenshi (smirking wider as he started walking toward the desert edge of the city): “The one and only. Quiet kid back then. Now he’s dropping meteors and making gods nervous. I owe him one. So get me on that run.”
He hung up, slipping the burner phone into his pocket as he disappeared into the neon-lit alleys of Neo-Haven.
Somewhere in the dark Nevada desert, a hidden smuggling Gate was already humming to life.
Kenshi Kashimo was heading to the Blood Tournament.
And the multiverse was about to get a lot louder.
The VeilNet feed flickered for a brief second as the broadcast cut to a new camera angle.
The incubus anchor’s charming grin suddenly froze mid-sentence. A swirl of deep crimson and alluring pink mana enveloped him, and in a smooth, seductive shift, his form melted away.
When the feed stabilized, a stunning succubus now stood in his place — tall, curvaceous, with long flowing obsidian hair, glowing rose-gold eyes, and horns that curved elegantly like a crown. Her voice was velvet wrapped around sin as she took over the broadcast without missing a beat.
Succubus Anchor (Sultry, playful, and dangerously charismatic): “Mmm~ Did you miss me, darlings? Your favorite incubus needed a little… break. So let’s keep this party going, shall we?”
She winked at the camera, her tail flicking playfully as the arena lights pulsed in sync with her energy.
Succubus Anchor: “For those just tuning in — the Blood Tournament is heating up! The destined vessel and his Dark Maiden have just made their entrance, and the Weavers are looking… tense. Meanwhile, in the pit, our first Symbiote-Host match is about to ignite!”
The crowd roared again as the camera panned back to the shattered arena floor.
In the VIP box, Niro Hart chuckled softly, raising his glass toward the screen.
Niro Hart: “Now that’s a much better view.”
Mireille’s eyes narrowed with jealous possessiveness, but she said nothing.
High above in the Weavers’ box, several heads turned toward the broadcast with varying levels of annoyance or amusement.
The Blood Tournament continued — now with a far more… captivating narrator.
The under-arena corridors were cool and damp, a stark contrast to the roaring chaos above.
Naobito gently nudged Sylvara forward, guiding her out of the blinding arena lights and into the shadows. The heavy steel gates slammed shut behind them, cutting off the deafening roar of the crowd. The adrenaline finally began to recede.
He flexed his right hand, the last few sparks of cyan and violet mana dancing across his knuckles. To his analytical mind, the fused Range Crest hadn’t been some divine miracle — it felt exactly like completing a complex schematic. Their souls had acted like two distinct power distribution systems, and the moment they aligned, the current bridged flawlessly.
Sylvara leaned against the cold stone wall, sliding down until she was sitting on the floor, burying her face in her hands.
Sylvara Kane (muffled, laughing hysterically): “I’m alive. We actually survived that. I think I’m going to throw up, but I’m alive.”
Footsteps echoed softly in the damp tunnel.
Reno (20) stood a few feet away, arms crossed casually over his dark combat gear. His face was a completely unreadable, emotionless mask. He didn’t waste movement, and he certainly didn’t waste words.
Reno (flatly, stating a fact): “Bracket is broken.”
Behind him, Saya Kurogane hurried forward. Her hands were completely empty — no katana, no concealed daggers. Despite wearing the traditional combat attire forced upon her by the Kurogane Clan and Master Kael, she looked like she wanted to be anywhere else in the multiverse.
Her dark eyes darted frantically over Naobito, scanning him for injuries before she finally let out a soft, shaky exhale. A faint blush dusted her cheeks as she stepped closer, her empathetic nature completely overriding any clan protocol.
Saya Kurogane (voice soft, thick with relief): “Naobito-san… you’re not hurt? When you dropped into the pit, I thought my heart was going to stop.”
She knelt gracefully next to Sylvara, offering the trembling girl a small, warm smile and a steadying hand on her shoulder.
Saya (looking up at Naobito, biting her lip): “The Elders… and Master Kael. They are in an uproar. No one knows what to do with you now. But I am just glad you are safe.”
Before Naobito could respond to her quiet affection, a rusted wall-monitor above them flickered to life.
Succubus Anchor (through the static-laced speaker, voice dripping with playful sin): “And now, darlings, prepare yourselves! Our next combatants are stepping into the pit. Let’s see how a standard bond holds up in our third Symbiote-Host Deathmatch!”
Reno didn’t blink. He just stared at the monitor, his voice maintaining that same deadpan, chilling calm.
Reno: “That’s my bracket.”
He turned his head slightly, locking his cold eyes on Naobito.
Reno: “Watch the one on the left. He doesn’t fight. His symbiote just eats.”
Naobito nodded once, the weight of everything settling on his shoulders. The tournament was moving fast, and the real threats were only just beginning to step into the light.
Yurei (soft, almost protective in his mind): “Let them come, little lamb. We’ll be ready.”
The corridor fell quiet again, the distant roar of the arena a constant reminder that the Blood Tournament was far from over.
Crimson Veil Arena – General Stands
Kenshi Kashimo slipped into the roaring crowd like a shadow in a blue hoodie.
He had his hood pulled low, silver faux-hawk hidden, face mostly obscured. No one gave the unassuming young man in black jeans a second glance. He found an empty seat next to a family of non-human creatures — a burly orc father, a gentle orc mother, and their wide-eyed teenage daughter — who looked completely out of their depth in the sea of mana users.
Down in the pit, Reno was already dodging the frantic, snapping jaws of his opponent’s ravenous symbiote. The sheer force of the monster’s misses cracked the arena floor. The orc mother flinched, pulling her daughter closer.
Kenshi plopped down casually, flashing them a quick, smug grin.
Kenshi Kashimo: “First time at one of these bloodbaths, huh? Don’t worry, I’ll give you the quick version so you don’t get lost.”
The orc father grunted, eyeing him suspiciously, but didn't push him away.
Kenshi (leaning forward, resting his forearms on his knees): “Alright, basics. Look at the kid in the black gear down there. He’s surviving because of how he manages his Seams — the threads of his power. He’s got low reserves, but insane output. He burns hot and fast.”
He gestured vaguely to the crowd around them.
Kenshi: “Normal people like you and me? We run on what the scholars call Base-Thread. But back home on Earth, we just call it Nature Mana. Green stuff. Low reserves, low output. But fighters like him? They’re running on a whole different engine.”
The teenage orc girl peeked out from her mother’s arm, her fear momentarily overridden by curiosity.
Orc Girl: “What about the monster? How does he fight that?”
Kenshi (smirking, pointing a finger at the pit): “With his Innate Weave. Though honestly, everyone from the street rats on Earth to the Weavers sitting up in those VIP boxes just calls it a gimmick. It’s your one neat trick. Some guys have crazy reserves but no gimmick. Others have a broken gimmick but run out of gas in two minutes.”
Right on cue, the ravenous symbiote slammed a heavy, mutated fist into the barrier wall near their section. A chunk of reinforced stone shattered, sending a jagged piece of shrapnel spinning directly toward the orc family.
The father shouted, trying to shield his wife.
Kenshi didn't even stand up. He casually reached out a hand.
Smack.
He caught the stone bare-handed. A sharp, brilliant flash of blue mana rippled up his arm, instantly absorbing the kinetic force of the impact and converting it into a soft, harmless glow before fading away. He tossed the dead rock over his shoulder like a piece of trash.
The orc family stared at him in stunned silence.
Orc Girl (whispering): “What’s your gimmick?”
Kenshi’s smirk widened, his blue eyes glinting under the hood. He didn't explain the kinetic conversion, nor did he mention his rare blue mana.
Kenshi: “Me? Most fighters treat their mana like a gas tank. They store it, they burn it. I don’t do storage. I just do speed.”
He nodded toward the cratered center of the pit where the Goliath had been launched into orbit just minutes prior.
Kenshi: “That guy from the last match? The one with the violet wings who just punched a demon lord into deep space? Naobito. We grew up in the same orphanage back on Earth. Back then, he couldn't even throw a proper punch. Now he’s apparently got some crazy demon riding shotgun.”
The orc father stared at Kenshi, re-evaluating the "normal" kid in the blue hoodie.
Orc Father (grumbling, a mix of respect and unease): “Sounds like your friend is a walking apocalypse.”
Kenshi (laughing softly, leaning back into his seat): “Oh, he is. But he’s my apocalypse. I’m just here to make sure he doesn’t do anything too stupid without backup.”
He crossed his arms behind his head, watching Reno's match with that same smug, relaxed energy. The Blood Tournament raged on. And in the stands, an old friend waited, ready to jump the barrier the second his buddy needed him.
Crimson Veil Arena – The Pit
Reno moved like a loaded gun that had already decided the outcome.
His opponent was a bonded pair — a hulking, red-skinned demon symbiote fused with a wiry human host who controlled it through sheer sadistic will. The demon’s claws were extended, dripping corrosive Malevolence Mana, while the human rider laughed maniacally from its shoulders.
The symbiote lunged, claws slashing in a frenzy.
Reno didn’t dodge dramatically. He simply stepped inside the arc, caught the demon’s wrist with one hand, and twisted. The snap of bone was audible even over the roaring crowd. The human rider screamed in shared pain.
Reno’s other hand drew one of his six-shooters in a smooth, practiced motion. He pressed the barrel against the demon’s chest and pulled the trigger.
Bang.
A single obsidian round — packed with dense Neutralization Mana — punched straight through the symbiote’s core. The red Malevolence Mana destabilized instantly, collapsing inward like a dying star. The demon howled as its body began to disintegrate from the inside out.
The human host tried to pull away, eyes wide with terror, but Reno was already moving. He grabbed the man by the throat with his free hand, lifted him clean off the ground, and slammed him into the cracked obsidian floor.
The second shot was point-blank to the chest.
Bang.
The human’s scream cut off instantly as the Neutralization round erased his mana signature completely. The body convulsed once, then went still — the symbiote’s remains crumbling into red dust around the corpse.
Reno holstered his six-shooter without a word.
The entire fight had lasted less than twenty seconds.
The arena fell into a stunned hush for a heartbeat… then erupted in a deafening roar.
VeilNet Anchor (the succubus, voice dripping with excitement): “And that, darlings, is how you end a match! Reno — cold, efficient, and absolutely merciless. The symbiote and its host are both gone. Clean kill!”
In the Weavers’ VIP box, Elara Vane allowed herself a small, satisfied smile.
Elara: “Good boy.”
Reno didn’t acknowledge the crowd. He simply turned and walked toward the exit tunnel, blood and red dust on his boots, face completely emotionless.
He had done his job.
And somewhere in the stands, Kenshi Kashimo watched with a sharp, knowing smirk.
The Blood Tournament continued.
But the message was clear: the pawns of the Twelve were not to be underestimated.
The under-arena corridor was quiet except for the distant roar of the crowd above and the soft, shaky breathing of Sylvara Kane.
Saya Kurogane knelt on the cold stone floor beside her, one hand gently resting on the girl’s shoulder, the other offering a clean cloth for the blood still trailing from her ears. Sylvara was still trembling, her scavenged gear torn and stained, eyes wide from the shock of her own awakening.
Saya (softly, voice thick with concern): “You’re safe now. Just breathe. You’re not alone.”
The heavy steel gate at the far end of the corridor slid open with a metallic grind.
Reno walked in.
His boots left faint red dust and dark blood tracks on the floor with every step. His face remained a perfect blank mask — no pride, no satisfaction, no emotion. He stopped a few meters away, calmly raised one of his six-shooters, and flicked the cylinder open with a practiced thumb.
Click. Click. Click.
He ejected the spent shell casings one by one. They clattered onto the stone floor like empty judgments. Saya flinched at the sound, her eyes darting away in quiet horror at the casual way he handled the aftermath of the kill.
Reno didn’t look at her. He simply reloaded with the same mechanical precision, then turned his cold gray eyes toward Naobito.
Reno (flat, pragmatic, like stating the weather): “You broke the bracket, Yagami.”
He holstered the gun with a soft snap.
Reno: “To the crowd, you’re a god. But to the Weavers up in those boxes? You’re a glitch. And they always patch glitches.”
The corridor fell into a heavy silence.
Saya looked up at Reno, her expression a mixture of quiet anger and sadness.
Saya: “Is that all you see him as? A problem to be fixed?”
Reno didn’t answer her. He simply adjusted the strap of his combat gear and leaned against the wall, already watching the tunnel exit as if expecting the next threat to walk through it at any second.
Yurei (quiet and sharp in Naobito’s mind): “He’s not wrong, little lamb. The Weavers don’t like anomalies they can’t control. And right now… we are the biggest anomaly in their perfect weave.”
Naobito stood between them — between Saya’s gentle compassion and Reno’s cold pragmatism — the weight of both perspectives settling on his shoulders.
Sylvara looked up from the floor, still pale, but her eyes were clearer now as she watched the three of them.
The Blood Tournament continued to rage above them.
But down here, in the damp tunnels, the real battle lines were already being drawn.
The corridor remained thick with tension.
Saya still knelt beside Sylvara, Reno leaned against the wall like a loaded gun, and Naobito stood between them, the weight of Reno’s warning still hanging in the air.
Then the heavy steel door at the far end of the tunnel rattled.
A Veil Warden’s armored glove slammed against it from the outside, followed by a muffled shout.
Veil Warden: “Unauthorized personnel—! Stop right—”
The door suddenly jerked once. There was a sharp crack of kinetic force being absorbed, followed by a soft metallic click.
The lock disengaged.
The door slid open.
Kenshi Kashimo strolled in like he owned the place, blue hoodie still on, silver faux-hawk slightly messy from the crowd, a smug grin already plastered across his face. He tossed a dead, sparking security badge over his shoulder as the door shut behind him.
Kenshi Kashimo (cheerful, waving casually): “Hey, Nao-chan. Long time no see.”
The entire tunnel froze.
Saya’s eyes widened. Sylvara blinked in confusion. Reno’s hand twitched toward his holster on pure instinct.
Naobito just stared.
Naobito Yagami (deadpan, after a long second): “…Kenshi?”
Kenshi grinned wider, walking right past Reno as if the cold-blooded killer wasn’t even there. He stopped in front of Naobito and gave him a light, friendly punch on the shoulder.
Kenshi: “You look like shit, man. But damn… you’ve gotten strong. Dropping demon lords into deep space? That’s new. Last time I saw you, you were still fixing broken clocks in that tiny apartment.”
He glanced at Reno’s bloodstained boots, then at Saya’s worried expression, then at the still-trembling Sylvara on the floor. His smirk didn’t fade, but his blue eyes sharpened just a fraction.
Kenshi: “So… who’s trying to kill my old friend this time? Or did you already take care of them?”
Yurei (inside Naobito’s mind, amused): “A childhood friend who breaks into restricted zones and acts like this is a family reunion. I like him already.”
Reno’s cold stare remained locked on the newcomer.
Reno (flatly): “Unauthorized entry. Security breach.”
Kenshi shot him a lazy, unimpressed look.
Kenshi: “Yeah, yeah. Tell it to the rock I caught earlier. I’m here for him.” He jerked his thumb toward Naobito. “And from the look of things, he could use someone who isn’t either trying to patch him like a glitch or treat him like a fragile glass doll.”
He turned back to Naobito, the grin softening into something more genuine for a brief second.
Kenshi: “You good, Nao?”
Naobito let out a long breath, the tension in his shoulders easing just a little.
Naobito Yagami: “…Yeah. I’m good.”
The gloomy, heavy atmosphere of the tunnel had just been cracked wide open by one loud, smug, blue-hooded chaos agent from Neo-Haven.
And for the first time since the tournament began, Naobito had an unexpected ally standing right beside him.
Weavers’ VIP Box – Security Feed
The holographic security feed flickered to life above the center of the box, showing the under-arena corridor in crisp detail.
Naobito stood between Saya and Reno. Sylvara was still sitting on the floor. And then the heavy steel door slid open.
Kenshi Kashimo strolled in, blue hoodie, silver faux-hawk slightly messy, smug grin already in place.
The feed froze for half a second as the facial recognition software tagged him.
A soft, elegant smirk curved Lady Elara Vane’s lips.
Elara (quietly, almost amused): “Kashimo. Of course.”
Lucien Moreau’s usual knowing smile deepened into something softer and more entertained. He had already seen this thread.
Isabella Cortez leaned forward with a wide, playful smirk, flames flickering briefly across her fingers.
Isabella: “Oh, I like this one. Look at that entrance.”
Across the circle, Master Kael Voss closed his eyes and slowly pinched the bridge of his nose.
Kael (under his breath): “Not him again…”
Viktor Drago let out a low growl and rubbed his temples with both hands, iron-gray chains rattling restlessly around his throne.
Viktor: “Earth freelancers. Always slipping through the cracks.”
High Weaver Selene Arkwright raised one hand and pressed her fingertips to her forehead in a restrained, elegant facepalm, her expression a mixture of resignation and calculation.
Selene: “Another variable…”
Then, unexpectedly soft, Amara Khalil — Weaver of Souls — smiled.
It wasn’t the polite smile of a Weaver. It was warm, almost maternal, the kind of smile reserved for someone she remembered fondly.
Amara (quietly, with genuine affection): “Kenshi… It’s been a long time.”
Several heads turned toward her in mild surprise.
Omar al-Sayed raised an eyebrow, stroking his beard.
Omar: “You know this boy, Amara?”
Amara didn’t look away from the feed, her smile lingering.
Amara: “I met him once, years ago, in Neo-Haven. He helped me extract a vessel without bloodshed. Beneath the swagger… there is a surprisingly kind heart.”
Mei Ling Zhao remained completely silent, eyes half-lidded, simply watching.
Asha Nkosi grinned with predatory curiosity but said nothing.
Dr. Elias Thorn leaned forward, already studying Kenshi’s mana signature with clinical interest.
Freya Haldorsdottir and Soren Blackwood stayed quiet, the former’s lightning flickering faintly, the latter’s shadows shifting with mild interest.
Elara (still smirking, glancing at the others): “For those of you who don’t know him — Kenshi Kashimo. Earth-born kinetic anomaly. Sometimes useful. Always messy. And apparently… still loyal to the quiet boy who used to fix broken clocks.”
The security feed continued, showing Kenshi casually greeting Naobito like they were still kids in an orphanage.
In the VIP box, the Twelve True Weavers watched with a mixture of amusement, irritation, affection, and calculation.
The Blood Tournament had just gained another unpredictable variable.
And this one was already standing next to their biggest problem.
Under-Arena Corridor
Kenshi Kashimo was still grinning, one hand casually resting on Naobito’s shoulder like they were back in the orphanage courtyard, when the words finally registered.
He froze.
His blue eyes went wide. The smug smirk vanished for the first time since he walked into the tunnel.
Kenshi Kashimo (voice cracking slightly higher than usual): “YOUR MOM IS WHAT?!”
The corridor fell dead silent.
Saya blinked. Sylvara looked between them in confusion. Reno’s expression didn’t change, but one eyebrow twitched almost imperceptibly.
Kenshi took a step back, pointing a finger at Naobito like the man had just claimed he was part dragon.
Kenshi: “Hold up. Hold the fuck up. You’re telling me the Mistress of Threads — the actual cosmic sewing-machine goddess who runs the entire Law of Containment — is your mom? The same quiet kid who used to cry when we broke a clock is the son of the most terrifying woman in the multiverse?!”
He ran both hands through his silver faux-hawk, pacing in a tight circle.
Kenshi: “I shared my last bag of chips with you when we were eleven. I taught you how to throw a half-decent punch. And this whole time you were a cosmic failsafe designed by the woman who literally weaves reality?!”
Naobito just stared at him, deadpan.
Naobito Yagami: “…Yes.”
Kenshi stopped pacing and stared at him for a long second.
Then he let out a short, incredulous laugh that was half disbelief and half pure chaos.
Kenshi: “Bro. You’re telling me I used to protect the secret son of a goddess from bullies. That’s the funniest shit I’ve ever heard.”
Yurei (laughing darkly in Naobito’s mind): “I like this one more and more. He’s loud, but he’s honest.”
Kenshi finally dropped his hands and looked at Naobito again, the shock slowly melting back into that familiar smug grin — though his eyes still held a spark of genuine disbelief.
Kenshi: “Alright. Fine. Your mom’s a cosmic horror. Cool. Doesn’t change anything. I’m still not letting you fight this whole tournament alone.”
He cracked his knuckles, blue kinetic mana flickering briefly around his fists.
Kenshi: “So… who’s trying to kill my best friend next?”
Kenshi’s question still hung in the damp corridor air.
As if the universe itself had been waiting for the perfect punchline, the rusted wall-monitor above them suddenly crackled to life with a sharp static burst.
The holographic feed shifted from the empty pit to a new camera angle — this time focusing on the Weavers’ VIP box.
Succubus Anchor (voice dripping with delighted excitement): “Oh, darlings… the brackets have been updated again! For the next Symbiote-Host exhibition match, the Mistress has approved a special challenge!”
The screen zoomed in on one of the thrones.
Asha Nkosi, Weaver of Beasts, stood up slowly. Her tall, muscular frame radiated primal energy. Tribal patterns glowed across her dark skin as she cracked her neck, a wide, predatory grin spreading across her face. She looked directly into the camera — and somehow, it felt like she was looking straight through the screen at Naobito.
Succubus Anchor: “The Weaver of Beasts herself has requested the honor! Asha Nkosi will step into the pit against Naobito Yagami and his Dark Maiden!”
In the corridor, the reaction was immediate.
Saya’s face went pale. Sylvara’s eyes widened in pure fear. Even Reno’s expression tightened by a fraction.
Kenshi just stared at the screen, mouth slightly open.
Kenshi: “…You’ve gotta be kidding me.”
On the feed, Asha Nkosi’s voice came through loud and clear, filled with pure, bloodthirsty joy.
Asha Nkosi: “I don’t want to kill you, little vessel. I want to fight you. No holding back. No mediocre performance. Show me the full strength of the Key of Duality… or I’ll make you regret ever stepping into my world.”
She slammed one fist into her open palm, the impact sending a visible shockwave of green-and-gold Beast mana through the VIP box.
Asha Nkosi (grinning wider): “I’ll be waiting in the pit. Don’t keep me waiting.”
The feed cut back to the empty arena.
In the corridor, the silence was deafening.
Yurei (inside Naobito’s mind, voice equal parts amused and excited): “The Beast Weaver herself wants a real fight… This is going to be delicious.”
Naobito exhaled slowly, violet energy flickering around his knuckles.
Naobito Yagami: “Looks like we just got our answer.”
Kenshi ran a hand down his face, then let out a short, almost manic laugh.
Kenshi: “Your mom’s a goddess, your dad’s immortal, and now the Beast Weaver wants to throw hands for fun. Your life is insane, Nao.”
He cracked his neck, blue kinetic mana already sparking faintly around his fists.
Kenshi: “Alright. Fine. Let’s go make sure she doesn’t eat you.”
The Walkout
The heavy steel gates of the gladiator tunnel groaned open.
A line of heavily armored Veil Wardens immediately crossed their mana-forged spears, blocking the exit with a wall of glowing energy.
Veil Warden Captain: “Halt! This is a sanctioned 1v1 match. Unauthorized fighters will be—”
Kenshi Kashimo didn’t slow down.
He walked straight into the crossed spears. The moment the weapons struck his chest, a sharp blue flash of kinetic mana erupted. The force of the impact vanished into his body like it had never existed. The spears slid harmlessly off him as he continued forward with a smug, unbothered grin.
Kenshi: “Sorry, officers. My best friend’s got a date with a goddess. I’m just the plus-one.”
Naobito followed in silence, six ethereal wings flickering once behind him before settling. The Wardens staggered aside, unwilling to press the issue against the boy who had already launched a demon lord into deep space.
The two of them stepped out into the blinding lights of the Crimson Veil Arena.
The crowd exploded.
The VIP Box Meltdown
The moment Kenshi’s boots hit the arena floor alongside Naobito, the Weavers’ VIP box erupted.
Viktor Drago slammed both fists down on the arms of his throne so hard that several iron chains shattered and whipped through the air.
Viktor Drago (roaring): “Earth vermin! Deploy the suppression chains! Drag that freeloading rat out of my arena!”
Beside him, Master Kael Voss was rigid with barely contained fury, silver Neutralization Mana flickering violently around his hands.
Kael Voss: “This is a complete degradation of the Sacred Bracket. First the vessel breaks the order… now this chaotic Earth anomaly walks in like the rules mean nothing!”
Soren Blackwood’s shadows shifted with quiet amusement.
Soren (dryly): “The rules do appear to be unraveling rather quickly tonight.”
Lucien Moreau simply smiled, steepling his fingers.
Lucien: “How entertaining.”
Asha’s Veto
Before Viktor could fully authorize the arena’s automated defenses, a booming, primal voice cut across the entire stadium — amplified by pure Beast mana and overriding every other sound on the VeilNet feed.
Asha Nkosi stood in the center of the pit, arms crossed, a wild, thrilled grin on her face. Green-and-gold energy already coiled around her muscular frame like living animals waiting to be unleashed.
Asha Nkosi: “Enough.”
The VIP box fell silent.
She looked straight up at the Weavers, then at the two young men standing at the edge of the pit.
Asha Nkosi: “I invoke my authority as a True Weaver. The match parameters are amended. This is no longer a 1v1.”
She spread her arms wide, the arena floor trembling under her feet.
Asha Nkosi: “I accept the challenge of both of them. The Key of Duality and his little kinetic friend. If they want to fight together… then I will no longer hold back.”
Her grin widened into something almost feral.
Asha Nkosi: “Come, little vessel. Come, loud Earth boy. Show me everything you have. I want a fight worthy of the Beast Weaver.”
The crowd lost its collective mind.
In the tunnel entrance, Saya covered her mouth in pure shock. Sylvara’s eyes were huge. Reno simply watched, expression unreadable.
Naobito and Kenshi stood side by side under the arena lights as Asha Nkosi’s primal mana began to rise like a gathering storm.
The real fight was about to begin.
Crimson Veil Arena – The Pit
The arena floor shuddered under the sheer weight of Asha Nkosi’s power. Green and gold Beast mana flared around the Weaver like a localized hurricane, cracking the obsidian tiles beneath her boots.
Naobito stood perfectly still in the eye of the storm, his violet energy calm, analytical, and completely dwarfed by the goddess's raw output.
Yurei’s voice echoed in his mind, sharp and hungry. “She’s going to blitz us, Naobito. Her physical stats are off the charts. Let me flood your muscles with Dark Mana—”
“No,” Naobito thought back, his inner voice unnervingly calm. “You take the wheel.”
“What? Naobito, a vessel can’t just—”
Naobito raised one hand, two fingers pressed together in a loose seal, and spoke a single word aloud.
Naobito: “Kōtai.” (Shift).
Instantly, thick, pitch-black bandages and writhing, ink-like tentacles erupted from his collarbone, spine, and chest. They spiraled violently outward, weaving over his skin at blinding speed, encasing him in a dense, pulsing cocoon of dark matter. The raw physical mass of the cocoon shifted, snapping and reforming bone structure, height, and silhouette in a matter of milliseconds.
Then, the bandages unraveled into dark mist.
Where the quiet, analytical boy had just stood, Yurei Himura stood in the flesh for the first time in centuries.
She slowly clenched her right hand into a fist. The muscles responded instantly, but something felt wrong. The strength was hers — dark, corrosive, and vast — yet the architecture underneath felt synthetic. The tendons, the bone density, the way the joints locked… none of it belonged to her original form. It was Naobito’s structure, rebuilt and tailored around her essence.
She pressed two fingers to her chest.
No heartbeat.
Only a steady, artificial pulse of Corruption Mana holding the shell together.
A strange, claustrophobic realization settled over her. She was present. She was physical. But she was not fully in control of the body she now wore.
Yurei (telepathically, voice tight with unease): “Naobito… this body doesn’t feel like mine. The muscles are mine, but the framework is yours. There’s no heartbeat. It’s like I’m wearing a perfectly crafted cage.”
Naobito (calm, from deep within the Hollow Shell): “That’s because you are. The structure is stable. Focus on the fight.”
Yurei exhaled sharply through her nose, still unsettled, but the approaching threat forced her attention outward.
Kenshi took one step closer, blinking as he looked her up and down.
Kenshi (lowering his hands for a split second): “Wait… you can just do that now? Since when do you do the whole terrifying monster metamorphosis thing?”
Yurei shot him a sharp, sideways glance. Kenshi held her gaze for half a second, then shrugged and rolled his shoulders. Blue kinetic mana flared around his arms as he pushed his Innate Weave into Over-Rev.
Kenshi: “You know what, never mind. We’ll talk about it later. I’ll take the blind spots.”
Across the pit, Asha Nkosi planted her back foot.
The bedrock beneath her didn’t merely crack — it imploded. A localized vacuum formed for a fraction of a second as the sheer kinetic force of her step crushed the air itself. Green-and-gold Beast Mana erupted from her body and, for a single terrifying moment, coalesced into the massive spectral jaws of an apex predator. The phantom maw stretched wide enough to swallow the entire center of the arena before snapping shut around them.
Asha Nkosi (roaring with pure exhilaration): “COME!”
The Beast Weaver launched herself forward like a living meteor.
Yurei’s eyes flared with dark light. Black bandages and razor-edged wings of Corruption Mana flared outward as she raised both arms to meet the charge. Kenshi vanished in a blur of blue speed, already angling for Asha’s blind side.
The three forces collided in the center of the Crimson Veil Arena.
Beast Mana, Kinetic Over-Rev, and pure Dark Corruption detonated against one another in a blinding explosion of green, gold, blue, and violet light. The shockwave ripped outward, shattering what little remained of the reinforced floor and forcing the crowd to shield their faces.
The real fight had begun.
The collision of Beast Mana, Kinetic Over-Rev, and pure Dark Corruption detonated in the center of the Crimson Veil Arena.
The shockwave ripped outward in a blinding explosion of green, gold, blue, and violet light, turning the air itself into white noise. The reinforced floor shattered further. The crowd screamed as the force of the impact rattled the entire stadium.
For a single suspended moment, the three combatants vanished inside the storm of power.
The Ethereal Sanctum – Somewhere Beyond the Veil
Far above the Crimson Veil Arena, existing in a dimension layered just outside of standard reality, the true Mistress of Threads sat in quiet observation.
Hana Yagami rested on a throne woven from starlight and cosmic silk. Ethereal screens of pure mana floated before her, projecting the blinding explosion currently tearing apart the pit.
A few feet away, Kiritaka Yagami leaned against a pillar of solid light, arms crossed, squinting at the projection.
Kiritaka: “Man, that Asha kid really doesn’t hold back, does she? Nao’s taking a beating.”
He paused, tilting his head as he watched Yurei’s monstrous form clash with the Beast Weaver. He scratched the back of his neck, looking mildly confused.
Kiritaka: “Hey, honey? Remind me again… what exactly does our little guy’s gimmick do? The… Hollow Shell thing? I always get it mixed up with possession.”
Hana didn’t move. Slowly, the cosmic sewing-machine goddess turned her head. Her glowing, ethereal eyes locked onto her husband. The look she gave him was so profoundly, intensely deadpan that it could have leveled a mountain.
Hana Yagami: “He is your son, Kiritaka.”
Kiritaka (holding his hands up defensively): “I know, I know! I just forget the specifics! He says Kōtai, the bandages come out, and then boom, demon girl. Explain it to me like I’m an idiot.”
Hana sighed, a sound that resonated like wind chimes across the galaxy. She turned her attention back to the floating screens.
Hana: “He is not the vessel. He is the cage. Hollow Shell allows him to weave his own mana around a housed entity, completely rebuilding their physical presence from the inside out using his own architecture. He isn’t surrendering control to that Dark Maiden. He swallowed her.”
Kiritaka (nodding slowly): “Right, right. So he’s wearing her.”
Hana: “More than that. While he sits safely in the center of the shell, he is actively absorbing her foundation. He reads the information of her soul, her memories, and her Innate Weave. Eventually, if he masters it, he won’t just read her information…”
Her ethereal eyes narrowed slightly as she watched Yurei parry a devastating blow from Asha.
Hana: “…He will be able to rewrite it.”
Kiritaka blinked, staring at the screen for a long moment before letting out a low whistle.
Kiritaka: “Right. So our kid is a walking reality-editor wearing a demon as a biological mech suit. That’s… terrifying. Good for him.”
Back in the Arena
The explosion of power finally cleared enough for the crowd to see again.
Yurei skidded backward across the shattered floor, dark bandages flaring to absorb the residual force. Kenshi reappeared in a blur of blue light on her left flank, kinetic energy still crackling around his fists. Across from them, Asha Nkosi stood tall, completely unharmed, green-and-gold Beast Mana coiling around her like living predators.
The Beast Weaver grinned wider than ever.
Asha Nkosi: “Good… That’s more like it.”
The real fight had only just begun.
The shockwave from the first collision still rolled across the shattered pit when Yurei moved.
Black Corruption Mana coalesced in her hands, swirling and solidifying into a long, elegant scythe whose blade pulsed with deep violet light. The weapon’s edge hummed with corrosive hunger, the kind that could eat through both flesh and pure mana.
Yurei (grinning sharply, voice carrying across the arena): “Let’s see if the Beast Weaver can handle a real cut.”
She vanished in a blur of dark motion, the scythe trailing a crescent of pure Corruption as she closed the distance on Asha.
